Canonical diagonal liftings I: dormant PGL n \mathrm{PGL}_{n} -opers on algebraic curves
Yasuhiro Wakabayashi

Abstract
. The present paper forms the first part of a series in which we treat some topics on dormant opers and Frobenius-Ehresmann structures from an arithmetic point of view. In the present paper, motivated by the issue of lifting homogeneous linear differential equations in prime characteristic p p , we generalize the moduli theory of dormant PGL n \mathrm{PGL}_{n} -opers on pointed stable curves. One of the main results is the construction of a compactified moduli space classifying dormant PGL n \mathrm{PGL}_{n} -opers of level N > 0 N>0 . We also examine its geometric structure, including a factorization property according to clutching morphisms. Furthermore, the generic étaleness of the moduli space for n = 2 n=2 is proved by obtaining a detailed understanding of the deformation space in terms of cohomology groups. As a consequence, we give a certain unique lifting of any dormant PGL 2 \mathrm{PGL}_{2} -oper of level N N on a general curve to characteristic p N p^{N} ; this lifting is called the canonical diagonal lifting. Canonical diagonal liftings enable us to establish an equivalence between the counting problems of related objects in characteristic p p and p N p^{N} .

Key words: Diagonal lifting, Diagonal reduction, algebraic curve, dormant oper, flat bundle, Frobenius-Ehresmann structure, Moduli space,
p
-curvature.

0.
Introduction

0.1.
Differential equations and opers

Let us consider the homogeneous linear differential equation
Dy=0

on a smooth algebraic curve
X
over
\mathbb{C}
associated to an operator
D

expressed locally as

(1)

\displaystyle D:=\frac{d^{n}}{dx^{n}}+q_{1}\frac{d^{n-1}}{dx^{n-1}}+\cdots+q_{n-1}\frac{d}{dx}+q_{n}

(
n>1
).
Here,
x
denotes a local coordinate in
X
and
q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}
are variable coefficients.
To each such differential equation,
one can associate
a connection on a vector bundle whose matrix representation is locally given by

(2)

\displaystyle\nabla=\frac{d}{dx}-\begin{pmatrix}-q_{1}&-q_{2}&-q_{3}&\cdots&-q_{n-1}&-q_{n}\\
1&0&0&\cdots&0&0\\
0&1&0&\cdots&0&0\\
0&0&1&\cdots&0&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&0&\cdots&1&0\end{pmatrix}.

A vector bundle equipped with such a connection is known as a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper
.
Conversely, any flat bundle (i.e., a vector bundle equipped with a flat connection) defining a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper becomes, after possibly carrying out a suitable gauge transformation, a connection of that form.
See
[
BeDr1
]
or
[
BeDr2
]
for the precise definition of a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper, or more generally, a
G
-oper for an algebraic group
G
of a certain sort.

In the case of
G=\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
,
G
-opers can be obtained from
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-opers via projectivization, and
arise in many areas of mathematics along with their higher-dimensional generalizations (called
Ehresmann structures
, following
[
Gol
]
and
[
CoCo
]
).
In fact, under the name of
projective structures
*
*

*

To be precise, a
projective structure
is defined as an additional structure on a
Riemann surface
consisting of local coordinate charts defining its complex structure such that on any two overlapping patches, the change of coordinates may be described as a Möbius transformation. Projective structures
are in bijection with
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers via the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence and the algebraization of the underlying Riemann surface.
,

\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers
play a major role in
Teichmüller theory and provide algebraic approaches (e.g.,
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
-representations of surface groups) to deal with analytic
uniformization data for Riemann surfaces.

Note that the assignment
y\mapsto{{}^{t}(}\frac{d^{n-1}y}{dx^{n-1}},\cdots,\frac{dy}{dx},y)
gives a bijective correspondence between
the solutions of the equation
Dy=0
with
D
as above
and the horizontal sections of
the associated

\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper.
In particular, such a differential equation has a full set of algebraic solutions (i.e., has
n
linearly independent algebraic solutions)
precisely when
the corresponding
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper
has finite monodromy.
The problem of classifying and counting homogeneous linear differential equations
with a full set of algebraic solutions
has long been one of the fundamental topics in mathematics.
In fact, the study of them
was
tackled and developed from the
19th century onwards
by many mathematicians:
H. A. Schwarz (for the hypergeometric equations), L. I. Fuchs, P. Gordan, and C. F. Klein (for the second order equations), C. Jordan (for the
n
-th order equations) et al.

Regarding the relationship
with the case of characteristic
p
(where
p
is a prime number),
there is a well-known conjecture, i.e., the so-called Grothendieck-Katz
p
-curvature conjecture; it
predicts the existence of
a full set of horizontal sections of a flat bundle in terms of its reductions modulo
p
for the various primes
p
.
See, e.g.,
[
And
]
or
[
Kat2
]
for detailed accounts of this topic.

0.2.
Counting problem of dormant opers

We want to focus on linear differential equations with a full set of solutions, as well as
their corresponding opers, in the case where the underlying curve has prime-power characteristic
p^{N}
(
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
).
Note that one may define the notion of a
G
-oper even in such a situation
because of the algebraic nature of its formulation.
One central theme of our study stated in the most primitive form is
to answer the following natural question:

How many homogeneous linear differential equations in characteristic
p^{N}

(associated to differential operators
D
as in (
1
)) have a full set of solutions?

We can find some previous works dealing with
the related topics for
N=1

(cf.
[
Iha
]
,
[
JoPa
]
,
[
Jos
]
,
[
LaPa
]
,
[
Moc2
]
,
[
Oss1
]
,
[
Wak1
]
,
[
Wak2
]
,
[
Wak3
]
, and
[
Wak6
]
).
One of the common key ingredients in the development of these works is the study of the
p
-curvature
of a connection.
Recall that the
p
-curvature of a connection is an invariant that measures the obstruction to the compatibility of
p
-power structures appearing in certain associated spaces of infinitesimal symmetries.
A
G
-oper (for an arbitrary
G
) is called
dormant
if it has vanishing
p
-curvature.
It follows from a classical result by Cartier (cf.
[
Kat1
, Theorem (5.1)]
) that
a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper in characteristic
p
is dormant if and only if the corresponding equation
Dy=0
has a full set of solutions.
Hence,
given that it is more convenient to deal with
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers rather than with
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-opers for technical reasons,
the above question for
N=1

can be
reduced to asking
the number of
all possible dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers.
(As is well-known,
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers correspond bijectively to
n
-th order linear differential operators with unit principal symbol and vanishing subprincipal symbol.)

In what follows, we briefly review
previous results
concerning the counting problem of dormant opers.
Let
X
be a connected proper smooth curve of genus
g>1
over an algebraically closed field of characteristic
p>2
.
In the case of
g=2
,
S. Mochizuki (cf.
[
Moc2
, Chap. V, Corollary 3.7]
), H. Lange-C. Pauly (cf.
[
LaPa
, Theorem 2]
), and B. Osserman (cf.
[
Oss1
, Theorem 1.2]
)
computed, by applying different methods,
the number of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers on a general
X
.
For example, this computation was made by using a correspondence with the base locus of the Verschiebung rational map on the moduli space of rank
2
semistable bundles.

Moreover, by extending the relevant formulations to the case where
X
admits marked points or nodal singularities,
S. Mochizuki also gave
an explicit description of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers on each totally degenerate curve
in terms of
radius
(cf.
[
Moc2
, Introduction, Theorem 1.3]
).
The notion of radius is an invariant determining a sort of boundary condition at a marked point to glue together dormant opers in accordance with the attachment of underlying curves at this point.
Note that Mochizuki’s description is essentially equivalent to a previous result obtained by Y. Ihara (cf.
[
Iha
, § 1.6]
), who investigated the situation when a given hypergeometric differential equation in characteristic
p
had a full set of solutions.
As a result of this explicit description, we obtained
a combinatorial procedure for explicitly computing the number of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers
in accordance with a sort of fusion rule.

The explicit description also leads to a work by
F. Liu and B. Osserman (cf.
[
LiOs
, Theorem 2.1]
), who have shown that
the value in question
may be expressed as a degree
3g-3
polynomial with respect to “
p
”.
They did so by applying
Ehrhart’s theory, which concerns computing the cardinality of the set of lattice points inside a polytope.

For general rank cases,
K. Joshi and C. Pauly showed that there are only finitely many dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers on a fixed curve (cf.
[
JoPa
, Corollary 6.1.6]
).
Also, Joshi conjectured, with his amazing insight, an explicit description
of their total number (cf.
[
Jos
, Conjecture 8.1]
).
As a consequence of developing the moduli theory of dormant
G
-opers on pointed stable curves,
this conjecture was affirmatively solved for a general curve (cf.
[
Wak6
, Theorem H]
).
This was done
by establishing, on the basis of the idea of Joshi
et al. (cf.
[
JoPa
]
,
[
Jos
]
),
a relationship with a special case of the Vafa-Intriligator formula that computes the degree of a certain type of Quot scheme.
Moreover, when
n=2
, we can extend this result to
pointed stable
curves
by comparing with the fusion ring associated to the
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{l}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
-WZW (= Wess-Zumino-Witten) conformal field theory (cf.
[
Wak6
, Theorem 7.41]
).

0.3.
Generalization to characteristic
p^{N}

The purpose of the present paper is
to generalize the enumerative geometry of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers to prime-power characteristic.
Suppose that
X
is a geometrically connected, proper, and smooth curve in characteristic
p^{N}
.
Just as in the case of characteristic
p
,
an oper, or more generally a flat bundle, on
X

will be called
dormant
if it is spanned by its horizontal sections, or equivalently, isomorphic locally to the trivial flat bundle (cf. Definition
4.2.2
).
This means that a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper (or a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper) is dormant if and only if the corresponding differential equation
Dy=0
has a full set of solutions.
In particular,

Our central theme posed earlier can be rephrased essentially as the issue of counting dormant opers on a (general) curve in characteristic
p^{N}
.

(Note that we also discuss, at the same time, the case where the underlying curve has nodal singularities or marked points; in such a generalized situation, the dormancy condition has to be described in a different and more complicated manner.)

Unfortunately, the lack of a reasonable invariant
exactly like the
p
-curvature for
N=1

makes it difficult to handle with dormant opers in characteristic
p^{N}
, as well as
linear differential equations.
To overcome this difficulty, we relate, partly on the basis of the argument of
[
Moc2
, § 2.1, Chap. II]
, dormant opers on
X
to certain objects defined on the reduction
X_{0}
of
X
modulo
p
;
we shall refer to the operation resulting from this argument as
diagonal reduction/lifting
.
One remarkable observation is that each dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
X
induces,
via diagonal reduction, a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
X_{0}
equipped with a structure of
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module extending its flat structure, i.e., a structure of
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}
-module.
Here,
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
denotes the ring of differential operators of level
N-1
introduced by P. Berthelot in
[
PBer1
]
and
[
PBer2
]
.
A dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper
on
X_{0}
equipped with such an additional structure is called a
dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper of level
N
,
or a
dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
,
for short.
Similarly, we obtain the notion of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper.
In particular,
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(1)}
-opers are nothing but
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers in the classical sense.
See Definitions
4.2.1
,
4.2.2
,
4.3.1
, and
4.3.2
for their precise definitions.

We expect that, for a general curve
X
, the assignment
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
given by taking the diagonal reductions is
invertible
, i.e.,
each dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit}
on
X_{0}
may be lifted uniquely to a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Rightarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit}
on
X
.
The goal of the present paper is to show that it is in fact true for
n=2
.
The results of the lifting
\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Rightarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit}
will be called the
canonical diagonal liftings
.
The bijective correspondence given by
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
and
\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Rightarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit}
enables us to obtain a detailed understanding
of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers in characteristic
p^{N}

by applying, via diagonal reduction, various methods and perspectives inherent in characteristic-
p
-geometry established in
[
Wak6
]
.
In particular, because of a factorization property on the moduli space classifying dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers in accordance with clutching morphisms,
we
reduce the counting problem under consideration to the cases of small
g
and
r
.
Some explicit computations based on this argument will be made in subsequent papers of this series (cf.
[
Wak9
]
).

0.4.
First part of the paper:
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules and dormant flat bundles

In the rest of this Introduction, we shall describe the contents and some (relatively important) results of the present paper.
Note that we substantially apply various results and discussions of
[
Wak6
]
, in which the author developed the theory of (dormant) opers on pointed stable curves from the viewpoint of logarithmic geometry.
With that in mind, our study could be placed in a higher-level generalization of (a part of) that theory.
Our discussions may be divided into three parts in accordance with topics.

The first part of the present paper is devoted to a general study of flat bundles formulated in terms of logarithmic geometry.
In §
1
,
we discuss flat bundles on a log curve.
To do this, we use the logarithmic generalization of the sheaf “
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
” (
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) introduced by C. Montagnon (cf.
[
Mon
]
).
This formulation is essential in investigating how the related moduli spaces behave when
the underlying curve degenerates.
Also, the Cartier operator associated to a
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module (i.e., a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module with vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature) is defined by composing the usual Cartier operators of the flat bundles constituting that
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module at the respective levels (cf. Definition
1.6.5
).

In §
2
,
we generalize the classical notion of a dormant flat bundle (i.e., a flat bundle with vanishing
p
-curvature)
to characteristic
p^{m+1}
(cf. Definition
2.1.4
).
At the same time, we define the diagonal reduction of a dormant flat bundle, as well as a diagonal lifting of a
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module.
Roughly speaking, the diagonal reduction
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}
of a given dormant flat bundle
\mathscr{F}
is obtained in
such a way that, for each
l\leq m
, the reduction of
\mathscr{F}
modulo
p^{l+1}
corresponds, via the Cartier operator, to the reduction of the level of
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}
to
l
.
If the log structure of the underlying curve is trivial, then the dormancy condition can be characterized by the vanishing of the
p
-curvature of the flat bundles appearing in the inductive procedure for constructing the diagonal reduction (cf. Proposition
2.2.4
, (i)).
In this sense, a dormant flat bundle in characteristic
p^{m+1}
may be seen as something like a successive
p
-flat
deformation of a flat bundle in characteristic
p
.

In §
3
,
we discuss the local description of a dormant flat bundle around a marked/nodal point of the underlying pointed stable curve.
For each scheme
S
flat over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
,
we shall set

(3)

\displaystyle U_{\oslash}:=\mathcal{S}pec(\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!])

(cf. (
181
)), where
t
denotes a formal parameter.
By equipping
U_{\oslash}
with the log structure determined by the divisor
t=0
,
we obtain a log scheme
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}
and hence obtain the notion of a dormant flat bundle on
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}
.
The log scheme
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}
may be regarded as a formal neighborhood of each marked point in a pointed curve.
One of the results in §
3
generalizes
[
Kin
, Proposition 1.1.12]
,
[
Oss2
, Corollary 2.10]
and tells us by what information a dormant flat bundle can be exactly characterized.
The statement is described as follows:

Theorem A

(cf. Proposition-Definition
3.5.4
)
.

Suppose that the reduction
S_{0}
of
S
modulo
p
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Spec}(R)
for a local ring
(R,\mathfrak{m})
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
whose residue field
R/\mathfrak{m}
is algebraically closed.
Let
\mathscr{F}=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a dormant flat bundle on
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S
of rank
n>0
.
Then, there exists
an isomorphism of flat bundles

(4)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d_{i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{F}

(cf. (
239
) for the definition of
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,(-)}
)
for some
d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
.
In particular, the monodromy operator
\mu(\nabla)
of
\nabla
in the sense of Definition
3.2.1
can be transformed into the diagonal matrix with diagonal entries
-d_{1},\cdots,-d_{n}
.
Moreover, the resulting multiset
[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]
depends only on the isomorphism class of
\mathscr{F}
.

We remark that another main result in that section gives an explicit description of a
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module on a nodal/marked point of a pointed stable curve in characteristic
p
(cf. Proposition-Definition
3.4.1
); this result commutes, in a certain sense, with Theorem
A
via diagonal reduction.

0.5.
Second part of the paper: Moduli theory of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers

In §§
4
-
5
, we discuss
dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on pointed curves in characteristic
p
and their moduli space.
An important achievement of our discussion is to construct a smooth compactification of the moduli space by allowing nodal singularities on the underlying curves.
For a pair of nonnegative integers
(g,r)
with
2g-2+r>0
,
we denote by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
the moduli stack classifying
r
-pointed stable curves of genus
g
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Also, let us fix integers
n
,
N
with
1<n<p
and
N>0
.
The central character of the present paper is the category

(5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

(cf. (
290
))
consisting of pairs
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
, where
\mathscr{X}
denotes an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
in characteristic
p
(i.e., an object classified by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
) and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
denotes a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
By forgetting the data of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers, we obtain
the projection

(6)

\displaystyle\Pi_{g,r}:\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}

(cf. (
291
)).
Such a relative formulation enables us to investigate how the moduli space deforms when the underlying curve degenerates.
Additionally, when it has actually occurred,
elements of a certain set

(7)

\displaystyle\Xi_{n,N}\left(\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta\right)

(cf. (
348
)) provide
a boundary condition for gluing dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers in accordance with an attachment of two curves along respective marked points; this boundary condition generalizes the classical notion of radius (cf. Definition
5.3.2
).

Our results concerning the geometric structures of

\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
and
\Pi_{g,r}
are summarized as follows.

Theorem B

(cf. Theorems
4.5.5
,
5.4.7
,
and Corollaries
4.6.2
,
5.5.4
)
.

(i)

The category
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
may be represented by a nonempty proper Deligne-Mumford stack over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and the projection
\Pi_{g,r}

is finite.
Moreover, the stack
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
decomposes into the disjoint union

(8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}=\coprod_{\rho\in\Xi_{n,N}}\mathcal{O}p_{\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},

where, for each
\rho\in\Xi_{n,N}
,
\mathcal{O}p_{\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
(cf. (
349
)) denotes the open and closed substack of
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
classifying dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers of radii
\rho
.

(ii)

Let
\mathbb{G}:=({{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG,\{(g_{j},r_{j})\}_{j=1}^{J},\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})
be clutching data of type
(g,r)
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Definition 7.4]
)
and
\rho
an element of
\Xi_{n,N}
.
Denote by

\Pi_{\rho,g,r}
the natural projection
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
, i.e., the restriction of
\Pi_{g,r}
to
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,g,r}
.
Then, for
each set of
\mathbb{G}
-
\Xi_{n,N}
-radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
with
\rho_{\mathbb{G}\Rightarrow\emptyset}=\rho
(cf. Definition
5.4.1
and (
364
)),
there exists a canonical clutching morphism

(9)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Clut}_{\mathbb{G},\rho_{\mathbb{G}}}:\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{O}p_{\rho^{j},g_{j},r_{j}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}.

Moreover,
the square diagram

\textstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{\mathbb{G}}:=\{\rho^{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho^{j},g_{j},r_{j}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{\mathbb{G}}}\mathrm{Clut}_{\mathbb{G},\rho_{\mathbb{G}}}}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{\mathbb{G}}}\prod_{j}\Pi_{\rho^{j},g_{j},r_{j}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,g,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{\rho,g,r}}
\textstyle{\prod_{j=1}^{J}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{j},r_{j}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Clut}_{\mathbb{G}}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}}

is commutative and Cartesian, where

–

the products “
\prod
” are all taken over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
the disjoint union on the upper-left corner runs over the sets of
\mathbb{G}
-
\Xi_{n,N}
-radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
with
\rho_{\mathbb{G}\Rightarrow\emptyset}=\rho
;

–

\mathrm{Clut}_{\mathbb{G}}
denotes the usual clutching morphism associated to
\mathbb{G}
(cf. (
363
)).

In §
6
, we focus on the case of
n=2
and develop the deformation theory of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers.
The deformation space of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper is described by using the hypercohomology group of a certain complex associated to that oper (cf. Theorem
6.3.5
).
By this description together with the local study of finite-level
\mathcal{D}
-modules discussed in the first part,
we can show more than the facts stated in Theorem
B
.
In particular, the following assertion, which is a higher-level generalization of (a part of)
[
Moc2
, Chap. II, Theorem 2.8]
, holds.

Theorem C

(cf. Corollaries
6.3.6
,
6.4.4
, and Theorem
6.7.1
)
.

Suppose that
n=2
(and
p>2
).
Then, the Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

is smooth over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and equidimensional of dimension
3g-3+r
.
Moreover, the projection
\Pi_{g,r}
is faithfully flat and étale over the points of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
classifying totally degenerate curves.
In particular,
for each element

\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}\in((\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\})^{\times r}\left(=\Xi_{2,N}^{\times r}\right)
,
the projection
\Pi_{\rho,g,r}
is finite, faithfully flat, generically étale,
and its degree satisfies the equality

(14)

\displaystyle\mathrm{deg}(\Pi_{\rho,r,g})=\sum_{\rho_{\mathbb{G}}:=\{\rho^{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathrm{deg}(\Pi_{\rho^{j},g_{j},r_{j}})

for each clutching data
\mathbb{G}
as in Theorem
B
, (ii), where the sum in the right-hand side runs over the sets of
\mathbb{G}
-
\Xi_{2,N}
-radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
with
\rho_{\mathbb{G}\Rightarrow\emptyset}=\rho
.

0.6.
Third part of the paper: Canonical diagonal liftings of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers

In §
7
,
the canonical diagonal lifting of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
is constructed by using the various results obtained up to that point.
As discussed in Proposition
2.4.4
,
there exists a direct linkage between raising the level of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper in characteristic
p
to
N
and lifting that oper to characteristic
p^{N}
.
In particular,
when a given smooth curve is
N
-ordinary in the sense of Definition
6.7.4
,
any dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on it admits a canonical lifting.
This procedure for constructing these canonical liftings reverses the operation of taking the diagonal reductions, and it can be applied to general curves because the
N
-ordinary locus in the moduli space of curves is dense.

Now, let
S
be a flat scheme over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
and
X
a geometrically connected, proper, and smooth curve over
S
of genus
g>1
.
Denote by
S_{0}
and
X_{0}
the reductions modulo
p
of
S
and
X
, respectively.
Also, denote by

(15)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{1,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Op}_{N,X_{0}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\right)

(cf. (
573
)) the set of isomorphism classes of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers on
X/S
(resp., dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers on
X_{0}/S_{0}
).
Taking the diagonal reductions induces a map of sets

(16)

\displaystyle\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\spadesuit}:\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,X}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,X_{0}}.

(cf. (
575
)). Then, we obtain the following assertion.

Theorem D

(cf. Theorem-Definition
7.2.1
, Remark
7.2.2
, and Corollary
7.2.5
)
.

Suppose that
X_{0}/S_{0}
is general in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0}
.
Then, the map
\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\spadesuit}
has an inverse map

(17)

\displaystyle\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Rightarrow$}_{\!\!\spadesuit}:\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,X_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,X}.

That is to say, for each dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0}
on
X_{0}/S_{0}
,
there exists a unique (up to isomorphism) dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Rightarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0}
on
X/S
whose diagonal reduction is isomorphic to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0}
.
In particular, if
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
for an algebraically closed field
k
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, then the set
\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,X}
is finite and its cardinality

\sharp(\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,X})
satisfies

(18)

\displaystyle\sharp(\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,X})=\left(\sharp(\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,X_{0}})=\right)\mathrm{deg}(\Pi_{g,0}),

where the right-hand side denotes the degree of
\Pi_{g,0}
for
n=2
.

For each
\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit}
as in the above theorem,
we shall refer to
the resulting dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Rightarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0}

as the
canonical diagonal lifting
of
\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit}
.

The above theorem is expected to hold for a general (sufficiently small) rank
n
.
To prove it in accordance with the arguments in the present paper, we will have to prove the generic étaleness of the moduli space
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,0}
for that case.
Also, we remark that other lifting constructions of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers can be found in

[
LSYZ
]
,
[
Moc1
]
, and
[
Moc2
]
, but differ from ours (cf. Remark
7.2.4
).

At the end of the present paper, we reformulate the above theorem as
an assertion for

Frobenius-projective structures
.
A Frobenius-projective structure was originally introduced by Y. Hoshi as a characteristic-
p
analogue of a projective structure (cf.
[
Hos
, Definition 2.1]
).
Roughly speaking, a Frobenius-projective structure of level
N
(or simply, an
F^{N}
-projective structure) is a maximal collection of étale coordinate charts on
a curve
valued in the projective line whose transition functions descend to its
N
-th Frobenius twist.
The statement of
[
Hos
, Theorem A]
may be interpreted as the existence of a natural bijection between dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers and
F^{N}
-projective structures.
(In
[
Wak5
, Definition 2.2.2]
, the author introduced the notion of a Frobenius-Ehresmann structure, which is a multi-dimensional generalization of a Frobenius-projective structure.
The same kind of bijection as mentioned above holds for those structures, see

[
Wak4
, Theorem A]
and
[
Wak5
, Theorem A]
.)

In Definition
7.3.2
, the definition of an
F^{N}
-projective structure is extended to characteristic
p^{N}
.
For
X/S
as before, denote by

(19)

\displaystyle F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X_{0}}\right)

(cf. (
585
))
the set of
F^{1}
-projective structures on
X/S
(resp., the set of
F^{N}
-projective structures on
X_{0}/S_{0}
).
Then, there exist canonical bijections

(20)

\displaystyle F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,X}\ \ \text{and}\ \ F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,X_{0}},

generalizing Hoshi’s correspondence (cf. Theorem
7.3.4
).
These bijections and Theorem
D
together imply
that
the map of sets

(21)

\displaystyle\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\blacklozenge}:F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X}\rightarrow F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X_{0}}

(cf. (
590
))
induced by taking the diagonal reductions is bijective if
X_{0}/S_{0}
is general in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0}
.
For each
F^{N}
-projective structure
\mathcal{S}^{\blacklozenge}_{0}
on
X_{0}
,
its image via the inverse map
of
\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\blacklozenge}
specifies an
F^{1}
-projective structure on
X
; we shall refer to it as
the
canonical diagonal lifting
of

\mathcal{S}^{\blacklozenge}_{0}
(cf. Theorem-Definition
7.3.5
).

1.
\mathcal{D}
-modules of finite level

In this section, we recall some basics related to the ring of logarithmic differential operators of finite level and higher-level flat bundles on a log curve.
Throughout the present paper, we suppose that
all schemes are locally noetherian.
Also, let us fix a prime
p
.

1.1.
Logarithmic differential operators of finite level

First, we briefly recall the ring of logarithmic differential operators of finite level discussed in
[
PBer1
]
,
[
PBer2
]
, and
[
Mon
]
.

Let
m
be a nonnegative integer.
Also, let
S^{\mathrm{log}}
be an fs log scheme over

\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}

and
X^{\mathrm{log}}
an fs log scheme equipped with a log smooth morphism
f^{\mathrm{log}}:X^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Denote by
\Omega_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}

the sheaf of logarithmic
1
-forms on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}

and by

\mathcal{T}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}

the sheaf of logarithmic vector fields on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
, i.e., the dual of
\Omega_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
For simplicity, we occasionally write
\Omega
and
\mathcal{T}
instead of
\Omega_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
, respectively.

Suppose that
p
is locally nilpotent on
S
and that

S
is equipped with an
m
-PD structure
that extends to

X
via
f
.
Denote by
P_{(m)}^{\mathrm{log}}

the log
m
-PD envelope
of the diagonal embedding
X^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{log}}\times_{S^{\mathrm{log}}}X^{\mathrm{log}}
(cf.
[
Mon
, Proposition 2.1.1]
) and
by
\mathcal{P}_{(m)}
the structure sheaf of
P_{(m)}^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Recall that
the defining ideal
\overline{\mathcal{I}}
of the strict closed immersion
X^{\mathrm{log}}\hookrightarrow P_{(m)}^{\mathrm{log}}

admits the
m
-PD-adic filtration
\{\overline{\mathcal{I}}^{\{l\}}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
constructed in the manner of
[
PBer2
, Definition A. 3]
.
For each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, the quotient sheaf
\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}
of
\mathcal{P}_{(m)}
by
\overline{\mathcal{I}}^{\{l+1\}}

determines a strict closed subscheme

P^{l,\mathrm{log}}_{(m)}
of
P_{(m)}^{\mathrm{log}}
.
We shall write

\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{l}
and
\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{l}
for the morphisms
P_{(m)}^{l,\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{log}}
induced by
the first and second projections
X^{\mathrm{log}}\times_{S^{\mathrm{log}}}X^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{log}}
,
respectively.
Note that
\mathcal{P}^{l}_{(m)}
may be regarded as a sheaf on
X
.
Moreover, it has an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure via
\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{l}
(resp.,
\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{l}
); it will be applied whenever we are considering an action of
\mathcal{O}_{X}

by left (resp., right) multiplication.
When there is a fear of confusion, we use the notation
\mathrm{pr}_{1*}^{l}(\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l})
(resp.,
\mathrm{pr}_{2*}^{l}(\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l})
) for writing the sheaf
\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}
equipped with this
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.

Given an integer
m^{\prime}\geq m
, we obtain a natural morphism

(22)

\displaystyle\varsigma^{l}_{m^{\prime},m}:\mathcal{P}_{(m^{\prime})}^{l}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}

preserving both the left and right
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structures.
As
mentioned in
[
Mon
, § 2.3]
,
there exists a canonical
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra morphism

(23)

\displaystyle\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}}:\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{l}_{(m)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}^{l^{\prime}}_{(m)}

for each pair of nonnegative integers
(l,l^{\prime})
such that if
m^{\prime}
is an
integer

\geq m
, then
the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{(m^{\prime})}^{l+l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{m^{\prime}}^{l,l^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\varsigma^{l+l^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime},m}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{(m^{\prime})}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m^{\prime})}^{l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varsigma^{l}_{m^{\prime},m}\otimes\varsigma^{l^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime},m}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}.}

The morphism
\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}}
determines a morphism of log schemes

(28)

\displaystyle\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}\sharp}:P_{(m)}^{l,\mathrm{log}}\times_{X^{\mathrm{log}}}P_{(m)}^{l^{\prime},\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow P_{(m)}^{l+l^{\prime},\mathrm{log}}.

over
X^{\mathrm{log}}
.

For each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
we shall set

(29)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\leq l}^{(m)}:=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathrm{pr}_{1*}^{l}(\mathcal{P}^{l}_{(m)}),\mathcal{O}_{X})

(cf.
[
Mon
, Definition 2.3.1]
).
In particular, we have natural identifications
\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\leq 0}^{(m)}=\mathcal{O}_{X}
,
\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\leq l}^{(m)}/\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\leq l-1}^{(m)}=\mathcal{T}^{\otimes l}
(
l\geq 1
).
The
sheaf of logarithmic differential operators of level
m
is defined by

(30)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)}:=\bigcup_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\leq l}^{(m)}.

For simplicity, we occasionally write
\mathcal{D}_{\leq l}^{(m)}
and
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}

instead of
\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\leq l}^{(m)}
and
\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S~\mathrm{log}}^{(m)}
, respectively.
The morphisms
\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}}
(for
l,l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) determine a structure of (possibly noncommutative)
f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-algebra

\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
on
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
.
The collection of morphisms
\varsigma_{m,m^{\prime}}^{l}
(for
l,m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
m^{\prime}\leq m
) induces an inductive system of sheaves

\{\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
.
We shall write
{{}^{L}\mathcal{D}}^{(m)}
(resp.,
{{}^{R}\mathcal{D}}^{(m)}
) for the sheaf
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
endowed with a structure of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module arising from left (resp., right) multiplication by sections of
\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}^{(m)}\left(=\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)
.

Note that
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}
is nothing but the sheaf of noncommutative rings “
\mathscr{D}_{\hslash,Y^{\mathrm{log}}/T^{\mathrm{log}}}^{<\infty}
” defined in
[
Wak6
, Eq. (463)]
, where the triple
(\hslash,T^{\mathrm{log}},Y^{\mathrm{log}})
is taken to be
(1,S^{\mathrm{log}},X^{\mathrm{log}})
.
By taking this into account, we suppose that
the sheaf
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}
(as well as the sheaves
\mathcal{D}_{\leq l}^{(0)}
’s for
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) is defined in the sense of
loc. cit.

whenever we deal with
it
without choosing a (
0
-)PD structure on
S
.
On the other hand,
S
is always assumed to be equipped the trivial
m
-PD structure when it is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.

1.2.
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules on log curves

To simplify the discussion,
we restrict ourselves to the case where

X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is a log curve in the sense of
[
Wak6
, Definition 1.40]
.

Given a log scheme
Y^{\mathrm{log}}
, we shall write
\alpha_{Y^{\mathrm{log}}}:\mathcal{M}_{Y^{\mathrm{log}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}
for the morphism of sheaves of monoids defining the log structure of
Y^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Recall from
[
Mon
, § 2.2.2]
that
there exists a short exact sequence

(31)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\varDelta^{-1}(1+\overline{\mathcal{I}})\xrightarrow{\lambda}\varDelta^{-1}(\mathcal{M}_{P_{(m)}^{\mathrm{log}}})\xrightarrow{\varDelta^{*}}\mathcal{M}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}}\longrightarrow 0,

where

\varDelta
denotes the natural morphism
X^{\mathrm{log}}\hookrightarrow P_{(m)}^{\mathrm{log}}
and

\lambda
is the morphism obtained by restricting
\varDelta^{-1}(\alpha^{-1}_{P_{(m)}^{\mathrm{log}}}):\varDelta^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{P_{(m)}})\hookrightarrow\varDelta^{-1}(\mathcal{M}^{\times}_{P^{\mathrm{log}}_{(m)}})
.
For any local section
a\in\mathcal{M}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}}
,

\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{*}(a)
and
\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}(a)
have the same image in
\mathcal{M}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
From the above exact sequence, there exists a unique section
\mu_{(m)}(a)\in\varDelta^{-1}(1+\overline{\mathcal{I}})
with
\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}(a)=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(a)\cdot\lambda(\mu_{(m)}(a))
.
Thus, the assignment
a\mapsto\mu_{(m)}(a)
defines a well-defined morphism
\mu_{(m)}:\mathcal{M}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}}\rightarrow\varDelta^{-1}(1+\overline{\mathcal{I}})
.

Now, let us take a (locally defined) logarithmic coordinate
x\in\mathcal{M}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\mathrm{gr}}
, i.e.,
the section
d\mathrm{log}(x)
forms a local generator of
\Omega
.
We shall set
\eta:=\mu_{(m)}(x)-1
.
For each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
the collection
\{\eta^{\{j\}}\,|\,j\leq l\}
(cf.
[
Mon
, § 1.2.3]
for the definition of
(-)^{\{j\}}
) forms a local basis of
\mathcal{P}^{l}_{(m)}
.
Hence, by setting
\partial^{\langle j\rangle}
as the dual of
\eta^{\{j\}}
,
we obtain the local basis
\{\partial^{\langle j\rangle}\}_{j\leq l}
of
\mathcal{D}_{\leq l}^{(m)}
.
For any nonnegative integers
j^{\prime}
and
j^{\prime\prime}
,
the following equality holds:

(32)

\displaystyle\partial^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}\cdot\partial^{\langle j^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=\sum_{j=\mathrm{max}\{j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}\}}^{j^{\prime}+j^{\prime\prime}}\frac{j!}{(j^{\prime}+j^{\prime\prime}-j)!\cdot(j-j^{\prime})!\cdot(j-j^{\prime\prime})!}\cdot\frac{q_{j^{\prime}}!\cdot q_{j^{\prime\prime}}!}{q_{j}!}\cdot\partial^{\langle j\rangle}

(cf.
[
Mon
, Lemme 2.3.4]
),
where,
for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, let
(q_{j},r_{j})
be the pair of nonnegative integers uniquely determined by the condition that
j=p^{m}\cdot q_{j}+r_{j}
and
0\leq r_{j}<p^{m}
.
In particular, we have
\partial^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}\cdot\partial^{\langle j^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=\partial^{\langle j^{\prime\prime}\rangle}\cdot\partial^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}
.

The following assertion will be applied in the subsequent discussion (cf. Lemma
3.1.1
).

Lemma 1.2.1
.

Let us keep the above notation.
Moreover, suppose that
S
is a scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Then,
for each nonnegative integer
l\leq m
,
the following equality holds:

(33)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{p^{l}-1}(-1)^{j}\cdot\partial^{\langle j\rangle}=\prod_{s=0}^{l-1}(1-(\partial^{\langle p^{s}\rangle})^{p-1}),

where we set
\prod_{s=0}^{-1}(-):=1
.

Proof.

Let
a
,
b
, and
d
be nonnegative integers with

1\leq a\leq p-1
and
p^{d}(p-1+pb)<p^{m}
(which implies
q_{j}=0
for any
j\leq p^{d}(a-1)+p^{d+1}b
).
Since
S
is a scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
we have

(34)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76219pt\partial^{\langle p^{d}(a-1)+p^{d+1}b\rangle}\cdot\partial^{\langle p^{d}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=p^{d}(a-1)+p^{d+1}b}^{p^{d}a+p^{d+1}b}\frac{j!}{(p^{d}a+p^{d+1}b-j)!\cdot(j-p^{d}(a-1)-p^{d+1}b)!\cdot(j-p^{d})!}\cdot\partial^{\langle j\rangle}

\displaystyle\,=(a-1)\cdot\partial^{\langle p^{d}(a-1)+p^{d+1}b\rangle}+a\cdot\partial^{\langle p^{d}a+p^{d+1}b\rangle},

where the first equality follows from (
32
).
By using this, we have

(35)

\displaystyle\partial^{\langle p^{d}a+p^{d+1}b\rangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a}\cdot\left(\partial^{\langle p^{d}\rangle}-(a-1)\right)\cdot\partial^{\langle p^{d}(a-1)+p^{d+1}b\rangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a}\cdot\left(\partial^{\langle p^{d}\rangle}-(a-1)\right)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{a-1}\cdot\left(\partial^{\langle p^{d}\rangle}-(a-2)\right)\cdot\partial^{\langle p^{d}(a-2)+p^{d+1}b\rangle}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \vdots

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a!}\cdot\partial^{\langle p^{d+1}b\rangle}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{a}(\partial^{\langle p^{d}\rangle}-(a-j)).

Here, note that the equality

(36)

\displaystyle 1-x^{p-1}=\sum_{a=0}^{p-1}\frac{(-1)^{a}}{a!}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{a}(x-(a-j))

holds in
\mathbb{F}_{p}[x]
.
Indeed, if
f(x)
denotes the right-hand side of (
36
), then, for each
n=1,\cdots,p-1
, we have

(37)

\displaystyle f(n)=\sum_{a=0}^{n}\frac{(-1)^{a}}{a!}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{a}(x-(a-j))\Bigl|_{x=n}=\sum_{a=0}^{n}(-1)^{a}\cdot\binom{n}{a}=(1-1)^{n}=0.

Moreover, since the equality
f(0)=1
is immediately verified, we obtain (
36
), as desired.
The equalities (
35
) and (
36
) together imply

(38)

\displaystyle\sum_{a=0}^{p-1}(-1)^{p^{d}a+p^{d+1}b}\cdot\partial^{\langle p^{d}a+p^{d+1}b\rangle}

\displaystyle=\partial^{\langle p^{d+1}b\rangle}\cdot\left(\sum_{a=0}^{p-1}\frac{(-1)^{p^{d}a+p^{d+1}b}}{a!}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{a}(\partial^{\langle p^{d}\rangle}-(a-j))\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p^{d+1}b}\cdot\partial^{\langle p^{d+1}b\rangle}\cdot\left(\sum_{a=0}^{p-1}\frac{(-1)^{a}}{a!}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{a}(\partial^{\langle p^{d}\rangle}-(a-j))\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p^{d+1}b}\cdot\partial^{\langle p^{d+1}b\rangle}\cdot(1-(\partial^{\langle p^{d}\rangle})^{p-1}).

By applying (
38
) for various
b
’s and
d
’s, we obtain the following sequence of equalities:

(39)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{p^{l}-1}(-1)^{j}\cdot\partial^{\langle j\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b=0}^{p^{l-1}-1}\sum_{a=0}^{p-1}(-1)^{a+pb}\cdot\partial^{\langle a+pb\rangle}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{dGG1})}}{{=}}(1-(\partial^{\langle 1\rangle})^{p-1})\cdot\sum_{b=0}^{p^{l-1}-1}(-1)^{pb}\cdot\partial^{\langle pb\rangle}

\displaystyle=(1-(\partial^{\langle 1\rangle})^{p-1})\cdot\sum_{b=0}^{p^{l-2}-1}\sum_{a=0}^{p-1}(-1)^{pa+p^{2}b}\cdot\partial^{\langle pa+p^{2}b\rangle}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{dGG1})}}{{=}}(1-(\partial^{\langle 1\rangle})^{p-1})(1-(\partial^{\langle p\rangle})^{p-1})\cdot\sum_{b=0}^{p^{l-2}-1}(-1)^{p^{2}b}\cdot\partial^{\langle p^{2}b\rangle}

\displaystyle\ \,\vdots

\displaystyle=\prod_{s=0}^{l-1}(1-(\partial^{\langle p^{s}\rangle})^{p-1}).

This completes the proof of this assertion.
∎

By a
(left)
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module
, we shall mean a pair
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
consisting of an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{F}

and an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism of
f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-algebras
\nabla:{{}^{L}\mathcal{D}}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})\left(:=\mathcal{E}nd_{f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}(\mathcal{F})\right)
.
We refer to
\nabla
as a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
on
\mathcal{F}
.
When we want to clarify the level “
m
”,
we write
\nabla^{(m)}
instead of
\nabla
.
Also, an
invertible
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module
is a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{L},\nabla)
such that
\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle.
Given a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
,
we shall write

(40)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)

for the subsheaf of
\mathcal{F}
on which
\mathcal{D}_{+}^{(m)}
acts as zero via
\nabla
, where
\mathcal{D}_{+}^{(m)}
denotes the kernel of the canonical projection
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
.

Under the bijective correspondence mentioned in
[
Wak6
, Eq. (468)]
,
we will not distinguish between a
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}
-module structure on an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{F}
and an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F}
on
\mathcal{F}
in the sense of
[
Wak6
, Definition 4.1]
.
(Since we have assumed that
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is a log curve, any
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection is automatically flat.)
In particular, if
\nabla
is an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on
\mathcal{F}
,
then we have
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)=\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F})
.
By a
flat module
(resp., a
flat bundle
) on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
,
we mean
an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module (resp., a vector bundle on
X
) together with an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on it.
(Note that flat modules/bundles can be discussed without a PD structure on
S
.)

Let
(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ})
and
(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet})
be
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules.
Then, a
morphism
from
(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ})
to
(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet})
is defined as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{F}_{\circ}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}
compatible with the respective
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structures
\nabla_{\circ}
,
\nabla_{\bullet}
in a natural sense.

There exists the trivial

\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure

(41)

\displaystyle\nabla_{X,\mathrm{triv}}^{(m)}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{X})

on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
determined uniquely by the condition that
if we write
t:=\alpha_{X^{\mathrm{log}}}(x)
, where
x
denotes a logarithmic coordinate as introduced at the beginning of this subsection, then

(42)

\displaystyle\nabla_{X,\mathrm{triv}}^{(m)}(\partial^{\langle j\rangle})(t^{n})=q_{j}!\cdot\binom{n}{j}\cdot t^{n}

for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
(cf.
[
Mon
, Lemme 2.3.3]
).
Thus, we obtain the trivial (invertible)
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module

(\mathcal{O}_{X},\nabla_{X,\mathrm{triv}}^{(m)})
.

Let

m^{\prime}
be an integer
\geq m
.
To each
\mathcal{D}^{(m^{\prime})}
-module

(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m^{\prime})})
,
we can associate a
\mathcal{D}^{(m^{\prime})}
-module structure

(43)

\displaystyle\nabla^{(m^{\prime})\Rightarrow(m)}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})

on
\mathcal{F}
defined as the composite of
\nabla^{(m^{\prime})}
and the natural morphism
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\rightarrow{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m^{\prime})}
.
In particular, we obtain
a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module

(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m^{\prime})\Rightarrow(m)})
.

1.3.
m
-PD stratifications on
G
-bundles

Let us fix a smooth affine algebraic group
G
over
S
.
Denote by
\mathcal{O}_{G}
the
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebra defined as the coordinate ring of
G
.
Also, let us fix
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Definition 1.3.1
.

(i)

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a
G
-bundle on
X
.
An
m
-PD stratification
on
\mathcal{E}/X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
(or simply, an
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
) is
a collection

(44)

\displaystyle\phi:=\{\phi_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}},

where each
\phi_{l}
denotes an isomorphism
P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\left(=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{l*}(\mathcal{E})\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}\times_{X}P^{l}_{(m)}\left(=\mathrm{pr}^{l*}_{1}(\mathcal{E})\right)

of
G
-bundles on
P_{(m)}^{l}

satisfying the following conditions:

–

\phi_{0}
coincides with the identity morphism
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}
of
\mathcal{E}\left(=\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{0*}(\mathcal{E})=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{0*}(\mathcal{E})\right)
, and the equality
\phi_{l^{\prime}}|_{P_{(m)}^{l}}=\phi_{l}
holds for any pair of integers

(l,l^{\prime})
with
l\leq l^{\prime}
;

–

The cocycle condition holds:
to be precise, for any pair of nonnegative integers
(l,l^{\prime})
, the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}\sharp*}(\phi_{l+l^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{q_{2}^{l,l^{\prime}*}(\phi_{l+l^{\prime}})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{q_{1}^{l,l^{\prime}*}(\phi_{l+l^{\prime}})}

where
q_{1}^{l,l^{\prime}}
and
q_{2}^{l,l^{\prime}}
denote the morphisms defined as

(49)

\displaystyle q_{1}^{l,l^{\prime}}:P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}P_{(m)}^{l}\hookrightarrow P_{(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ q_{2}^{l,l^{\prime}}:P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}P_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow P_{(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}},

respectively.

Also, by an
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundle

on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
, we mean a pair

(50)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E},\phi)

consisting of a
G
-bundle
\mathcal{E}
on
X
and an
m
-PD stratification
\phi
on
\mathcal{E}
.

(ii)

Let
(\mathcal{E}_{\circ},\phi_{\circ})
and

(\mathcal{E}_{\bullet},\phi_{\bullet})
be
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundles on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
, where
\phi_{\circ}:=\{\phi_{\circ,l}\}_{l}
,
\phi_{\bullet}:=\{\phi_{\bullet,l}\}_{l}
.
An
isomorphism of
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundles
from

(\mathcal{E}_{\circ},\phi_{\circ})
to
(\mathcal{E}_{\bullet},\phi_{\bullet})

is defined as an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
h:\mathcal{E}_{\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}_{\bullet}
such that, for each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, the following square diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}_{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\circ,l}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\times h}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{\circ}\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{h\times\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\bullet,l}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{\bullet}\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l}.}

Example 1.3.2

(Trivial
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundle)
.

The trivial
G
-bundle
X\times_{S}G
on
X
admits an
m
-PD stratification.
Indeed, for each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, let
\phi_{l,\mathrm{triv}}
denote the composite of natural morphisms

(55)

\displaystyle P^{l}_{(m)}\times_{X}(X\times_{S}G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}P^{l}_{(m)}\times_{S}G\xrightarrow{(a,b)\mapsto(b,a)}G\times_{S}P^{l}_{(m)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(X\times_{S}G)\times_{X}P^{l}_{(m)}.

Then, the resulting collection

(56)

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathrm{triv}}:=\{\phi_{l,\mathrm{triv}}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}

forms an
m
-PD stratification on
X\times_{S}G
, which will be called the
trivial
m
-PD stratification
.

Remark 1.3.3

(Case of
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a rank
n\left(>0\right)
vector bundle on
X
, and denote by

\mathcal{E}
the
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-bundle corresponding to
\mathcal{F}
.
Suppose that we are given an
m
-PD stratification
\phi:=\{\phi_{l}\}_{l}
on
\mathcal{E}
.
Since
\mathcal{E}
represents the sheaf
\mathcal{I}som(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus n},\mathcal{F})
,
each
\phi_{l}
(
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) may be regarded as an isomorphism

(57)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}som(\mathcal{O}_{P_{(m)}^{l}}^{\oplus n},\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{l*}(\mathcal{F}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{I}som(\mathcal{O}_{P_{(m)}^{l}}^{\oplus n},\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{l*}(\mathcal{F})),

which is given by composition with some isomorphism
\phi^{\natural}_{l}:\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{l*}(\mathcal{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{l*}(\mathcal{F})
.
The resulting collection
\phi^{\natural}:=\{\phi^{\natural}_{l}\}_{l}
defines
an
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{F}
in the classical sense.
Moreover, it determines a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}
(cf.
[
Mon
, Proposition 2.6.1]
).
The assignment
\phi\mapsto\phi^{\natural}
gives an equivalence between

m
-PD stratifications on
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structures on
\mathcal{F}
.

In what follows, we describe an
m
-PD stratification on a
G
-bundle
not only a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-bundle (cf. Remark
1.3.3
above)
by using
the classical notion of an
m
-PD stratification defined for an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a
G
-bundle on
X
.
Since
\mathcal{E}
is affine over
X
because of the affineness assumption on
G
, it determines an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra;
we shall write
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
for this
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra by abuse of notation (hence,
\mathcal{S}pec(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})=\mathcal{E}
).
If
\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathcal{E}\times_{S}G\rightarrow G
denotes
the
G
-action on
\mathcal{E}
, then it corresponds to
an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra morphism
\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{O}_{G}
. (We shall refer to
\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}
as the
G
-coaction
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
.)

Now, let

\phi:=\{\phi_{l}\}_{l}
be an
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
.
For each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, the isomorphism
\phi_{l}
defines a
\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}
-algebra isomorphism

(58)

\displaystyle\phi^{\natural}_{l}:\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}.

The
G
-equivariance condition on
\phi_{l}
can be interpreted as the commutativity of the following square diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\natural}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{O}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{l}^{\natural}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{O}_{G}\left(=(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{O}_{G})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\right).}

Moreover, the commutativity of (
–
‣
(i)
) reads the commutativity of the following diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{m}^{l+l^{\prime}\sharp*}(\phi^{\natural}_{l+l^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{q_{1}^{l,l^{\prime}*}(\phi^{\natural}_{l+l^{\prime}})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}^{l}_{(m)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}^{l^{\prime}}_{(m)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{q_{2}^{l,l^{\prime}*}(\phi^{\natural}_{l+l^{\prime}})}

Thus,
the resulting collection

(67)

\displaystyle\phi^{\natural}:=\{\phi^{\natural}_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}

forms an
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
in the usual sense (cf.
[
PBer1
, Definition 2.3.1]
).

Conversely, suppose that we are given an
m
-PD stratification
\phi^{\natural}:=\{\phi_{l}^{\natural}\}_{l\geq 0}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
such that each
\phi^{\natural}_{l}
is a
\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}
-algebra isomorphism
\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}
and
the square diagram (
1.3
)
for this collection is commutative.
By applying the functor
\mathcal{S}pec(-)
to various
\phi_{l}^{\natural}
’s,
we obtain a collection
of isomorphisms
P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l}
(
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) forming
an
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
.

Remark 1.3.4

(
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module associated to an
m
-PD stratification)
.

One may verify that giving a collection as in (
67
) is equivalent to giving
a
compatible
collection

(68)

\displaystyle\phi^{\natural\natural}:=\{\phi_{l}^{\natural\natural}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}},

where each
\phi_{l}^{\natural\natural}
denotes an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}
, satisfying the following two conditions:

•

For each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, the following square diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{l}^{\natural\natural}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{O}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{l}^{\natural\natural}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{O}_{G}\left(=(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{O}_{G})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\right).}

•

The equality
\phi_{0}^{\natural\natural}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}
holds, and for each pair of nonnegative integers
(l,l^{\prime})
, the following square diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\otimes\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{l^{\prime}}^{\natural\natural}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{l+l^{\prime}}^{\natural\natural}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{l}^{\natural\natural}\otimes\mathrm{id}}

In particular, the collection
\phi^{\natural\natural}
may be regarded as a left
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
(cf.
[
PBer1
, Proposition 2.3.2]
).

Remark 1.3.5

(Case of
m=0
)
.

Recall from
[
Wak6
, Definition 1.28]
the notion of a
flat
G
-bundle
on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
(Although all the schemes and algebraic groups appearing in
loc. cit.
are defined over a field, the various formulations related to flat
G
-bundles can be generalized to the situation where
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
and
G
are as introduced at the beginning of §
1.2
and §
1.3
, respectively.)
Then, by applying an argument similar to the proof of

[
Wak5
, Proposition 7.8.1]
(which deals with the non-logarithmic case), we see that there exists
an equivalence of categories

(77)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of }\\
\text{$0$-PD stratified $G$-bundles on $X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}$}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of }\\
\text{flat $G$-bundles on $X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}$}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

On that basis,
whenever a
0
-PD stratification is mentioned without fixing a (
0
-)PD structure
on
S
, it is assumed to mean a flat connection.

Let
(\mathcal{E},\phi)
(where
\phi:=\{\phi_{l}\}_{l}
) be an
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundle on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Also, let
G^{\prime}
be another smooth affine algebraic group over
S
and
w:G\rightarrow G^{\prime}
a morphism of
S
-groups.
Denote by
\mathcal{E}\times^{G,w}G^{\prime}
, or simply by
\mathcal{E}\times^{G}G^{\prime}
, the
G^{\prime}
-bundle induced from
\mathcal{E}
via change of structure group along
w
.
For each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
the isomorphism
\phi_{l}
induces a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant isomorphism

(78)

\displaystyle\phi_{l}\times^{G}G^{\prime}:P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}(\mathcal{E}\times^{G}G^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{E}\times^{G}G^{\prime})\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l}.

The collection

(79)

\displaystyle\phi\times^{G}G^{\prime}:=\{\phi_{l}\times^{G}G^{\prime}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}

forms
an
m
-PD stratified
G^{\prime}
-bundle on
\mathcal{E}\times^{G}G^{\prime}
.

1.4.
Relative Frobenius morphisms

In the rest of this section,
we suppose that

S
is a scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
(equipped with the trivial
m
-PD structure).
We shall write

F_{X}
and
F_{S}
for
the absolute Frobenius (i.e.,
p
-power) endomorphisms of
X
and
S
, respectively.
Also, let us fix a nonnegative integer
m
.
The
(m+1)
-st Frobenius twist of
X
over
S
is, by definition, the base-change
X^{(m+1)}\left(:=X\times_{S,F_{S}^{m+1}}S\right)
of
X
along
the
(m+1)
-st iterate
F_{S}^{m+1}
of
F_{S}
.
Denote by
f^{(m+1)}:X^{(m+1)}\rightarrow S
the structure morphism of

X^{(m+1)}
, which defines a log curve

X^{(m+1)\mathrm{log}}:=X^{(m+1)}\times_{X}X^{\mathrm{log}}
over
S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
The
(m+1)
-st relative Frobenius morphism of
X
over
S
is the unique morphism
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)}:X\rightarrow X^{(m+1)}
over
S
that makes the following diagram commute:

To simplify the notation,
we write
F_{X/S}:=F_{X/S}^{(1)}
, and write
\Omega^{(m+1)}:=\Omega_{X^{(m+1)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
,
\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)}:=\mathcal{T}_{X^{(m+1)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
Also, for convenience, we occasionally write
X^{(0)}
,
f^{(0)}
,
\Omega^{(0)}
, and
\mathcal{T}^{(0)}
instead of
X
,
f
,
\Omega
, and
\mathcal{T}
, respectively.

Remark 1.4.1

(Cartier type)
.

Recall from
[
FKa
, Theorem 1.1]
and
[
Ogu1
, Remark 1.2.3]
(or the comment following
[
KKa
, Definition (4.8)]
) that the log curve
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}

is
of Cartier type
.
Hence, the exact relative Frobenius map in the statement of
[
Ogu2
, Theorem 3.1.1]
coincides with the usual relative Frobenius morphism
F_{X/S}
.

Proposition 1.4.2
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be
a relatively torsion-free sheaf on
X
of rank
n>0
(cf.
[
Ses
, Chap. 7, Définition 1]
or
[
Wak6
, Definition 3.1]
for the definition of a relatively torsion-free sheaf).
Then, the
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-module
F_{X/S*}^{(m+1)}(\mathcal{F})

is a relatively torsion-free sheaf of rank
n\cdot p^{m+1}
.

Proof.

First, let us consider the case where
m=0
.
Since
F_{X/S*}(\mathcal{F})
is flat over
S
,
we may assume, after restricting to the fiber over each geometric point of
S
, that

S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
for an algebraically closed field
k
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Recall from
[
FKa
, Theorem 1.1]
that
X
has at most nodal singularities.
When restricted to the smooth locus
X^{\mathrm{sm}}
of
X
,
\mathcal{F}
becomes locally free and
F_{X/k}
becomes finite and flat of degree
p
.
This implies that
F_{X/k*}(\mathcal{F})|_{X^{\mathrm{sm}(1)}}
is locally free (and hence, relatively torsion-free) of rank
n\cdot p
.
Hence, the problem is reduced to proving that
F_{X/k*}(\mathcal{F})
is (relatively) torsion-free of rank
n\cdot p
at each nodal point of
X
.

Let us take
a nodal point
q
of
X
, and write
q^{(1)}:=F_{X/k}(q)\in X^{(1)}(k)
.
Denote by
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{q}
(resp.,
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,q}
; resp.,
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(1)},q^{(1)}}
) the completion of the stalk of
\mathcal{F}
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}_{X}
; resp.,
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}
) at
q
(resp.,
q
; resp.,
q^{(1)}
) with respect to its maximal ideal
and by
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{X,q}
(resp.,
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{X^{(1)},q^{(1)}}
) the maximal ideal of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,q}
(resp.,
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(1)},q^{(1)}}
).
It follows from
[
Ses
, Chap. 8, Proposition 2]

that
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{q}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,q}^{\oplus a}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{X,q}^{\oplus(n-a)}
for some
a\in\{0,\cdots,n\}
.
Hence, it suffices to consider the cases where
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{q}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,q}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{q}\cong\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{X,q}
.
Since the former case was essentially proved in
[
Wak6
, Proposition 3.2]
, we only consider the latter case, i.e.,
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{q}\cong\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{X,q}
.

Recall that
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,q}\cong k[\![x,y]\!]/(xy)
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{X,q}=x\cdot k[\![x]\!]\oplus y\cdot k[\![y]\!]
.
By using the injection
F_{X/k}^{*}:\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(1)},q^{(1)}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,q}
induced by
F_{X/k}
, we shall identify
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(1)},q^{(1)}}
with the subring
k[\![x^{p},y^{p}]\!]/(x^{p}y^{p})
of
k[\![x,y]\!]/(xy)
.
In particular, we have
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{X^{(1)},q^{(1)}}\cong x^{p}\cdot k[\![x^{p}]\!]\oplus y^{p}\cdot k[\![y^{p}]\!]
.
Let us consider the
k[\![x^{p},y^{p}]\!]/(x^{p}y^{p})
-linear morphism

(86)

(x^{p}\cdot k[\![x^{p}]\!]\oplus y^{p}\cdot k[\![y^{p}]\!])^{\oplus p}\rightarrow x\cdot k[\![x]\!]\oplus y\cdot k[\![y]\!]

given by

(87)

(x^{p}\cdot A_{i}+y^{p}\cdot B_{i})_{i=1}^{p}\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{p}(x^{i}\cdot A_{i}+y^{i}\cdot B_{i})

for
any
A_{i}\in k[\![x^{p}]\!]
and
B_{i}\in k[\![y^{p}]\!]
(
i=1,\cdots,p
).
This morphism is verified to be bijective.
It follows that
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{X,q}
is isomorphic to
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{X^{(1)},q^{(1)}}^{\oplus p}
, which is relatively torsion-free.
This completes the proof of the assertion for
m=0
.

Moreover, the assertion for
m>0
can be proved by successively applying the assertion just proved.
Thus, we have finished the proof of this proposition.
∎

1.5.
p^{m+1}
-curvature

The image of the natural morphism

\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\leq p^{m+1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(m+1)}_{\leq p^{m+1}}
coincides with
\mathcal{D}^{(m+1)}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}
.
If
\varpi:\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\leq p^{m+1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(m+1)}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}
denotes the resulting surjection, then the composite

(88)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\leq p^{m+1}}\xrightarrow{\varpi}\mathcal{D}^{(m+1)}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}

is an isomorphism.
The composite
\varpi^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\leq p^{m+1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}
of
\varpi
and the inverse of (
88
)
defines a split surjection of the short exact sequence

(89)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}^{(m)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m)}\longrightarrow\left(\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m)}/\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}^{(m)}=\right)\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{m+1}}\longrightarrow 0.

Thus, we obtain the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear composite

(90)

\displaystyle\psi_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}:F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)})\left(=\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{m+1}}\right)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m)}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)},

where the first arrow denotes the split injection of (
89
) corresponding to
\varpi^{\prime}
.
Note that this morphism coincides, via the adjunction relation
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(-)\dashv F_{X/S*}^{(m+1)}(-)
, with the
p^{m+1}
-curvature map
\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)}\rightarrow F_{X/S*}^{(m+1)}(\mathcal{D}^{(m)})
discussed in
[
Ohk
, Definition 3.10]
.

If

x
is a logarithmic coordinate as in §
1.2
and
\partial
denotes the dual base of
d\mathrm{log}(x)
, then

\psi_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
sends
\partial^{\otimes p^{m+1}}
to
\partial^{\langle p^{m+1}\rangle}
(cf.
[
Ohk
, Proposition 3.11]
).

Definition 1.5.1
.

(i)

Let
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module.
The composite

(91)

\displaystyle\psi(\nabla):F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)})\left(=\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{m+1}}\right)\xrightarrow{\psi_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})

is called the
p^{m+1}
-curvature (map)
of
\nabla
.
Also, we shall say that
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
is
p^{m+1}
-flat
, or
dormant
, if
\psi(\nabla)=0
.

(ii)

Let
(\mathcal{E},\phi)
be an
m
-stratified
G
-bundle on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
The
p^{m+1}
-curvature (map)
of
\phi
is defined to be the
p^{m+1}
-curvature

(92)

\displaystyle\psi(\phi):=\psi(\phi^{\natural\natural}):F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)})\left(=\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{m+1}}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})

of
the
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
\phi^{\natural\natural}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
corresponding to
\phi
(cf. Remark
1.3.4
).
Also, we shall say that
(\mathcal{E},\phi)
is
p^{m+1}
-flat
, or
dormant
, if
\psi(\phi)=0
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Definition 3.8]
for the case of
m=0
).

Remark 1.5.2

(The
p^{m+1}
-curvature of a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-bundle)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{E}
be as in Remark
1.3.3
.
Also, let
\nabla
be a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}
, and denote by
\phi
the
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
corresponding to
\nabla
via the equivalence mentioned in Remark
1.3.3
.
Then, it is immediately verified that
\nabla
has vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature
if and only if
\phi
has vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature.
In the case where
m=0
and
G
is an algebraic group as before,
(
77
) restricts to
an equivalence of categories

(93)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of $p$-flat $0$-PD stratified}\\
\text{$G$-bundles on $X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}$}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of }\\
\text{$p$-flat $G$-bundles on $X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}$}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Remark 1.5.3

(Classical definition)
.

When the log structures of
S
and
X
are trivial,
the notion of
p^{m+1}
-curvature defined above is essentially the same as the notion of
p
-
m
-curvature
introduced in
[
LSQ
, Definition 3.1.1]
(for
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules) and
[
Wak5
, Definition 7.7.1]
(for
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundles).
In particular, a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module or an
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundle is
p^{m+1}
-flat in the sense of Definition
1.5.1
if and only if it has vanishing
p
-
m
-curvature in the classical sense.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-module.
By the comment at the beginning of
[
Wak6
, § 4.10.3]
,
there exists a canonical
\mathcal{D}_{X^{(m)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(0)}
-module structure on

F_{X^{(m)}/S}^{*}(\mathcal{G})
.
Moreover,
by applying
[
Mon
, Corollaire 3.3.1]
to this,
we obtain a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure

(94)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathcal{G},\mathrm{can}}^{(m)}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{G}))

on the pull-back
F^{(m+1)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})\left(=F^{(m)*}_{X/S}(F_{X^{(m)}/S}^{*}(\mathcal{G}))\right)
.
It is immediately verified that
\nabla_{\mathcal{G},\mathrm{can}}^{(m)}
has vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature.

Definition 1.5.4
.

We shall refer to
\nabla_{\mathcal{G},\mathrm{can}}^{(m)}
as the
canonical
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
on
F^{(m+1)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})
.

Note that, for a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
, the subsheaf
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)
of
\mathcal{F}
(cf. (
40
)) may be regarded as an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-module via the underlying homeomorphism of
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)}
.
Here, suppose that
the relative characteristic
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
(cf.
[
FKa
, Introduction]
) is trivial, which implies that
X/S
is smooth and
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}=\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
.
Then, the assignments
\mathcal{G}\mapsto(F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{G}),\nabla_{\mathcal{G},\mathrm{can}}^{(m)})
and
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)\mapsto\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)
determine an equivalence of categories

(95)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the category of }\\
\text{$\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}$-modules}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the category of $\mathcal{D}^{(m)}$-modules}\\
\text{ with vanishing $p^{m+1}$-curvature}\\
\end{pmatrix}

(cf.
[
LSQ
, Corollary 3.2.4]
).

1.6.
Cartier operator of a
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module

Let
(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
be a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module.
For an integer
a
with
0\leq a\leq m+1
, we set

(96)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{[a]}:=\begin{cases}\mathcal{F}&\text{if $a=0$};\\
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow(a-1)})&\text{if $a>0$}.\end{cases}

In particular,
\mathcal{F}^{[a]}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(a)}}
-module.

In what follows, let us define an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on the
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(a)}}
-module
\mathcal{F}^{[a]}
for
a=0,\cdots,m
.
First, we shall set
\nabla^{[0]}
(or
(\nabla^{(m)})^{[0]}
)
:=\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}
.
Next, let us choose
a\in\{1,\cdots,m\}
.
Since

\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{a}-1}^{(a)}=\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{D}^{(a-1)}_{\leq p^{a}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(a)}_{\leq p^{a}}\right)
and

\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{a}}^{(a)}/\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{a}-1}^{(a)}=\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{a}}
, we obtain
an exact sequence

(97)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{(a-1)}_{\leq p^{a}}\cap\mathcal{D}^{(a-1)}_{+}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(a)}_{\leq p^{a}}\cap\mathcal{D}^{(a)}_{+}\xrightarrow{\delta}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{a}}\left(=F_{X/S}^{(a)*}(\mathcal{T}^{(a)})\right)\rightarrow 0,

where the first arrow is the morphism obtained by restricting the morphism
\mathcal{D}^{(a-1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(a)}
.
Let us take a local section
\partial
of
\mathcal{T}^{(a)}
.
There exists locally a section
\widetilde{\partial}
in
\delta^{-1}((F_{X/S}^{(a)})^{-1}(\partial))\left(\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{(a)}_{\leq p^{a}}\cap\mathcal{D}^{(a)}_{+}\right)
.
The exactness of (
97
) and the definition of
\mathcal{F}^{[a]}
imply that
the
f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-linear endomorphism
\nabla_{\partial}^{[a]}:=\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow{(a)}}(\widetilde{\partial})
of
\mathcal{F}^{[a]}
does not depend on the choice of
\widetilde{\partial}
(i.e., depends only on
\partial
).
Hence, the morphism

(98)

\displaystyle\nabla^{[a]}\left(\text{or}\ (\nabla^{(m)})^{[a]}\right):\mathcal{F}^{[a]}\rightarrow\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]}

determined by assigning
\partial\mapsto\nabla_{\partial}^{[a]}
is well-defined, and
this is verified to form an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection.
Thus, we obtain a flat module

(99)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}^{[a]},\nabla^{[a]})

on the log curve
X^{(a)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
The collection
\{\mathcal{F}^{[a]}\}_{0\leq a\leq m+1}
defines a decreasing filtration on
\mathcal{F}
such that
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})=\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]}
for every
a=0,1,\cdots,m
.

Proposition 1.6.1
.

Let us keep the above notation.

(i)

If
\nabla^{(m)}
has vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature,
then
\nabla^{[m]}
has vanishing
p
-curvature.

(ii)

For every
a=0,\cdots,m-1
,
the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
\nabla^{[a]}

has vanishing
p
-curvature.

Proof.

First, we shall prove assertion (i).
Denote by
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]})
) the subsheaf of
\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F}^{[m]})
)
consisting of endomorphisms preserving the
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla^{(m)}
(resp., the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
\nabla^{[m]}
).
The sheaf
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]})
) is equipped with an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-module structure in a natural manner.
The
p^{m+1}
-curvature
\psi(\nabla^{(m)}):F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})
of
\nabla^{(m)}
(resp., the
p
-curvature
\psi(\nabla^{[m]}):F_{X^{(m)}/S}^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F}^{[m]})
of
\nabla^{[m]}
) restricts to an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-linear morphism

(100)

\displaystyle\psi(\nabla^{(m)})^{\nabla}:\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \psi(\nabla^{[m]})^{\nabla}:\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]})\right).

We obtain an equivalence

(101)

\displaystyle\psi(\nabla^{(m)})=0\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \psi(\nabla^{(m)})^{\nabla}=0\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \psi(\nabla^{[m]})=0\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \psi(\nabla^{[m]})^{\nabla}=0\right).

Hence, the assertion follows from (
101
) and the commutativity of the following diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi(\nabla^{[m]})^{\nabla}}
\scriptstyle{\psi(\nabla^{(m)})^{\nabla}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]}),}
