where the lower horizontal arrow
\mathrm{Res}
denotes the morphism given by
h\mapsto h|_{\mathcal{F}^{[m]}}
for any
h\in\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
.

To prove assertion (ii),
we note that the
\mathcal{D}^{(a)}
-module structure
\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow(a)}
has vanishing
p^{(a+1)}
-curvature.
Hence, the assertion can be proved by the same argument as the proof of the former assertion of (i), where
\nabla^{(m)}
and
\nabla^{[m]}
are replaced by
\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow(a)}
and
\nabla^{(a)}
, respectively.
∎

The following assertion is a slight generalization of
[
Wak6
, Proposition 3.2]
.

Proposition 1.6.2
.

Let us keep the above notation.
Suppose that
\psi(\nabla^{(m)})=0
and that
\mathcal{F}
is a relatively torsion-free sheaf of rank
n>0
.
Also, let us fix an integer
a
with
0\leq a\leq m+1
.

(i)

The
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(a)}}
-module
\mathcal{F}^{[a]}
is relatively torsion-free of rank
n
.
In particular, the
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-module
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{(m)})
is a relatively torision-free sheaf of rank
n
.

(ii)

The formation of
(\mathcal{F}^{[a]},\nabla^{[a]})
commutes with base-change over
S
-schemes.
To be precise, let
s:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
be a morphism of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-schemes, and use the notation “
s^{*}(-)
” to denote the result of base-changing along
s
.
(In particular, we obtain a
\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime\mathrm{log}}/S^{\prime\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)}
-module
(s^{*}(\mathcal{F}),s^{*}(\nabla^{(m)}))
, where
X^{\prime\mathrm{log}}:=S^{\prime}\times_{S^{\mathrm{log}}}X^{\mathrm{log}}
.)
Then, the natural morphism of flat modules

(106)

\displaystyle(s^{*}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]}),s^{*}(\nabla^{[a]}))\rightarrow((s^{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{[a]},(s^{*}(\nabla^{(m)}))^{[a]})

constructed inductively on
a
is an isomorphism.
In particular, the natural morphism
s^{*}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{(m)}))\rightarrow\mathcal{S}ol(s^{*}(\nabla^{(m)}))
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

There is nothing to prove when
a=0
.
Assertions (i) and (ii) for
a=1
can be proved by arguments entirely similar to the proof of
[
Wak6
, Proposition 6.13]
together with Proposition
1.4.2
for
m=0
.
Moreover, the remaining cases, i.e., the assertions for
a>1
, can be proved by successively applying the assertions for
a=1
and Proposition
1.6.1
.
∎

Remark 1.6.3

(Local case)
.

Assertion (ii) of Proposition
1.6.2
remains true (for the same reason) even if we replace
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
with
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S
or
U_{\otimes}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
introduced in §
3.1
later.
The assertion for these situations will be used in the proof of Proposition-Definition
3.4.1
.

Corollary 1.6.4
.

Let us keep the above notation.
Suppose that either
\mathcal{F}
is relatively torsion-free or the relative characteristic
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is trivial.
Then, the converse of Proposition
1.6.1
, (i), is true.
To be precise,
\nabla^{(m)}
has vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature if
\nabla^{[m]}
has vanishing
p
-curvature.

Proof.

Denote by
\iota:U\hookrightarrow X^{(m+1)}
the open subscheme of
X^{(m+1)}
where
the relative characteristic of
X^{(m+1)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is trivial.
Since
U
is scheme-theoretically dense (cf.
[
FKa
, Lemma 1.4]
),
the assumption imposed above and Propositions
1.4.2
and
1.6.2
together imply that
the natural morphisms

(107)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})\rightarrow\iota_{*}(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)}))),\hskip 8.53581pt\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]})\rightarrow\iota_{*}(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]})))

are injective.
By the equivalence of categories (
95
),
there exists a canonical isomorphism

(108)

\displaystyle\iota_{*}(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\iota_{*}(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]}))),

which is compatible with

\mathrm{Res}
(in (
1.6
))
via the morphisms (
107
).
It follows that
the morphism
\mathrm{Res}
is injective.
Hence, the assertion follows from this fact together with (
101
) and the commutativity of (
1.6
).
∎

Next, suppose further that
(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
is
p^{m+1}
-flat.
Let us take an integer
a
with
0\leq a\leq m
.
Recall from
[
Ogu1
, Proposition 1.2.4]
that the
Cartier operator
associated to
(\mathcal{F}^{[a]},\nabla^{[a]})
is, by definition, an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(a+1)}}
-linear morphism

(109)

\displaystyle\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]}\rightarrow\Omega^{(a+1)}\otimes F_{X^{(a)}/S*}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})

satisfying a certain condition, where the domain of this morphism is regarded as an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(a+1)}}
-module via
F_{X^{(a)}/S}
.
Since
(\mathcal{F}^{[a]},\nabla^{[a]})
has vanishing
p
-curvature,
the image of this morphism lies in
\Omega^{(a+1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]}\left(=\Omega^{(a+1)}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})\right)
(cf. the comment following
[
Ogu1
, Proposition 1.2.4]
).
Hence, by restricting the codomain of (
109
),
we obtain an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(a+1)}}
-linear morphism

(110)

\displaystyle C_{(\mathcal{F}^{[a]},\nabla^{[a]})}:\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]}\rightarrow\Omega^{(a+1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]}.

Moreover,
the composite
C_{(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]})}\circ\cdots\circ C_{(\mathcal{F}^{[1]},\nabla^{[1]})}\circ C_{(\mathcal{F}^{[0]},\nabla^{[0]})}

determines an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-linear morphism

(111)

\displaystyle C_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})}:\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{(m+1)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{(m)}).

Definition 1.6.5
.

We shall refer to
C_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})}
as the
Cartier operator
of
(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
.

Remark 1.6.6

(Functoriality of the Cartier operator)
.

The formation of the Cartier operators is functorial in the following sense:
Let
h:(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ})\rightarrow(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet})
be a morphism of
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules.
Then, this morphism restricts to an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{S}ol(h):\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\circ})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\bullet})
, and the following square diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C_{(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ})}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\otimes h}
\textstyle{\Omega^{(m+1)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\circ})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(h)}
\textstyle{\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C_{(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet})}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{(m+1)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\bullet}^{(m)}).}

1.7.
Dual of the Cartier operator

Suppose that the relative characteristic
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is trivial (which implies that
X/S
is smooth).
Let
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module with
\mathcal{F}
locally free.
By Grothendieck-Serre duality, there exists a canonical isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules

(116)

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}^{(a)}:f_{*}^{(a)}(\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]\vee})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}^{(a)}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})^{\vee}

for each
a=0,\cdots,m+1
.
Also, the inclusion
\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{[a]}
(
a=0,\cdots m
) induces an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-linear morphism

(117)

\displaystyle\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$C$}\hskip-1.42262pt_{\mathrm{Ker}}^{\,(a)}:\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}^{(a)}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}^{(a+1)}(\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]})^{\vee}.

On the other hand,
we obtain the composite isomorphism

(118)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{H}om(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}),\Omega^{(a+1)})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{H}om(\Omega^{(a+1)}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}),\Omega^{(a+1)})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})^{\vee}\left(=\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]\vee}\right),

where the first arrow follows from
[
Wak6
, Proposition 6.15]

and the second arrow denotes the isomorphism induced by
C_{(\mathcal{F}^{[a]},\nabla^{[a]})}
.
Under the identification
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})=\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]\vee}
given by (
118
),
the Cartier operator
C_{(\mathcal{F}^{[a]\vee},\nabla^{[a]\vee})}

determines a morphism

(119)

\displaystyle\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$C$}\hskip-1.42262pt_{\mathrm{Coker}}^{\,(a)}:f_{*}^{(l)}(\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]\vee})\rightarrow f_{*}^{(a+1)}(\Omega^{(a+1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]\vee}).

Then, one may verify that the following square diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{f_{*}^{(a)}(\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$C$}\hskip-1.42262pt_{\mathrm{Coker}}^{\,(a)}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\int_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}^{(a)}}
\textstyle{f_{*}^{(a+1)}(\Omega^{(a+1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\int_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}^{(a+1)}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}^{(a)}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$C$}\hskip-1.42262pt_{\mathrm{Ker}}^{\,(a)}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}^{(a+1)}(\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]})^{\vee}.}

By combining the diagrams (
1.7
) for various
a
’s,
we obtain a commutative square diagram

\textstyle{f_{*}(\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$C$}\hskip-1.42262pt}_{\mathrm{Coker}}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\int_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}^{(0)}}
\textstyle{f_{*}^{(m+1)}(\Omega^{[m+1]}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\int_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}^{(m+1)}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{F})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$C$}\hskip-1.42262pt}_{\mathrm{Ker}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}^{(m+1)}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla))^{\vee}.}

2.
Diagonal reductions/liftings of flat modules

In this section, we discuss the constructive definition of a dormant flat module in prime-power characteristic, partly on the basis of the argument of
[
Moc2
, § 2.1, Chap. II]
.
At the same time, both the diagonal reduction of a dormant flat bundle and a diagonal lifting of a
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module are defined.
Also, we describe a map between certain deformation spaces induced by the operation of taking the diagonal reductions in terms of Cartier operator (cf. Proposition
2.4.4
).

Throughout this section, we fix a nonnegative integer
l
.
For an integer
n>1
, we denote by
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
the projective linear group of rank
n
.

2.1.
Dormant flat modules

Let

S^{\mathrm{log}}
be an fs log scheme whose underlying scheme
S
is flat over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
Also, let
f^{\mathrm{log}}:X^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{log}}

be a log curve over
S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
For each integer
m
with

0\leq m\leq l
, we will denote by a subscript
m
the result of reducing an object over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
modulo
p^{m+1}
.
In particular, we obtain a log curve
X_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
.

Let us fix an integer
m
with
0\leq m\leq l
.
For simplicity, we write
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{0}:=\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{X_{0}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}_{0}}
,
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}:=\mathcal{D}^{(0)}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
(cf. the comment at the end of §
1.1
), and
\Omega^{(m)}:=\Omega_{X^{(m)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
Denote by
\mathrm{Diag}^{\prime}_{m}
the set of pairs

(128)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})

where

•

\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
is a flat module
on
X_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
such that
\mathcal{F}
is relatively torsion-free;

•

\nabla_{0}^{(m)}
denotes a
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}_{0}
(= the reduction modulo
p
of
\mathcal{F}
).

In what follows, we shall define a subset
\mathrm{Diag}_{m}
of
\mathrm{Diag}^{\prime}_{m}
inductively on
m
.

First, we set
\mathrm{Diag}_{0}
to be the subset of
\mathrm{Diag}^{\prime}_{0}

consisting of pairs
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(0)})
, where
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
,
such that
\nabla_{0}^{(0)}
coincides with
\nabla
and has vanishing
p
-curvature.

Next, suppose that we have defined a subset
\mathrm{Diag}_{m-1}
of
\mathrm{Diag}^{\prime}_{m-1}
for
m\geq 1
.
Let us take an element

(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})

of
\mathrm{Diag}^{\prime}_{m}
, where
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
,
such that

(\mathscr{F}_{m-1},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})\in\mathrm{Diag}_{m-1}
.
We shall set

(129)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}:=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})\left(=\mathcal{F}_{0}^{[m]}\right),

which may be regarded as an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m)}}
-module.
As we will prove below (cf. Proposition
2.1.1
, (i)),
the pair
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}

satisfy the following properties:

(\alpha)_{m-1}

:

\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)}
has vanishing
p^{m}
-curvature;

(\beta)_{m-1}

:
Every local section of
\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{0}\right)

can be lifted, locally on
X
, to a horizontal section in
\mathcal{F}_{m-1}
with respect to the
S_{m-1}^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
\nabla_{m-1}
.

Now, let us choose
a local section
v
of
\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}
defined over an open subscheme
U
of
X
.
By the property
(\beta)_{m-1}
described above,
there exists, after possibly shrinking
U
,
a section
\widetilde{v}
of
\mathcal{F}
which is a lifting of
v

and horizontal modulo
p^{m}
with respect to
\nabla
.
Then,
\nabla(\widetilde{v})
lies in
p^{m}\cdot\Omega_{m}\otimes\mathcal{F}
.
By the flatness of
X_{m}
over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
,
we can divide it by
p^{m}
to obtain a local section
of
\Omega_{0}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{0}
; we denote this section by
\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{v})
.
Because of the property
(\alpha)_{m-1}
, the
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m-1)}
-module
(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})
associates the Cartier operator

(130)

\displaystyle C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})}:\Omega_{0}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{0}\rightarrow\Omega^{(m)}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}

(cf. (
111
)).
The image of
\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{v})
via
this morphism
specifies a local section
w
of
\Omega^{(m)}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}
.
The section
w
does not depend on the choice of the lifting
\widetilde{v}
,
and the resulting assignment
v\mapsto w
determines a well-defined
S_{0}^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection

(131)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathscr{F}}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow\Omega^{(m)}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}

on the
\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}^{(m)}}
-module

\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}
.
In particular, we obtain a flat module

(132)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}},\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})

on the log curve
X_{0}^{(m)\mathrm{log}}/S_{0}^{\mathrm{log}}
.

Then, we shall define

(133)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Diag}_{m}

to be the set of pairs
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
as above satisfying that
\nabla^{[m]}_{0}=\nabla_{\mathscr{F}}

and
\psi(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})=0
.
The assignments
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})\mapsto(\mathscr{F}_{m-1},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})
(
m=1,2,\cdots,l
) yields a sequence of sets

(134)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Diag}_{l}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\mathrm{Diag}_{m}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\mathrm{Diag}_{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Diag}_{0}.

The following assertion was used in the inductive construction of
\mathrm{Diag}_{m}
just discussed.

Proposition 2.1.1
.

Let
m
be an integer with
0\leq m\leq l
.

(i)

Each element
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
(where
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
) of
\mathrm{Diag}_{m}

satisfies the following three properties:

(\alpha)_{m}

:

\nabla_{0}^{(m)}
has vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature;

(\beta)_{m}

:
Every local section of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})\left(=\mathcal{F}_{0}^{[m+1]}\right)
can be lifted, locally on
X
, to a horizontal section in
\mathcal{F}
with respect to the
S_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
\nabla
;

(\gamma)_{m}

:
For every
a=0,\cdots,m
, we have
(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}_{a}},\nabla_{\mathscr{F}_{a}})=(\mathcal{F}_{0}^{[a]},(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{[a]})
;

(ii)

Let
(\mathscr{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ,0}^{(m)})
and
(\mathscr{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet,0}^{(m)})
(where
\mathscr{F}_{\circ}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ})
and
\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet})
) be elements of
\mathrm{Diag}_{m}

such that
\mathscr{F}_{\circ}=\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}
.
Then, the equality
\nabla_{\circ,0}^{(m)}=\nabla_{\bullet,0}^{(m)}
holds.

Proof.

First,
we shall prove assertion (i) inductively on
m
.
There is nothing to prove for the base step, i.e., the case of
m=0
.
In order to discuss the induction step, suppose that we have proved the three properties
(\alpha)_{m^{\prime}}
,
(\beta)_{m^{\prime}}
, and
(\gamma)_{m^{\prime}}
(
0\leq m^{\prime}<m
) for any element of
\mathrm{Diag}_{m^{\prime}}
, in particular, for
(\mathscr{F}_{m^{\prime}},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m^{\prime})})
.
Since the equality

(135)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}},\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})=(\mathcal{F}_{0}^{[m]},(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{[m]})

holds,
the property
(\gamma)_{m-1}
for
(\mathscr{F}_{m-1},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})
implies that

(\gamma)_{m}
is satisfied for
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
.
Moreover, by (
135
), the assertion
(\alpha)_{m}
follows from Corollary
1.6.4
and the assumption
\psi(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})=0
.
To consider the property
(\beta)_{m}
,
let us take a local section
v
of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})\left(\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}\right)
.
Just as in the discussion at the beginning of this subsection,
we can choose (locally on
X
) local sections
\widetilde{v}
and
\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{v})
associated to
v
.
The following sequence of equalities holds:

(136)

\displaystyle C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})}\left(\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{v})\right)

\displaystyle=C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0}^{[m]},(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{[m]})}\left(C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})}\left(\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{v})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0}^{[m]},(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{[m]})}(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}}(v))

\displaystyle=C_{(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}},\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})}(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}}(v))

\displaystyle=0.

It follows that
the integer

(137)

\displaystyle a(\widetilde{v}):=\mathrm{min}\left\{a\,\Biggl|\,-1\leq a\leq m,\ C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a)})}\left(\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{v})\right)=0\right\}

is well-defined, where
C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(-1)})}:=\mathrm{id}
.
Now, suppose that
a(\widetilde{v})\geq 0
.
We shall set
u:=C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a(\widetilde{v})-1)})}\left(\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{v})\right)
, which is nonzero by the definition of
a(\widetilde{v})
.
Observe that

(138)

\displaystyle C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0}^{[a(\widetilde{v})]},(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{[a(\widetilde{v})]})}(u)=C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a(\widetilde{v}))})}\left(\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{v})\right)=0.

Hence, by the comment following
[
Ogu1
, Proposition 1.2.4]
, there exists (locally on
X
) a local section
u^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{F}_{0}^{[a(\widetilde{v})]}

satisfying
(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{[a(\widetilde{v})]}(u^{\prime})=u
.
Let us choose
a section
\widetilde{u}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{F}_{a(\widetilde{v})}
which is
a lifting of
u^{\prime}
and horizontal modulo
p^{a(\widetilde{v})}

with respect to
\nabla_{a(\widetilde{v})}
.
(Even if
a(\widetilde{v})>0
, such a section always exists locally on
X
because of
the equality
\mathcal{F}_{0}^{[a(\widetilde{v})]}=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a(\widetilde{v})-1)})
and the induction hypothesis, i.e., the property
(\beta)_{a(\widetilde{v})-1}
for
(\mathscr{F}_{a(\widetilde{v})-1},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a(\widetilde{v})-1)})
.)
By the flatness of
X_{m}
over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
,
we obtain a well-defined element
p^{m-a(\widetilde{v})}\cdot\widetilde{u}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{F}
.
If we write
\widetilde{v}^{\prime}:=\widetilde{v}-p^{m-a(\widetilde{v})}\cdot\widetilde{u}^{\prime}
, then
the following sequence of equalities holds:

(139)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a(\widetilde{v})-1)})}\left(\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{v}^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a(\widetilde{v})-1)})}\left(\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{v})\right)-C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a(\widetilde{v})-1)})}\left(\frac{1}{p^{m}}\cdot\nabla(p^{m-a(\widetilde{v})}\cdot\widetilde{u}^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=u-C_{(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a(\widetilde{v})-1)})}\left(\frac{1}{p^{a(\widetilde{v})}}\cdot\nabla(\widetilde{u}^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=u-\nabla_{\mathscr{F}_{a(\widetilde{v})}}(u^{\prime})

\displaystyle=u-(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{[a(\widetilde{v})]}(u^{\prime})

\displaystyle=u-u

\displaystyle=0.

This implies
a(\widetilde{v})>a(\widetilde{v}^{\prime})
.
By repeating the procedure for constructing
\widetilde{v}^{\prime}
from
\widetilde{v}
just discussed, we can find a lifting
\widetilde{v}^{\circledcirc}\in\mathcal{F}
of
v
with
a(\widetilde{v}^{\circledcirc})=-1
, i.e.,
\nabla(\widetilde{v}^{\circledcirc})=0
.
This proves the property
(\beta)_{m}
, thus completing the proof of assertion (i).

Moreover, we again use induction on
m
to prove assertion (ii).
Since the base step is trivial, it suffices to consider
the induction step.
Suppose that the assertion with
m
replaced by
m-1
(
m\geq 1
) has been proved.
Now, let

(\mathscr{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ,0}^{(m)})
and
(\mathscr{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet,0}^{(m)})

(where
\mathscr{F}_{\circ}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ})
and
\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet})
)
be elements of
\mathrm{Diag}_{m}
with
\mathscr{F}_{\circ}=\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
the equality
\nabla_{\circ,0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)}=\nabla_{\bullet,0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)}
holds via the equality
\mathcal{F}_{\circ,0}=\mathcal{F}_{\bullet,0}
.
This implies
\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}_{\circ}}=\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}}
.
Under this equality,
the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
\nabla_{\mathscr{F}_{\circ}}
coincides with
\nabla_{\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}}
because of the assumption
\mathscr{F}_{\circ}=\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}
.
Hence, it follows from Lemma
2.1.2
proved below that
\nabla_{\circ,0}^{(m)}=\nabla_{\bullet,0}^{(m)}
.
This completes the proof of this proposition.
∎

The following lemma was applied in the proof of the above proposition.

Lemma 2.1.2
.

Suppose that

S^{\mathrm{log}}
is an fs log scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and that

X^{\mathrm{log}}
is a log curve over
S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Let
m
be a positive integer, and
let
(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ}^{(m)})
,

(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet}^{(m)})
be
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{0}
-modules.
We shall assume the following conditions:

(a)

The equality
(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})=(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})
holds;

(b)

The
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
(\nabla_{\circ}^{(m)})^{[m]}
corresponds to
(\nabla_{\bullet}^{(m)})^{[m]}
via the equality
\mathcal{F}_{\circ}^{[m]}=\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{[m]}
resulting from the condition (a);

(c)

\mathcal{F}_{\circ}\left(=\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}\right)
is relatively torsion-free.

Then, we have
\nabla_{\circ}^{(m)}=\nabla_{\bullet}^{(m)}
.

Proof.

We shall set
\mathcal{W}:=\mathrm{Ker}((\nabla_{\circ}^{(m)})^{[m]})=\mathrm{Ker}((\nabla_{\bullet}^{(m)})^{[m]})
.
Denote by
U
the open subscheme of
X_{0}
where the relative characteristic
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is trivial.
The
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-linear inclusion
\left(\mathcal{W}=\right)\mathrm{Ker}((\nabla_{\circ}^{(m)})^{[m]})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\circ}
(resp.,
\left(\mathcal{W}=\right)\mathrm{Ker}((\nabla_{\bullet}^{(m)})^{[m]})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}
) extends to a morphism
of
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules

(140)

\displaystyle(F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{W}),\nabla_{\mathcal{W},\mathrm{can}}^{(m)})\rightarrow(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ}^{(m)})\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ (F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{W}),\nabla_{\mathcal{W},\mathrm{can}}^{(m)})\rightarrow(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet}^{(m)})\right).

By the equivalence of categories (
95
),
this morphism becomes an isomorphism when restricted to
U
.
This means that

\nabla_{\circ}^{(m)}
coincides with
\nabla_{\bullet}^{(m)}
over
U
.
Since
U
is a scheme-theoretically dense in
X_{0}
(cf.
[
FKa
, Lemma 1.4]
) and
\mathcal{F}_{\circ}\left(=\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}\right)
is relatively torsion-free by assumption,
we obtain the equality
\nabla_{\circ}^{(m)}=\nabla_{\bullet}^{(m)}
, as desired.
∎

Corollary 2.1.3
.

Let
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
(where
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
) be an element of
\mathrm{Diag}_{m}
.
Then, each horizontal local section of
\mathcal{F}
specifies a section of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
via reduction modulo
p
.
Moreover, the resulting morphism of sheaves

(141)

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla)_{0}=\right)\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})

is surjective.

Proof.

We shall prove the assertion by induction on
m
.
The base step, i.e., the case of
m=0
, is trivial because

\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
.
To consider the induction step, suppose that we have proved the assertion with
m
replaced by
m-1
(
m\geq 1
).
Let us take an arbitrary local section
v
in
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)
.
By the induction hypothesis, the reduction modulo
p
of
v
, i.e.,
v_{0}\left(=(v_{m-1})_{0}\right)
, belongs to
\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}\left(=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})\right)
.
Since the reduction modulo
p^{m}
of
v
is horizontal with respect to
\nabla_{m-1}
,
it follows from the definition of
\nabla_{\mathscr{F}}
that

\nabla_{\mathscr{F}}(v_{0})=\nabla(v)=0
.
Thus, we have
v_{0}\in\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
.
This completes the proof of the former assertion.
The latter assertion, i.e., the surjectivity of (
141
), follows from the property
(\beta)_{m}
asserted in Proposition
2.1.1
, (i).
∎

Definition 2.1.4
.

(i)

Let
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a flat module on
X^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}/S^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}
.
We shall say that
\mathscr{F}
is
dormant

if there exists a
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module structure

\nabla_{0}^{(m)}
on
\mathcal{F}_{0}

satisfying
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})\in\mathrm{Diag}_{m}
.

(ii)

Let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be a flat
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle (where
n>1
) on
X^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}/S^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}
in the sense of
[
Wak6
, Definition 1.28]
.
We shall say that
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is
dormant
if, after possibly replacing
X
with its étale covering, it may be described as the projectivization of a rank
n
dormant flat bundle on
X^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}/S^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}
.

Remark 2.1.5

(Some properties on dormant flat bundles)
.

Whether a flat module
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
(or a flat
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
) as in the above definition is dormant or not
does not depend on the integer “
l
”.
Also, the property of being dormant is of local nature with respect to the étale topology on
X
.
In the case of
m=0
, a flat module (or a flat
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle) on
X_{0}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{0}^{\mathrm{log}}
is dormant if and only if it has vanishing
p
-curvature.

Remark 2.1.6

(Well-definedness of
(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}},\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})
)
.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a flat module on
X_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}
whose reduction modulo
p^{m}
is dormant.
Then, by Proposition
2.1.1
, (ii),
the flat module
(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}},\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})
is well-defined in the sense that it does not depend on the choice of a
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla_{0}^{(m)}
with
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})\in\mathrm{Diag}_{m}
(because such a choice
\nabla_{0}^{(m)}
is, if it exists, uniquely determined).

2.2.
Diagonal reductions/liftings

Let
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a dormant flat module on
X^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}/S^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}
.
According to Proposition
2.1.1
, (ii),
there exists a unique
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module structure

(142)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\nabla:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F}_{0})

on
\mathcal{F}_{0}
with
(\mathscr{F},{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\nabla)\in\mathrm{Diag}_{m}
.
In particular, we obtain
a
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module

(143)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F}_{0},{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\nabla),

which has
vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature (cf. Corollary
1.6.4
).

Also, let
\mathscr{E}:=(\mathcal{E},\phi)
be a flat
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle on
X_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Then, by choosing étale locally on
X
a dormant flat bundle
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
inducing
\mathscr{E}
and successively taking the projectivization of
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\nabla
, we obtain a well-defined
m
-PD stratification

(144)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\phi:=\{{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\phi_{l}:P_{(m)}^{l}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}\times_{X}P_{(m)}^{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}

on
\mathcal{E}_{0}
.
We shall set

(145)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}:=(\mathcal{E}_{0},{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\phi),

which forms an
m
-PD stratified
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle on
X^{\mathrm{log}}_{0}/S_{0}^{\mathrm{log}}
.

Definition 2.2.1
.

(i)

With the above notation,
we shall refer to
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}
(resp.,
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}
) as the
diagonal reduction
of
\mathscr{F}
(resp.,
\mathscr{E}
).

(ii)

Given a
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module (resp., an
m
-PD stratified
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle)
\mathscr{G}
,
we shall refer to any dormant flat module
\mathscr{F}
(resp., any dormant flat
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle
\mathscr{E}
) on
X_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
satisfying
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{G}
(resp.,
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{G}
) as
a
diagonal lifting
of
\mathscr{G}
.

Remark 2.2.2

(Compatibility with level reduction)
.

If
m^{\prime}
is a nonnegative integer
\leq m
, then
the reduction
\mathscr{F}_{m^{\prime}}
(resp.,
\mathscr{E}_{m^{\prime}}
) modulo
p^{m^{\prime}+1}
of
\mathscr{F}
(resp.,
\mathscr{E}
) is dormant
and
the diagonal reduction

{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(\mathscr{F}_{m^{\prime}})
(resp.,

{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(\mathscr{E}_{m^{\prime}})
) is obtained by reducing the level of
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}
(resp.,
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}
) to
m^{\prime}
.

Example 2.2.3

(Trivial flat bundle)
.

One may verify that the diagonal reduction of the trivial flat bundle
(\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}},\nabla^{(0)}_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}})
(cf. (
41
)) is the trivial
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}},\nabla^{(m)}_{X_{0},\mathrm{triv}})
, i.e.,

(146)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}},\nabla^{(0)}_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}})=(\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}},\nabla^{(m)}_{X_{0},\mathrm{triv}}).

Proposition 2.2.4
.

Suppose that the relative characteristic
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is trivial.

(i)

Let
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a flat module on
X_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
whose reduction modulo
p^{m}
(i.e.,
\mathscr{F}_{m-1}
) is dormant.
Then,

\mathscr{F}
is dormant if and only if
\psi(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})=0
(cf. Remark
2.1.6
).

(ii)

Let
n
be a positive integer and
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
a rank
n
flat bundle on
X_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Note that, since
\nabla
is
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})
-linear,
the sheaf of horizontal sections
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla)
forms an
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})
-submodule of
\mathcal{F}
.
Then, the following three conditions are equivalent to each other:

(a)

\mathscr{F}
is dormant;

(b)

\mathscr{F}
is locally trivial, i.e., isomorphic, Zariski locally on
X
, to
(\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}},\nabla_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})^{\oplus n}
;

(c)

The
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})
-module
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla)
is locally free of rank
n
.

Proof.

First, we shall prove assertion (i).
Since the “only if” part of the required equivalence is immediate from Proposition
1.6.1
, we only consider the “if” part.
Suppose that
\psi(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})=0
.
Then,
the morphism

(147)

\displaystyle F_{X_{0}^{(m)}/S_{0}}^{*}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}}))\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}

induced naturally by the inclusion
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}
is an isomorphism (cf.
[
Kat1
, Theorem (5.1)]
).
On the other hand,
if we set
(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{0}^{(m-1)}):={{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}_{m-1}
,
then it follows from
the equivalence of categories (
95
)
that
the morphism

(148)

\displaystyle\left(F_{X_{0}/S_{0}}^{(m)*}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m-1)}))=\right)F_{X_{0}/S_{0}}^{(m)*}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{0}

induced by
the inclusion
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m-1)})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{0}
is an isomorphism.
Hence, we obtain the composite isomorphism

(149)

\displaystyle F^{(m+1)*}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}}))\left(=F^{(m)*}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}(F^{*}_{X_{0}^{(m)}/S_{0}}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})))\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{(m)*}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}})\xrightarrow{(\ref{YY1})}\mathcal{F}_{0},

where the first arrow denotes the pull-back of (
147
) by
F_{X_{0}/S_{0}}^{(m)}
.
Denote by
\nabla_{0}^{(m)}
the
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}_{0}
corresponding to
\nabla_{\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\mathscr{F}}),\mathrm{can}}^{(m)}
(cf. Definition
1.5.4
) via this isomorphism.
One may verify that
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
belongs to
\mathrm{Diag}_{m}
, meaning that
\mathscr{F}
is dormant.
This proves the “if” part of the required equivalence.

Next, we shall consider assertion (ii).
The implication (b)
\Rightarrow
(c) is clear.
Also, since the property of being dormant is closed under taking direct sums,
the
implication (b)
\Rightarrow
(a)
follows from the fact that the trivial flat bundle is dormant (cf. Example
2.2.3
).

In what follows, we shall prove the implication (a)
\Rightarrow
(b).
Suppose that
\mathscr{F}
is dormant, i.e.,
there exists a
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla_{0}^{(m)}
on
\mathcal{F}_{0}
with
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})\in\mathrm{Diag}_{m}
.
By the equivalence of categories (
95
),
the morphism
F^{(m+1)*}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)}))\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{0}

extending the inclusion
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{0}
is an isomorphism.
Hence, the faithful flatness of
F^{(m+1)}_{X/S}
(cf.
[
FKa
, Theorem 1.1]
)
implies that

\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
forms a rank
n
vector bundle on
X^{(m+1)}
.
By this fact and the property
(\beta)_{m}
for
(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
(cf. Proposition
2.1.1
, (i)),
we can find, for each point
q
of
X_{m}
, a collection of data

(150)

\displaystyle(U;v_{1},\cdots,v_{n}),

where
U
denotes an open neighborhood of
q
in
X_{m}
and

v_{1},\cdots,v_{n}
are sections of
\mathcal{F}
defined on
U
which are horizontal with respect to
\nabla
and whose reductions
v_{1,0},\cdots,v_{n,0}
modulo
p
form a local basis of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
.
The morphism

\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\oplus n}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}|_{U}

given by
(a_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot v_{i}
(for any
a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{O}_{U}
)
is compatible with the respective

S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connections
(\nabla_{U,\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})^{\oplus n}
and
\nabla
.
The resulting morphism of
flat modules

(151)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{O}_{U},\nabla_{U,\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})^{\oplus n}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}|_{U}

is an isomorphism by Nakayama’s lemma.
In particular,
\mathscr{F}
is locally trivial, and
this completes the proof of (a)
\Rightarrow
(b).

Finally, we shall prove (c)
\Rightarrow
(b).
To this end, we may assume that there exists an
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})
-linear isomorphism
h^{\nabla}:\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})^{\oplus n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla)
.
For each
j=1,\cdots,n
, denote by
e_{j}
the image of
1
via the inclusion into the
j
-th factor
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})^{\oplus n}
.
Then, we obtain the morphism of flat bundles

(152)

\displaystyle h:(\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}},\nabla_{X_{m},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})^{\oplus n}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}

given by
(a_{j})_{j=1}^{n}\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{j}\cdot h^{\nabla}(e_{j})
.
The reduction of
h
modulo
p
determines a morphism

(153)

\displaystyle h_{0}:(\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}},\nabla_{X_{0},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})^{\oplus n}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{0}.

Since
\mathscr{F}_{0}
has vanishing
p
-curvature, it follows from (the proof of)
[
Wak6
, Proposition 4.60]
that
h_{0}
is an isomorphism.
Hence, by Nakayama’s lemma and a routine argument,
h
turns out to be an isomorphism.
This proves (c)
\Rightarrow
(b).
∎

Corollary 2.2.5
.

Suppose that the relative characteristic
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is trivial.
Let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be a flat
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle on
X^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}/S^{\mathrm{log}}_{m}
.
Then,
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is dormant if and only if it is, étale locally on
X
, isomorphic to the trivial flat
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle (cf.
[
Wak6
, § 1.3.2]
or Example
1.3.2
).

Proof.

The assertion follows from the equivalence (a)
\Leftrightarrow
(b) obtained in Proposition
2.2.4
, (ii).
∎

Let
h:\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{G}
be a morphism between dormant flat modules on
X_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
, and write
(\mathcal{F}_{0},\nabla_{\mathscr{F},0}):={{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}
and
(\mathcal{G}_{0},\nabla_{\mathscr{G},0}):={{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{G}
.
By the functoriality of Cartier operator (cf. Remark
1.6.6
),
we see that
h
induces a morphism of flat bundles
h_{a}:(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}_{a}},\nabla_{\mathscr{F}_{a}})\rightarrow(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{G}_{a}},\nabla_{\mathscr{G}_{a}})
for every
a=0,\cdots,m
.
It follows from the property
(\gamma_{m})
asserted in Proposition
2.1.1
, (i), that
h_{a}
may be regarded as a morphism

(\mathcal{F}_{0}^{[a]},\nabla_{\mathscr{F},0}^{[a]})\rightarrow(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{[a]},\nabla_{\mathscr{G},0}^{[a]})
.
Since the
h_{a}
’s are compatible with each other via restriction,
the morphism
h_{0}:\mathcal{F}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}
preserves the
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module structure.
In other words, it determines a morphism of
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-modules

(154)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!h:{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}\rightarrow{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{G}.

The assignments
\mathscr{F}\mapsto{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}
and
h\mapsto{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!h
define a functor

(155)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-):\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the category of dormant}\\
\text{flat modules
on $X_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}$}\end{matrix}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the category of dormant}\\
\text{ (i.e., $p^{m+1}$-flat) $\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}$-modules}\end{matrix}\right).

This functor commutes with the formations of direct sums, tensor products, and determinants (in the case where the underlying
\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}}
-modules of dormant flat modules are locally free of finite rank).

2.3.
Change of structure group

We shall prove the following two assertions, which are generalizations of
[
Wak4
, Lemma 3.6.3]
and
[
Wak6
, Proposition 4.22]
.

Proposition 2.3.1
.

Let
n
be a positive integer with

p\nmid n
and

\mathcal{L}
a line bundle on
X
.
Suppose that
either
l=0
or
m=0
is satisfied.
Also, suppose that we are given
a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}}

on the
n
-fold tensor product
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}
of
\mathcal{L}
.

(i)

There exists a unique

\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}

on
\mathcal{L}
satisfying
\nabla^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{L}}=\nabla_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}}
(cf.
[
Mon
, Corollaire 2.6.1]
for the definition of the
n
-th fold tensor product of a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure).

(ii)

If, moreover,
(\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}})

is dormant, then
the resulting
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})
is dormant.

Proof.

First, we shall consider assertion (i).
Denote by

\phi_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}}
the
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}
corresponding to
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}}
(cf. Remark
1.3.3
).
Let us take an open covering
\mathscr{U}:=\{\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
of
X
such that, for each
\alpha\in I
, there exists a trivialization
\tau_{\alpha}:\mathcal{L}|_{U_{\alpha}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{U_{\alpha}}
.
For each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,

\tau_{\alpha}
induces isomorphisms

(156)

\displaystyle{{}^{L}}\tau_{\alpha,l,d}:\mathcal{P}^{l}_{(m)}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\otimes d}|_{U_{\alpha}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{P^{l}_{(m)}}|_{U_{\alpha}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ {{}^{R}}\tau_{\alpha,l,d}:\mathcal{L}^{\otimes d}|_{U_{\alpha}}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{l}_{(m)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{P^{l}_{(m)}}|_{U_{\alpha}}.

The isomorphisms
{{}^{L}}\tau_{\alpha,l,n}
and
{{}^{R}}\tau_{\alpha,l,n}
allow us to regard

\phi_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes}}|_{U_{\alpha}}
as an automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{P^{l}_{(m)}}|_{U_{\alpha}}
; it may be described as the multiplication by some element
\eta_{\alpha,l}
of
\mathcal{O}_{P^{l}_{(m)}|_{U_{\alpha}}}^{\times}
.
Since
\eta_{\alpha,0}=1
and
p\nmid n
,
we can find a unique compatible collection
\{\lambda_{\alpha,l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
, where each
\lambda_{\alpha,l}
is an element of
\mathcal{O}_{P^{l}_{(m)}|_{U_{\alpha}}}^{\times}
with
\lambda_{\alpha,l}^{\otimes n}=\eta_{\alpha,l}
.
By
{{}^{L}}\tau_{\alpha,l,1}
and
{{}^{R}}\tau_{\alpha,l,1}
,
the automorphisms given by
multiplication by
\lambda_{\alpha,l}
for various
l
’s together
determine an
m
-PD stratification
\phi_{\mathcal{L},\alpha}
on
\mathcal{L}|_{U_{\alpha}}
.
The uniqueness of the collection
\{\lambda_{\alpha,l}\}_{l}
implies
\phi_{\mathcal{L},\alpha}|_{U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}}=\phi_{\mathcal{L},\beta}|_{U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}}
for any pair
(\alpha,\beta)\in I\times I
with
U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
.
Hence,
\phi_{\mathcal{L},\alpha}
may be glued together to obtain an
m
-PD stratification
\phi_{\mathcal{L}}
on
\mathcal{L}
.
Let
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}
denote the
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{L}
corresponding to
\phi_{\mathcal{L}}
.
Then, it follows from the definitions of
\phi_{\mathcal{L},\alpha}
’s that
the equality
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes n}=\nabla_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}}
holds, and that an
m
-PD stratification satisfying this equality is uniquely determined.
This completes the proof of assertion (i).

Next, we shall consider assertion (ii).
To consider the case of
l=0
, let
U
denote the open subscheme of
X
where the relative characteristic
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}

is trivial.
Since
U
is scheme-theoretically dense (cf.
[
FKa
, Lemma 1.4]
),
the problem is reduced, after restriction to
U
, to the case where
S^{\mathrm{log}}=S
and
X^{\mathrm{log}}=X
.
Hence, the assertion follows from
[
Wak4
, Lemma 3.6.3]
.

Finally, let us prove the case of
m=0
by induction on
l
.
The base step, i.e., the case of
l=0
, was already proved.
To consider the induction step, we suppose that the assertion with
l
replaced by
l-1
(
l\geq 1
) has been proved.
Denote by
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n},0}^{(l)}
the
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(l)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{L}_{0}^{\otimes n}
obtained as the diagonal reduction of

(\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}})
.
By applying assertion (i) to
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n},0}^{(l)}
,
we obtain uniquely a
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(l)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)}
on
\mathcal{L}_{0}
with
(\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)})^{\otimes n}=\nabla_{\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n},0}^{(l)}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\nabla_{\mathcal{L},l-1}
is dormant and the uniqueness of
\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)}
implies
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{L}_{l-1}=(\mathcal{L}_{0},\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)\Rightarrow(l-1)})
, where
\mathscr{L}:=(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})
.
If
U
is as above, then
the restriction
\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{L}}|_{U^{(l)}}
of
\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{L}}
is a line bundle on
U^{(l)}
.
The diagonal reduction of
\mathscr{L}^{\otimes n}
is
(\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)})^{\otimes n}
, so
the uniqueness portion of assertion (i) (together with the equivalence of categories (
95
))
implies that

\nabla_{\mathscr{L}}
and
(\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)})^{[l]}
are equal when restricted to

U^{(l)}
.
But, since
U^{(l)}
is scheme-theoretically dense in
X^{(l)}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{L}}
is relatively torsion-free (cf. Proposition
1.6.2
, (i)), the equality
\nabla_{\mathscr{L}}=(\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)})^{[l]}
holds.
This implies
(\mathscr{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})\in\mathrm{Diag}_{l}
, and hence
completes the proof of assertion (ii).
∎

Proposition 2.3.2
.

Let
n
be a positive integer with
p\nmid n
and

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}}
a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-bundle on
X
.
Suppose that
either
l=0
or
m=0
is satisfied.
Also, suppose that we are given a pair
(\phi_{\mathrm{PGL}},\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})
, where

•

\phi_{\mathrm{PGL}}
denotes an
m
-PD stratification on the
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{PGL}}:=\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}}\times^{\mathrm{GL}_{n}}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
induced from
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}}
via the natural quotient
\mu_{\mathrm{PGL}}:\mathrm{GL}_{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
;

•

\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
denotes an
m
-PD stratification on the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}:=\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}}\times^{\mathrm{GL}_{n}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
induced from
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}}
via the determinant map
\mu_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}:\mathrm{GL}_{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Then, the following assertions hold:

(i)

There exists a unique
m
-PD stratification
\phi_{\mathrm{GL}}
on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}}
satisfying the following equalities:

(157)

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathrm{GL}}\times^{\mathrm{GL}_{n},\mu_{\mathrm{PGL}}}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}=\phi_{\mathrm{PGL}},\hskip 28.45274pt\phi_{\mathrm{GL}}\times^{\mathrm{GL}_{n},\mu_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}\mathbb{G}_{m}=\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}

(cf. (
79
) for the change of structure group of an
m
-PD stratification).

(ii)

If, moreover, both
\phi_{\mathrm{PGL}}
and
\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
are dormant, then
the resulting pair
(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}},\phi_{\mathrm{GL}})

(or equivalently, the corresponding
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module)
is dormant.

Proof.

First, we shall consider assertion (i).
Let us take an open covering
\mathscr{U}:=\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
of
X
such that, for each
\alpha\in I
, there exists an
m
-PD stratification
\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}
on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}}|_{U_{\alpha}}
with
\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}\times^{\mathrm{GL}_{n}}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}=\phi_{\mathrm{PGL}}|_{U_{\alpha}}
.
By Proposition
2.3.1
, (i),
there exists an
m
-PD stratification
\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m},\alpha}
on the trivial
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle on
U_{\alpha}
such that
\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m},\alpha}^{\otimes n}\otimes(\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}\times^{\mathrm{GL}_{n}}\mathbb{G}_{m})=\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}|_{U_{\alpha}}
, where
the tensor product “
\otimes
” is defined
in a natural manner.
Write
\phi_{\alpha}:=\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}\otimes\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m},\alpha}
.
Then, it is verified that

\phi_{\alpha}\times^{\mathrm{GL}_{n}}\mathbb{G}_{m}=\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}|_{U_{\alpha}}
and

\phi_{\alpha}\times^{\mathrm{GL}_{n}}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}=\phi_{\mathrm{PGL}}|_{\alpha}
.
By the uniqueness assertion of Proposition
2.3.1
, (i),
an
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}}|_{U_{\alpha}}
satisfying these properties is uniquely determined.
In particular,
for any pair
(\alpha,\beta)\in I\times I
with
U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
,
we have
\phi_{\alpha}|_{U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}}=\phi_{\beta}|_{U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}}
.
This implies that

\{\phi_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
may be glued together to obtain an
m
-PD stratification
\phi_{\mathrm{GL}}
on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}}
satisfying (
157
).
This completes the proof of assertion (i).

Next, we shall prove assertion (ii) under the assumption that
l=0
.
Similarly to the proof of Proposition
2.3.1
, (ii),
it suffices to consider the case where
S^{\mathrm{log}}=S
and
X^{\mathrm{log}}=X
.
Denote by

\mathcal{Q}
(resp.,
\mathcal{N}
) the
\mathbb{P}^{n}
-bundle (resp., the line bundle)
on
X^{(m+1)}
corresponding to
(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{PGL}},\phi_{\mathrm{PGL}})
(resp.,
(\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}},\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})
) via the equivalence of categories asserted in

[
Wak5
, Proposition 7.7.3]
(cf. Remark
1.5.3
).
Since
p\nmid n
, there exists,
after possibly replacing
S
with its étale covering, a pair
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{M})
, where

•

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
denotes a rank
n
vector bundle on
X^{(m+1)}
whose projectivization is
\mathcal{Q}
;

•

\mathcal{M}
denote a line bundle on
X^{(m+1)}
with
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{N}
.

Write
\mathcal{V}:=\mathcal{M}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
.
Then, it is verified that
\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{V})\cong\mathcal{N}
and that the projectivization of
\mathcal{V}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{Q}
.
The vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
corresponds to
a
p^{m+1}
-flat
m
-PD stratified
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-bundle
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{GL}},\phi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{GL}})
via the equivalence of categories in
[
Wak5
, Proposition 7.7.3]
.
This equivalence of categories also implies
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{GL}},\phi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{GL}})\cong(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}},\phi_{\mathrm{GL}})
, so
\phi_{\mathrm{GL}}
is
p^{m+1}
-flat.

Finally, we shall prove assertion (ii) under the assumption that
m=0
.
The problem is of local nature with respect to the étale topology on
X
,
we may assume that there exists a rank
n
dormant flat bundle
(\mathcal{F},\nabla_{\mathcal{F}})
on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
whose projectivization corresponds to
\phi_{\mathrm{PGL}}
.
Denote by
(\mathcal{V},\nabla_{\mathcal{V}})
the flat bundle corresponding to
(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{GL}},\phi_{\mathrm{GL}})
.
Then, we obtain an isomorphism
h:\mathcal{V}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{F}
for some line bundle
\mathcal{L}
.
It follows from Proposition
2.3.1
, (i), that there exists
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}
on
\mathcal{L}
such that
the determinant of
h
defines an isomorphism of flat line bundles

(158)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{V}),\mathrm{det}(\nabla_{\mathcal{V}}))\cong(\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}),\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}).

But, since both
(\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{V}),\mathrm{det}(\nabla_{\mathcal{V}}))
(which corresponds to
(\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}},\phi_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})
)
and
(\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{det}(\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}))
are dormant,
Proposition
2.3.1
, (ii), implies that
(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})
is dormant.
By the uniqueness portion of assertion (i),

h
defines an isomorphism
(\mathcal{V},\nabla_{\mathcal{V}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{F},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\nabla_{\mathcal{F}})
.
In particular,
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
turns out to be dormant.
This completes the proof of assertion (ii).
∎

2.4.
Deformation space of a dormant flat bundle

Next, suppose that
X
is a geometrically connected, proper, and smooth curve over
S:=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
for
a flat
\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
-algebra
R
.
Let us fix an element
(\mathscr{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)})
(where
\mathscr{L}:=(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})
) of
\mathrm{Diag}_{l}
such that
\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle on
X
.

Let
m
be an integer with
0\leq m<l
, and let

(\mathscr{F},\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
(where
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
) be an element of
\mathrm{Diag}_{m}
such that
\mathcal{F}
is a vector bundle on
X
and
an identification
(\mathrm{det}(\mathscr{F}),\mathrm{det}(\nabla))=(\mathscr{L}_{m},\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)\Rightarrow(m)})
are fixed.
We shall write

(159)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Lift}(\nabla^{(m)}_{0})_{\mathscr{L}}\right)

for the set of
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m+1)}
-module structures
\nabla^{(m+1)}_{0}
on
\mathcal{F}_{0}
with
\nabla_{0}^{(m+1)\Rightarrow(m)}=\nabla_{0}^{(m)}

(resp.,
\nabla_{0}^{(m+1)\Rightarrow(m)}=\nabla^{(m)}_{0}
and
\mathrm{det}(\nabla^{(m+1)}_{0})=\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)\Rightarrow(m+1)}
).
According to
[
PBer2
, Théorème 2.3.6]
,
giving a
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m+1)}
-module structure classified by this set is equivalent to giving an
S
-connection on the
\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}^{(m)}}
-module
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})

(resp., an
S
-connection on the
\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}^{(m+1)}}
-module
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
whose determinant coincides with
\mathrm{det}((\nabla_{\mathcal{L},0}^{(l)})^{[m+1]}
).
Hence, the set
\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})_{\mathscr{L}}
) is, if it is nonempty, equipped with a canonical structure of torsor modeled on the
R
-module

(160)

\displaystyle H^{0}(X_{0}^{(m+1)},\Omega^{(m+1)}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})))\ \ \ \

\displaystyle\left(\text{resp.,}\ H^{0}(X_{0}^{(m+1)},\Omega_{0}^{(m+1)}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})))\right),

where
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})))
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})))
) denotes the sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}^{(m+1)}}
-linear endomorphisms of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)}))
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}^{(m+1)}}
-linear endomorphisms of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
with vanishing trace).
This torsor structure is defined in such a way that
if
\nabla^{\prime}
is the
S
-connection corresponding to an element of
\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})_{\mathscr{L}}
) and
A
is an element of the
R
-module displayed in (
160
),
then the result of the action by
A
on
\nabla^{\prime}
is the
S
-connection
\nabla^{\prime}+A
.

Proposition 2.4.1
.

The natural inclusion
\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})_{\mathscr{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
is compatible with the respective torsor structures via
the injection

(161)

\displaystyle H^{0}(X_{0}^{(m+1)},\Omega_{0}^{(m+1)}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})))\hookrightarrow H^{0}(X_{0}^{(m+1)},\Omega_{0}^{(m+1)}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})))

induced by the inclusion
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)}))\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{(m)}_{0}))
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the definitions of
\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
and
\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})_{\mathscr{L}}
.
∎

We shall denote by

(162)

\displaystyle\nabla^{(m)}_{0,\mathcal{E}nd}\ \left(\text{or}\ (\nabla^{(m)}_{0})_{\mathcal{E}nd}\right):{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S_{0}}}(\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}_{0}))

the

\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module structure on

\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}_{0})\left(:=\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}}}(\mathcal{F}_{0})\right)
induced naturally by
\nabla_{0}^{(m)}
.
It restricts to
a
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module structure

(163)

\displaystyle\nabla^{(m)}_{0,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}\ \left(\text{or}\ (\nabla^{(m)}_{0})_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}\right):{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{0}))

on the subsheaf
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{0})
consisting of
\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}}
-linear endomorphisms with vanishing trace.
The
S
-connection
\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}
(resp.,
\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}
) may be regarded as
a complex of sheaves
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}]
(resp.,
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}]
)
concentrated at degrees
0
and
1
.

Next, we shall write

(164)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}\right)

for the set of isomorphism classes of flat bundles
\mathscr{G}
on
X_{m+1}/S_{m+1}
with
\mathscr{G}_{m}=\mathscr{F}
(resp., flat bundles
\mathscr{G}
on
X_{m+1}/S_{m+1}
with
\mathscr{G}_{m}=\mathscr{F}
together with an identification
\mathrm{det}(\mathscr{G})=\mathscr{L}_{m+1}
compatible with the fixed identification
\mathrm{det}(\mathscr{F})=\mathscr{L}_{m}
).
It follows from well-known generalities of deformation theory that
the set
\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}
) is, if it is nonempty, equipped with a structure of torsor modeled on

\mathbb{H}^{1}(X_{0},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}])

(resp.,
\mathbb{H}^{1}(X_{0},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}^{(m)\Rightarrow{(0)}}])
) in the manner of
[
Wak6
, § 6.1.4]
.

Proposition 2.4.2
.

The natural inclusion
\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})
is compatible with the respective torsor structures via the morphism

(165)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{1}(X_{0},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}])\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}(X_{0},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}])

induced by the inclusion
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}]
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the definitions of
\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})
and
\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}
.
∎

Since the equivalence of categories (
95
) commutes with the formation of internal Homs,
there exists a natural identification

(166)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)}))=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd}^{(m)})\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)}))=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}^{(m)})\right).

Under this identification, the Cartier operator
associated to

(\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}_{0}),\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd}^{(m)})
(resp.,
(\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{0}),\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}^{(m)})
) yields a morphism of complex

(167)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}]\rightarrow(\Omega_{0}^{(m+1)}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})))[-1]

\displaystyle\left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}]\rightarrow(\Omega_{0}^{(m+1)}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})))[-1]\right),

where for each sheaf
\mathcal{G}
we define the complex
\mathcal{G}[-1]
to be
\mathcal{G}
considered as a complex concentrated at degree
1
.
By applying the
1
-st hypercohomology functor to this morphism, we obtain an
R
-linear morphism

(168)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{1}(X_{0},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}])\rightarrow H^{0}(X_{0}^{(m+1)},\Omega_{0}^{(m+1)}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})))\ \ \ \ \ \,

\displaystyle\left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathbb{H}^{1}(X_{0},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{0,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}])\rightarrow H^{0}(X_{0}^{(m+1)},\Omega_{0}^{(m+1)}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})))\right).

Proposition 2.4.3
.

The assignment
\mathscr{G}\mapsto{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{G}
for each
\mathscr{G}\in\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})
(resp.,
\mathscr{G}\in\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}
)
defines
a map of sets

(169)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})\rightarrow\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})_{\mathscr{L}}\right).

Moreover, this map is compatible with
the respective torsor structures via the morphism (
168
).

Proof.

The assertion follows immediately from
the fact mentioned in Remark
2.2.2
and the definition of the functor
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-)
.
∎

Finally, we shall denote by

(170)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{\psi}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{\psi}_{\mathscr{L}}\right)

the subset of
\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})_{\mathscr{L}}
) consisting of
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{(m)}
-module structures with vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature.
Also,
denote by

(171)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})^{\psi}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}^{\psi}\right)

the subset of
\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}
)
consisting of dormant flat bundles.
The following assertion is immediately verified.

Proposition 2.4.4
.

The assignment
\mathscr{G}\mapsto{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{G}
for each
\mathscr{G}\in\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})^{\psi}
(resp.,
\mathscr{G}\in\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}^{\psi}
)
yields a map of sets

(172)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})^{\psi}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{\psi}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})^{\psi}_{\mathscr{L}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})^{\psi}_{\mathscr{L}}\right)

Moreover, the following commutative square diagram is Cartesian:

\displaystyle\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 36.75154pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-21.03479pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern \vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 36.75154pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-21.03479pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.89543pt\raise-35.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 57.20842pt\raise-45.32336pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 57.20842pt\raise-45.32336pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{(m)})_{\mathscr{L}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\right).\hskip-8.53581pt

Proof.

The assertion follows from Propositions
1.6.1
, (i),
2.2.4
, (i), and Corollary
1.6.4
.
∎

3.
Local study of
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules

In this section, we study the local description of
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules in characteristic
p
around a marked/nodal point of the underlying pointed stable curve.
By applying the resulting description, we show that
each dormant flat bundle in characteristic
p^{m+1}
can be decomposed into the direct sum of rank
1
flat bundles of a certain type (cf. Proposition-Definition
3.5.4
).

Throughout this section, we fix a scheme
S
.

3.1.
Formal neighborhoods of a marked/nodal point

We shall set

(181)

\displaystyle U_{\oslash}:=\mathcal{S}pec(\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]),\hskip 14.22636ptU_{\otimes}:=\mathcal{S}pec(\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t_{1},t_{2}]\!]/(t_{1}t_{2})),

where
t
,
t_{1}
, and
t_{2}
are formal parameters.
For simplicity, we write
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}:=\mathcal{O}_{U_{\oslash}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}:=\mathcal{O}_{U_{\otimes}}
.

Hereinafter, let us fix a pair of nonnegative integers
(l,m)
with
l=0
or
m=0
, and suppose that
S
is a flat scheme over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
Denote by
S^{\mathrm{log}}
the log scheme defined as
S
equipped with the trivial log structure.
Also,
we equip
U_{\oslash}
with the log structure associated to the monoid morphism
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
given by
n\mapsto t^{n}
; if
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}
denotes the resulting log scheme, then
we obtain the sheaf of noncommutative rings on
U_{\oslash}
defined as

(182)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}:=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\mathcal{D}_{U_{\oslash,n}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)},

where
U_{\oslash,n}^{\mathrm{log}}
(
n\geq 1
) denotes the strict closed subscheme of
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}
defined by the ideal sheaf
(t^{n})\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]
.
This sheaf has two
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
-module structures
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
and
{{}^{R}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
arising from those of
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{U_{\oslash,n}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)}
’s and
{{}^{R}}\mathcal{D}_{U_{\oslash,n}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)}
’s, respectively.
The
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
-module
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
can be decomposed as the direct sum

\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\cdot\partial^{\langle j\rangle}
, where

\partial^{\langle j\rangle}
’s
denote the sections associated to the logarithmic coordinate
t
as defined in §
1.2
.
In particular, the multiplication in
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
is given by (
32
).

On the other hand, we equip
U_{\otimes}
(resp.,
S
) with a log structure associated to the monoid morphism
\mathbb{N}\oplus\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
(resp.,
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{S}
) given by
(n_{1},n_{2})\mapsto t^{n_{1}}\cdot t^{n_{2}}
(resp.,
n\mapsto 0^{n}
); denote by
U_{\otimes}^{\mathrm{log}}
(resp.,
S^{\mathrm{log}}
) the resulting log scheme.
By the diagonal embedding
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}\oplus\mathbb{N}
,
the projection
U_{\otimes}\rightarrow S
extends to a morphism of log schemes
U_{\otimes}^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
We obtain the sheaf on
U_{\otimes}
defined as

(183)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\otimes}:=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\mathcal{D}_{U_{\otimes,n}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)},

where
U_{\otimes,n}^{\mathrm{log}}
(
n\geq 1
) denotes the strict closed subscheme of
U_{\otimes}^{\mathrm{log}}
defined by the ideal sheaf
(t_{1},t_{2})^{n}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t_{1},t_{2}]\!]/(t_{1}t_{2})
.
Just as in the case of
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\oslash}
, this sheaf has two
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
-module structures
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\otimes}
and
{{}^{R}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\otimes}
.

For each
i=1,2
, denote by
\{\partial_{i}^{\langle j\rangle}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
the basis of
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\otimes}
associated to the logarithmic coordinate
t_{i}
.
In particular, we have
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\otimes}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}\cdot\partial_{i}^{\langle j\rangle}
.

Lemma 3.1.1
.

For each positive integer
j\leq p^{m}
, the following equality holds:

(184)

\displaystyle\partial_{2}^{\langle j\rangle}=(-1)^{j}\cdot\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{j}\binom{j-1}{j^{\prime}-1}\cdot\partial_{1}^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}.

In particular, for each nonnegative integer
a\leq m
,
the following equalities hold:

(185)

\displaystyle\partial_{2}^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}=\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{p^{a}}(-1)^{j^{\prime}}\cdot\partial_{1}^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}=-\partial_{1}^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}-1+\prod_{b=0}^{a-1}(1-(\partial_{1}^{\langle p^{b}\rangle})^{p-1}),

where we set
\prod_{b=0}^{-1}(-):=1
.

Proof.

Note that the morphism
\mu_{(m)}
and the sheaves
\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}
(
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) discussed in §
1.2
can be defined
even in the case where
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is replaced by
U_{\otimes}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
In particular, we obtain the sections

\eta_{i}:=\mu_{(m)}(t_{i})-1
(
i=1,2
) of
\mathcal{P}_{(m)}^{l}
.
Since
\mu_{(m)}
preserves the monoid structure, we have

(186)

\displaystyle(1+\eta_{1})(1+\eta_{2})=\mu_{(m)}(t_{1})\cdot\mu_{(m)}(t_{2})=\mu_{(m)}(t_{1}\cdot t_{2})=\mu_{(m)}(0)=1.

This implies

\displaystyle\eta_{1}=-1+(1+\eta_{2})^{-1}=-\eta_{2}+\eta_{2}^{2}-\eta_{2}^{3}-\eta_{2}^{4}+\cdots\left(=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(-\eta_{2})^{j}\right).

Hence, for each
j^{\prime}\leq p^{m}
,
the following equalities hold:

(187)

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{\{j^{\prime}\}}=\eta_{1}^{j^{\prime}}=(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(-\eta_{2})^{j})^{j^{\prime}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\binom{j^{\prime}+i-1}{j^{\prime}-1}\cdot(-\eta_{2})^{j^{\prime}+i}.

By taking duals, we obtain (
184
).
This completes the proof of the former assertion.
Moreover,
the latter assertion follows from (
184
) together with Lemma
1.2.1
.
∎

Next, we shall set

(188)

\displaystyle\iota_{1}:U_{\oslash}\hookrightarrow U_{\otimes}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \iota_{2}:U_{\oslash}\hookrightarrow U_{\otimes}\right)

to be the closed immersion corresponding to the surjection
\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t_{1},t_{2}]\!]/(t_{1}t_{2})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]
given by
t_{1}\mapsto t
and
t_{2}\mapsto 0
(resp.,
t_{1}\mapsto 0
and
t_{2}\mapsto t
).
The inclusion into the
i
-th factor
\mathbb{N}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{N}\oplus\mathbb{N}
determines a morphism of log schemes

(189)

\displaystyle U_{\oslash}\times_{\iota_{i},U_{\otimes}}U_{\otimes}^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}

whose underlying morphism of
S
-schemes coincides with the identity morphism of
U_{\oslash}
.
This morphism induces an identification
\iota_{i}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\otimes})={{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
.
The
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
-linear surjection

(190)

\displaystyle\omega_{i}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}\twoheadrightarrow\left(\iota_{i*}(\iota_{i}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\otimes}))=\right)\iota_{i*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})

corresponding to this identification via the adjunction relation “
\iota^{*}_{i}(-)\dashv\iota_{i*}(-)

maps
\partial_{i}^{\langle j\rangle}
to
\partial^{\langle j\rangle}
for every
j\geq 0
.

For a
\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
,
we shall write

(191)

\displaystyle\iota^{*}_{i}(\nabla):{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\iota_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F}))

for the
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\iota_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F})
induced naturally by
\nabla
under the identification
\iota_{i}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\otimes})={{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
.

3.2.
Monodromy operator

We shall set

(192)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{S}:=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{O}_{S}\cdot\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j\rangle},

where
\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle i\rangle}
’s are abstract symbols.
We equip
\mathcal{B}_{S}
with a structure of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebra given by

(193)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}\cdot\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=\sum_{j=\mathrm{max}\{j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}\}}^{j^{\prime}+j^{\prime\prime}}\frac{j!}{(j^{\prime}+j^{\prime\prime}-j)!\cdot(j-j^{\prime})!\cdot(j-j^{\prime\prime})!}\cdot\frac{q_{j^{\prime}}!\cdot q_{j^{\prime\prime}}!}{q_{j}!}\cdot\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j\rangle}.

In particular, the
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebra
\mathcal{B}_{S}
is commutative and generated by the sections
\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle 1\rangle},\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle p\rangle},\cdots,\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle p^{m}\rangle}
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module and
\mu
a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebras

\mathcal{B}_{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{G})
.
For each
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
we set

\mu^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}:=\mu(\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle p^{a}\rangle})
, which is an element of
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{G})
.
The morphism
\mu
is uniquely determined by
the
(m+1)
-tuple

(194)

\displaystyle\mu^{\langle\bullet\rangle}:=(\mu^{\langle 1\rangle},\mu^{\langle p\rangle}\cdots,\mu^{\langle p^{m}\rangle})\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{G})^{\oplus(m+1)}.

Denote by
\sigma_{\oslash}:S\rightarrow U_{\oslash}
(resp.,
\sigma_{\otimes}:S\rightarrow U_{\otimes}
) the closed immersion corresponding to the surjection
\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{S}
given by
t\mapsto 0
(resp., the surjection
\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t_{1},t_{2}]\!]/(t_{1}t_{2})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{S}
given by
t_{1}\mapsto 0
and
t_{2}\mapsto 0
).
It follows from (
32
) and (
193
) that the assignment
\partial^{\langle j\rangle}\mapsto\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j\rangle}
(
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) determines an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebras

\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{B}_{S}
.
This morphism induces, via the adjunction relation “
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(-)\dashv\sigma_{\oslash*}(-)
”,
an
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
-linear surjection

(195)

\displaystyle\omega:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}\twoheadrightarrow\sigma_{\oslash*}(\mathcal{B}_{S}).

Now, let
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module.
The
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla
induces an
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})
-action
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\nabla)
on
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})
; it gives
the composite

(196)

\displaystyle\mu(\nabla):\mathcal{B}_{S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})\xrightarrow{\sigma^{*}_{\oslash}(\nabla)}\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})).

In particular, we obtain

(197)

\displaystyle\mu(\nabla)^{\langle\bullet\rangle}:=(\mu(\nabla)^{\langle 1\rangle},\mu(\nabla)^{\langle p\rangle}\cdots,\mu(\nabla)^{\langle p^{m}\rangle})\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{\oplus(m+1)}.

Since
\mathcal{B}_{S}
is commutative,
the elements
\mu(\nabla)^{\langle 1\rangle},\cdots,\mu(\nabla)^{\langle p^{m}\rangle}
commute with each other.

Definition 3.2.1
.

We shall refer to
\mu(\nabla)
and
\mu(\nabla)^{\langle\bullet\rangle}
as
the
monodromy operator
of
\nabla
.
Also, for each
a=0,\cdots,m
,
we shall refer to
\mu(\nabla)^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}
as the
a
-th monodromy operator
of
\nabla
.

Note that there exists uniquely an automorphism

(198)

\displaystyle\mathrm{sw}:\mathcal{B}_{S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{B}_{S}

of the
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebra
\mathcal{B}_{S}

determined by

\mathrm{sw}(\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j\rangle})=(-1)^{j}\cdot\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{j}\binom{j-1}{j^{\prime}-1}\cdot\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}

for every positive integer
j\leq p^{m}
(cf. Lemma
3.1.1
).
In particular, the equality
\mathrm{sw}(\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle p^{a}\rangle})=\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{p^{a}}(-1)^{j^{\prime}}\cdot\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}
holds for
a\leq m
.
This automorphism is involutive, i.e.,
\mathrm{sw}\circ\mathrm{sw}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}
.

For an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module
\mathcal{G}
,
we shall set

(199)

\displaystyle\mathrm{sw}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{G})^{\oplus(m+1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{G})^{\oplus(m+1)}

to be the endomap of
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{G})^{\oplus(m+1)}
given by

(200)

\displaystyle\mathrm{sw}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{G}}((A_{a})_{a=0}^{m}):=((-A_{a}-\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{G}}+\prod_{b=0}^{a-1}(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{G}}-A_{b}^{p-1}))_{a=0}^{m}),

where
\prod_{b=0}^{-1}(-):=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{G}}
and
\prod_{b=0}^{a-1}B_{b}:=B_{0}\circ B_{1}\circ\cdots\circ B_{a-1}
(
a\geq 1
).
If
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
is as above, then
the second equality in (
185
) implies

(201)

\displaystyle(\mu(\nabla)\circ\mathrm{sw})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}=\mathrm{sw}_{\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})}^{\bullet}(\mu(\nabla)^{\langle\bullet\rangle}).

Proposition 3.2.2
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
-module.

(i)

Let
\nabla
be a
\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}
.
Then, the equality

(202)

\displaystyle\mu(\iota_{1}^{*}(\nabla))\circ\mathrm{sw}=\mu(\iota_{2}^{*}(\nabla))\left(\Longleftrightarrow\mathrm{sw}^{\bullet}_{\sigma_{\otimes}^{*}(\mathcal{F})}(\mu(\iota_{1}^{*}(\nabla))^{\langle\bullet\rangle})=\mu(\iota_{2}^{*}(\nabla))^{\langle\bullet\rangle}\ \text{by (\ref{dE110})}\right)

holds.

(ii)

Conversely,
let
\nabla_{i}
(for each
i=1,2
) be a
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\iota_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F})
,
and suppose that
the equality
\mu(\nabla_{1})\circ\mathrm{sw}=\mu(\nabla_{2})
(or equivalently,
\mathrm{sw}^{\bullet}_{\sigma_{\otimes}^{*}(\mathcal{F})}(\mu(\nabla_{1})^{\langle\bullet\rangle})=\mu(\nabla_{2})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}
) holds.
Then, there exists a unique
\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla
on
\mathcal{F}

satisfying the equalities
\iota_{1}^{*}(\nabla)=\nabla_{1}
and
\iota_{2}^{*}(\nabla)=\nabla_{2}
under the natural identifications
\iota_{1}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)})={{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
and
\iota_{2}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)})={{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
, respectively.

Proof.

We shall set
{{}^{L}}\breve{\mathcal{D}}_{\otimes}^{(m)}
to be the sheaf defined as

(203)

\displaystyle{{}^{L}}\breve{\mathcal{D}}_{\otimes}^{(m)}

\displaystyle:=\iota_{1*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})\times_{\omega,\mathcal{B}_{S},\mathrm{sw}\circ\omega}\iota_{2*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})
