\displaystyle\left(=\left\{(s_{1},s_{2})\in\iota_{1*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})\times\iota_{2*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})\,\Big|\,\iota_{1*}(\omega)(s_{1})=(\sigma_{\otimes*}(\mathrm{sw})\circ\iota_{2*}(\omega))(s_{2})\right\}\right).

By using the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\iota_{1*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})\times_{\sigma_{\otimes*}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}\iota_{2*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})
given by
t_{1}\mapsto(t,0)
and
t_{2}\mapsto(0,t)
,
we equip
{{}^{L}}\breve{\mathcal{D}}_{\otimes}^{(m)}
with an
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
-module structure.
It follows from Lemma
3.1.1
that the assignment

s\mapsto(\omega_{1}(s),\omega_{2}(s))
for each local section
s\in{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}
defines an
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
-linear isomorphism

(204)

\displaystyle{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}{{}^{L}}\breve{\mathcal{D}}_{\otimes}^{(m)}.

Hence, both assertions (i) and (ii) are direct consequences of this isomorphism.
∎

3.3.
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structures
\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}
and
\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)}

In §§
3.3
-
3.4
,
we suppose that
S
is a scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
(equipped with the trivial
m
-PD structure).
Note that the discussions in §§
1.5
-
1.6
can be applied
even when “
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
” is replaced by
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
or
U_{\otimes}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
In particular,
we can define the
p^{m+1}
-curvature of a
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module, as well as of a
\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}
-module.

The Frobenius twist
U_{\oslash}^{(m+1)}
(resp.,
U_{\otimes}^{(m+1)}
) of
U_{\oslash}
(resp.,
U_{\otimes}
) over
S
may be identified with
the relative affine scheme over
S
associated to the
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-subalgebra

(205)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t^{p^{m+1}}]\!]\ \left(\text{resp.},\ \mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t^{p^{m+1}}_{1},t_{2}^{p^{m+1}}]\!]/(t_{1}^{p^{m+1}}t_{2}^{p^{m+1}})\right)

of
\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t_{1},t_{2}]\!]/(t_{1}t_{2})
).
For simplicity, we write
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(m+1)}:=\mathcal{O}_{U_{\oslash}^{(m+1)}}
and

\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}^{(m+1)}:=\mathcal{O}_{U_{\otimes}^{(m+1)}}
.

Given an element
d
of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
,
we denote by
\widetilde{d}
the integer defined as the unique lifting of
d
via the natural surjection
\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
satisfying
0\leq\widetilde{d}<p^{m+1}
.
Let
\widetilde{d}_{[0]},\cdots,\widetilde{d}_{[m]}

be
the collection of integers uniquely determined by the condition that
\widetilde{d}=\sum_{a=0}^{m}p^{a}\cdot\widetilde{d}_{[a]}
and
0\leq\widetilde{d}_{[a]}<p
(
a=0,\cdots,m
).
Also,
for each
a=0,\cdots,m
, we write

\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}:=\sum_{b=0}^{a}p^{b}\cdot\widetilde{d}_{[b]}
and write

d_{[a]}
(resp.,
d_{[0,a]}
) for the image of
\widetilde{d}_{[a]}
(resp.,
\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}
) via the natural projection
\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{p}
(resp.,
\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}/p^{a+1}\mathbb{Z}
).

Now, let us fix an element
d\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
Then, there exists a unique
\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}
-module structure

(206)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})

on
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
determined by the condition that

\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}(\partial^{\langle j\rangle})(t^{n})=q_{j}!\cdot\binom{n-\widetilde{d}}{j}\cdot t^{n}

for every
j
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
The resulting
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module

(207)

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}:=(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)})

is isomorphic to the unique extension of

\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,0}^{(m)}

to
t^{-\widetilde{d}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\left(\supseteq\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\right)
.
In particular
\nabla^{(m)}_{\oslash,d}
has vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature.

Proposition 3.3.1
.

For each
a=0,\cdots,m
, we have

(208)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a)})=t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\right).

Moreover, under the identification
\left(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a-1)})=\right)t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a)}=\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
given by
t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}}\cdot h(t^{p^{a}})\leftrightarrow h(t)
for each
h(t)\in\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]
(where
t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a)}:=\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
if
a=0
),
the following equality of
S
-connections holds:

(209)

\displaystyle(\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)})^{[a]}=\nabla_{\oslash,d_{[a]}}^{(0)}.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the definition of
\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}
.
∎

Proposition 3.3.2
.

Under the natural identification
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}))=\Gamma(S,\mathcal{O}_{S})\left(\supseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}\right)
,
the following equality holds:

(210)

\displaystyle\mu(\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}=((-d)_{[0]},\cdots,(-d)_{[m]}).

Moreover,
the equality

(211)

\displaystyle\mu(\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)})\circ\mathrm{sw}=\mu(\nabla_{\oslash,-d}^{(m)})\ \left(\Longleftrightarrow\mathrm{sw}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mu(\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)})^{\langle\bullet\rangle})=\mu(\nabla_{\oslash,-d}^{(m)})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}\right)

holds.

Proof.

Since the former assertion follows from the definition of
\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}
, we only prove the latter assertion.
To this end, it suffices to consider the case of
d\neq 0
.
Let us write
c:=-d
.
Since
c\neq 0
, the nonnegative integer
a_{0}:=\mathrm{min}\left\{a\,|\,c_{[a]}\neq 0\right\}
is well-defined.
For each
a\leq a_{0}
, the following equalities hold:

(212)

\displaystyle-c_{[a]}-1+\prod_{b=0}^{a-1}(1-c_{[b]}^{p-1})=-c_{[a]}-1+\prod_{b=0}^{a-1}(1-0)=-c_{[a]}-1+1=d_{[a]}.

On the other hand, if
a>a_{0}
, then we have

(213)

\displaystyle-c_{[a]}-1+\prod_{b=0}^{a-1}(1-c_{[b]}^{p-1})=-c_{[a]}-1=d_{[a]},

where the first equality follows from
1-c_{[a_{0}]}^{p-1}=0
.
By (
212
), (
213
), and the former assertion,
the following sequence of equalities hold:

(214)

\displaystyle\mathrm{sw}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\left(\mu(\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{sw}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\left(c_{[0]},\cdots,c_{[m]}\right)

\displaystyle=(-c_{[a]}-1+\prod_{b=0}^{a-1}(1-c_{[b]}^{p-1}))_{a=0}^{m}

\displaystyle=\left(d_{[a]}\right)_{a=0}^{m}

\displaystyle=\mu(\nabla_{\oslash,-d}^{(m)})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}.

This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

By Propositions
3.2.2
, (ii), and
3.3.2
,
there exists a unique
\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}
-module structure

(215)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{\otimes})

on
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}

with
\iota_{1}^{*}(\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)})=\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}
and
\iota_{2}^{*}(\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)})=\nabla_{\oslash,-d}^{(m)}
.
It is verified that
\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)}
has vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature.
We shall write

(216)

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{\otimes,d}^{(m)}:=(\mathscr{O}_{\otimes},\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)}).

Proposition 3.3.3
.

We shall write
c:=-d
.
Also, let us fix
a\in\{0,\cdots,m\}
.

(i)

We have

(217)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a)})=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}^{(a+1)}&\text{if $\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}=0$;}\\
t_{1}^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}}\cdot\iota_{1*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)})\oplus t_{2}^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a]}}\cdot\iota_{2*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)})&\text{if otherwise.}\end{cases}

(ii)

Suppose that
a=0
or
\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}=0
.
Then,
under the identification
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}^{(a)}=\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
given by
h(t^{p^{a}})\leftrightarrow h(t)
for each
h(t)\in\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]
,
the following equality of
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connections holds:

(218)

\displaystyle(\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)})^{[a]}=\nabla^{(0)}_{\otimes,d_{[a]}}.

(iii)

Suppose that
a\geq 1
and

\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}\neq 0
.
Then,
under the identification

(219)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)\Rightarrow(a-1)})=\right)t_{1}^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}}\cdot\iota_{1*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a)})\oplus t_{2}^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a-1]}}\cdot\iota_{2*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a)})=\iota_{1*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})\oplus\iota_{2*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})

given by

(t_{1}^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}}\cdot h_{1}(t_{1}^{p^{a}}),t_{2}^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a-1]}}\cdot h_{2}(t_{2}^{p^{a}}))\leftrightarrow(h_{1}(t),h_{2}(t))
for each pair
(h_{1}(t),h_{2}(t))\in\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]^{\times 2}
,
the following equality of
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connections holds:

(220)

\displaystyle(\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)})^{[a]}=\iota_{1*}(\nabla_{\oslash,d_{[a]}}^{(0)})\oplus\iota_{2*}(\nabla_{\oslash,c_{[a]}}^{(0)}).

Proof.

The assertions follow from Proposition
3.3.1
.
∎

Also, the following assertion can be proved immediately.

Proposition 3.3.4
.

Let
d
and
c
be elements of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
Also, let
\odot\in\{\oslash,\otimes\}
.

(i)

The canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{\odot}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\odot}}\mathcal{O}_{\odot}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\odot}
defines an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{\odot}^{(m)}
-modules

(221)

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{\odot,d}^{(m)}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{\odot,c}^{(m)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{O}_{\odot,d+c}^{(m)}.

In particular, we have

\mathscr{O}_{\odot,d}^{(m)\vee}=\mathscr{O}_{\odot,-d}^{(m)}
.

(ii)

The following equality holds:

(222)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}(\mathscr{O}_{\odot,d}^{(m)},\mathscr{O}_{\odot,c}^{(m)})=\left\{\mathrm{mult}_{s}\,\Big|\,s\in\Gamma(U_{\odot},\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\odot,c-d}^{(m)}))\right\},

where
\mathrm{mult}_{s}
denotes the endomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\odot}
given by multiplication by
s
.
In particular, there exists a surjective morphism

\mathscr{O}_{\odot,d}^{(m)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\odot,c}^{(m)}

if and only if the equality
d=c
holds.

(iii)

Let
m^{\prime}
be a nonnegative integer
\leq m
.
Then, the following equality holds:

(223)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\odot,d}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m^{\prime})}=\nabla_{\odot,d_{[0,m^{\prime}]}}^{(m^{\prime})}.

3.4.
Local description of
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules

In this subsection, we show that a
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}_{\odot}^{(m)}
-module (where
\odot\in\{\oslash,\otimes\}
) can be described as the direct sum of

\mathscr{O}_{\odot,d}^{(m)}
’s
for some elements
d\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
This assertion generalizes
[
Kin
, Proposition 1.1.12]
, in which
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules on the formal disc
\mathrm{Spec}(k[\![t]\!])
over an algebraically closed field
k
were discussed.
See also
[
Oss2
, Corollary 2.10]
for the case of
m=0
and
[
Gie
, Theorem 3.3]
for the case of so-called
F
-divided (or, stratified) bundles, i.e., the case of
m=\infty
.

Proposition-Definition 3.4.1
.

Suppose that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
for
a local ring
(R,\mathfrak{m})
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
such that the residue field
k:=R/\mathfrak{m}
is algebraically closed.
Let
\odot\in\{\oslash,\otimes\}
, and let

(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
be a

p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}_{\odot}^{(m)}
-module such that
\mathcal{F}
is a (locally) free
\mathcal{O}_{\odot}
-module of
rank
n>0
.
Then, there exists an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{\odot}^{(m)}
-modules

(224)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}_{\odot,d_{i}}^{(m)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})

for some
d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
(This implies that, for each
a=0,\cdots,m
, the
a
-th monodromy operator
\mu(\nabla^{(m)})^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}
of
\nabla^{(m)}

can be transformed, after choosing a suitable trivialization
\sigma_{\odot}^{*}(\mathcal{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\oplus n}
of
\sigma_{\odot}^{*}(\mathcal{F})
, into
the diagonal matrix with diagonal entries
(-d)_{1[a]},\cdots,(-d)_{n[a]}
.)
Moreover, the resulting multiset

(225)

\displaystyle e(\nabla^{(m)}):=[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]

depends only on the isomorphism class of
(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
.
We shall refer to

e(\nabla^{(m)})

as the
exponent
of
(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
.

Proof.

First, let us consider the case where
\odot=\oslash
.
Hereinafter, we shall use subscripted “
k
” to denote the result of reducing modulo
\mathfrak{m}
.
Since the
\mathcal{D}_{\otimes,k}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{F}_{k},\nabla_{k}^{(m)})
has vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature,
it follows from
[
Kin
, Proposition 1.1.12]
that
there exist a multiset
[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]
of elements in
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
and
an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{\otimes,k}^{(m)}
-modules

(226)

\displaystyle\xi:\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,k},\nabla_{\oslash,d_{i},k}^{(m)})\cong\right)\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(t^{-\widetilde{d}_{i}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,k},\nabla_{\oslash,0,k}^{(m)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{F}_{k},\nabla_{k}^{(m)}),

where, for each
\widetilde{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we abuse notation by writing
\nabla_{\oslash,0,k}^{(m)}
for the
\mathcal{D}_{\otimes,k}^{(m)}
-module structure
on the line bundle
t^{-\widetilde{d}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,k}\left(\supseteq\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,k}\right)
extending

(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,k},\nabla_{\oslash,0,k}^{(m)})
.

Now, let us choose
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
.
Denote by
\nabla^{(m)}_{j}
the
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module structure on
t^{\widetilde{d}_{j}}\cdot\mathcal{F}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{F}\right)
obtained by restricting
\nabla^{(m)}
.
Then,
\xi
restricts to an isomorphism

(227)

\displaystyle\xi_{j}:\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(t^{-\widetilde{d}_{i}+\widetilde{d}_{j}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,k},\nabla_{\oslash,0,k}^{(m)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(t^{\widetilde{d}_{j}}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{k},\nabla_{j,k}^{(m)}).

If
e_{j}
denotes the image of
1\in\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,k}
via the inclusion into the
j
-th factor
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,k}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,k}^{\oplus n}
,
then it is a horizontal section in the domain of
\xi_{j}
.
In particular, we have
\xi_{j}(e_{j})\in\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{j,k}^{(m)})
.
Since the natural morphism
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{j}^{(m)})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{j,k}^{(m)})
is surjective by Proposition
1.6.2
, (ii),
we can find a section
v_{j}\in\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{j}^{(m)})
mapped to
\xi_{j}(e_{j})
via this surjection.
The section
v_{j}
determines a morphism of

\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-modules
(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,0}^{(m)})\rightarrow(t^{\widetilde{d}_{j}}\cdot\mathcal{F},\nabla_{j}^{(m)})
;
it can be extended uniquely to a morphism
\zeta_{j}:(t^{-\widetilde{d}_{j}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,0}^{(m)})\rightarrow(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
.
Thus, we obtain the composite

(228)

\displaystyle\zeta:\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,d_{j}}^{(m)})\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(t^{-\widetilde{d}_{i}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,0}^{(m)})\xrightarrow{\bigoplus_{i}\zeta_{i}}(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)}),

where the first arrow is the direct sum of the natural isomorphisms

(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,d_{j}}^{(m)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(t^{-\widetilde{d}_{i}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,0}^{(m)})
.
Note that the reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}
of
\bigoplus_{i}\zeta_{i}
coincides with the isomorphism
\xi
.
Hence,
\zeta
turns out to be an isomorphism by Nakayama’s lemma.
This proves the former assertion for
\odot=\oslash
.
Moreover, the latter assertion follows immediately from Proposition
3.3.4
, (ii), in the case of
\odot=\oslash
.

Next, we shall consider the case of
\odot=\otimes
.
It follows from the above discussion that
there exists an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-modules

(229)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\oslash,1}:\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(\iota_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}),\iota_{1}^{*}(\nabla_{\otimes,d_{i}}^{(m)}))=\right)\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla^{(m)}_{\oslash,d_{i}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\iota_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{F}),\iota_{1}^{*}(\nabla^{(m)}))

for some
d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
By Propositions
3.2.2
, (i), and
3.3.2
,
the exponent of
(\iota_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{F}),\iota_{2}^{*}(\nabla^{(m)}))
coincides with
[-d_{1},\cdots,-d_{n}]
.
Hence, we can find an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-modules

(230)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\oslash,2}:\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(\iota_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}),\iota_{2}^{*}(\nabla_{\otimes,d_{i}}^{(m)}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\iota_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{F}),\iota_{2}^{*}(\nabla^{(m)})).

The automorphism
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,2})^{-1}\circ\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,1})
defines an

n\times n
matrix
A:=(a_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(R)
(under the natural identification
\Gamma(S,\sigma_{\otimes}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}))=R
).
We shall set
D:=(D_{[0]},\cdots,D_{[m]})
, where each
D_{[a]}
(
a=0,\cdots,m
) denotes the diagonal matrix with diagonal entries
(-d)_{1[a]},\cdots,(-d)_{n[a]}
.
By regarding this matrix as an element of
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\oplus n})^{\oplus(m+1)}
,
we can identify
D
(resp.,
\mathrm{sw}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(D)
) with the monodromy operator
\mu(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\iota_{1}^{*}(\nabla_{\otimes,d_{i}}^{(m)}))^{\langle\bullet\rangle}
(resp.,
\mu(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\iota_{2}^{*}(\nabla_{\otimes,d_{i}}^{(m)}))^{\langle\bullet\rangle}
) of
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\iota_{1}^{*}(\nabla_{\otimes,d_{i}}^{(m)})
(resp.,
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\iota_{2}^{*}(\nabla_{\otimes,d_{i}}^{(m)})
).
Since both
\zeta_{\oslash,1}
and
\zeta_{\oslash,2}
preserve the
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module structure, we obtain the following equalities of elements in
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\oplus n})^{\oplus(m+1)}
:

(231)

\displaystyle\mu\circ\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,1})=\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,1})\circ D,\hskip 14.22636pt\mathrm{sw}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}^{\bullet}(\mu)\circ\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,2})=\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,2})\circ\mathrm{sw}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}^{\bullet}(D),

where
\mu:=\mu(\iota_{1}^{*}(\nabla^{(m)}))^{\langle\bullet\rangle}
.
These equalities imply

(232)

\displaystyle\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,2})\circ D\circ\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,2})\circ\mathrm{sw}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}^{\bullet}(\mathrm{sw}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}^{\bullet}(D))\circ\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{sw}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}^{\bullet}(\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,2})\circ\mathrm{sw}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}^{\bullet}(D)\circ\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,2})^{-1})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{sw}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}^{\bullet}(\mathrm{sw}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}^{\bullet}(\mu))

\displaystyle=\mu

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,1})\circ D\circ\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,1})^{-1}.

It follows that
DA=AD
, i.e.,
(D_{[0]}A,\cdots,D_{[m]}A)=(AD_{[0]},\cdots,AD_{[m]})
.
Hence, for each pair
(i,j)
with
d_{i}\neq d_{j}
,
we have
a_{ij}=a_{ji}=0
.
By Proposition
3.3.4
, (ii),
the matrix
A
defines an automorphism
\alpha
of the
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(\iota_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}),\iota_{2}^{*}(\nabla_{\otimes,d_{i}}^{(m)}))
when regarded as an element of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(R[\![t]\!])
via the natural inclusion
R\hookrightarrow R[\![t]\!]
.
The equality
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,1})=\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\zeta_{\oslash,2}\circ\alpha)
holds, so the two morphisms of
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-modules

\zeta_{\oslash,1}
,
\zeta_{\oslash,2}\circ\alpha
may be glued together (by using (
204
)) to obtain an isomorphism

(233)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{O}_{\otimes},\nabla_{\otimes,d_{i}}^{(m)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)}).

This completes the proof of the former assertion for
\odot=\otimes
.
Moreover, the latter assertion can be proved by applying Proposition
3.3.4
, (ii), in the case of
\odot=\otimes
.
Thus, we have finished the proof of this proposition.
∎

The following assertion is
a corollary of the above proposition.

Corollary 3.4.2
.

Let
a\in\{0,\cdots,m\}
, and let
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a
p^{m+1}
-flat
\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(m)}
-module such that
\mathcal{F}
is locally free of rank
n>0
.
Then, the natural
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}^{(a)}
-linear morphism

(\mathcal{F}^{\vee})^{[a]}\rightarrow(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})^{\vee}

restricts to an
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}^{(a+1)}
-linear isomorphism

(234)

\displaystyle\left((\mathcal{F}^{\vee})^{[a+1]}=\right)\mathrm{Ker}((\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Ker}((\nabla^{[a]})^{\vee}).

Proof.

It suffices to prove the assertion under the assumption in Proposition-Definition
3.4.1
.
Then, by that proposition, the problem is reduced to the case where
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)=(\mathcal{O}_{\otimes},\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)})
for an element
d
of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}
.

If
a=0
or
\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}=0
(
a\geq 1
), then the assertion follows immediately from Proposition
3.3.3
, (ii).
Before proving the remaining case,
let us make the following observation.
For each
u
,
v\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, we shall denote by
\nabla_{\oslash,(u,v)}^{(0)}
the

\mathcal{D}_{\otimes}^{(0)}
-module structure
on the
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
-module
\iota_{1*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})\oplus\iota_{2*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})
expressed as

\nabla_{\oslash,(u,v)}^{(0)}:=\iota_{1*}(\nabla_{\oslash,u}^{(0)})\oplus\iota_{2*}(\nabla_{\oslash,v}^{(0)})
.
By regarding
\iota_{1*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})\oplus\iota_{2*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})
as an
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
-submodule of
\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
via the injection

(\iota_{1*},\iota_{2*}):\iota_{1*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})\oplus\iota_{2*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}
,
we have

(235)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \mathrm{Hom}\left((\iota_{1*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})\oplus\iota_{2*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}),\nabla_{\oslash,(u,v)}^{(0)}),(\mathcal{O}_{\otimes},\nabla_{\otimes,0}^{(0)})\right)\left(=\Gamma(U_{\otimes},\mathcal{S}ol((\nabla_{\oslash,(u,v)}^{(0)})^{\vee}))\right)

\displaystyle=\left\{(\mathrm{mult}_{\alpha},\mathrm{mult}_{\beta})\,\Big|\,\alpha\in t_{1}^{p-\widetilde{u}}\cdot R[\![t_{1}^{p}]\!],\beta\in t_{2}^{p-\widetilde{v}}\cdot R[\![t_{2}^{p}]\!]\right\},

where

\mathrm{mult}_{(-)}
denotes the morphism given by multiplication by
(-)
.

Now, let us go back to the proof of the remaining case, i.e., the case where
a\geq 1
and
\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}\neq 0
.
Write
c:=-d
.
By taking account of (
235
) and Proposition
3.3.3
, (iii),
we obtain the following sequence of equalities:

(236)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \Gamma(U_{\otimes},\mathrm{Ker}(((\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)})^{[a]})^{\vee}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}\left((t_{1}^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}}\cdot\iota_{1*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a)})\oplus t_{2}^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a-1]}}\cdot\iota_{2*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a)}),(\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)})^{[a]}),(\mathcal{O}_{\otimes}^{(a)},\nabla_{U_{\otimes}^{(a)},\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})\right)

\displaystyle=\left\{(\mathrm{mult}_{\alpha},\mathrm{mult}_{\beta})\,\bigg|\,\alpha\in t_{1}^{-\widetilde{d}_{[0,a-1]}+p^{a}(p-\widetilde{d}_{[a]})}\cdot R[\![t_{1}^{p^{a+1}}]\!],\beta\in t_{2}^{-\widetilde{c}_{[0,a-1]}+p^{a}(p-\widetilde{c}_{[a]})}\cdot R[\![t_{2}^{p^{a+1}}]\!]\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{(\mathrm{mult}_{\alpha},\mathrm{mult}_{\beta})\,\bigg|\,\alpha\in t_{1}^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a]}}\cdot R[\![t_{1}^{p^{a+1}}]\!],\beta\in t_{2}^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}}\cdot R[\![t_{2}^{p^{a+1}}]\!]\right\}

\displaystyle=\Gamma(U_{\otimes},\mathrm{Ker}((\nabla_{\otimes,-d}^{(m)})^{[a]}))

\displaystyle=\Gamma(U_{\otimes},\mathrm{Ker}(((\nabla_{\otimes,d}^{(m)})^{\vee})^{[a]})).

This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

3.5.
Local description of dormant flat bundles

In the rest of this section, we suppose that
S
is a flat scheme over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
Note that the notion of a
dormant
flat bundle on
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
, as well as the functor
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-)
, can be defined in the same manner as the case where the underlying space is a log curve.
Just as in the discussion of §
2
,
we will denote by a subscript
m^{\prime}
(for each
m^{\prime}\leq m
) the result of reducing an object over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
modulo
p^{m^{\prime}+1}
.
Also, we use the notation “
\nabla_{\oslash,d,0}^{(m)}
” to denote the
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash,0}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,0}
introduced in (
206
).
In particular, we obtain
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d,0}^{(m)}:=(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,0},\nabla_{\oslash,d,0}^{(m)})
.

Let
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a flat module on
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S
.
Since
\nabla
induces an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-linear morphism
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\nabla):\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\Omega_{U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\otimes\mathcal{F})
,
we obtain the
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-linear composite

(237)

\displaystyle\mu(\nabla):\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\nabla)}\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\Omega_{U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\otimes\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\Omega_{U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})\otimes\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Res}_{\sigma_{\oslash}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}\left(\mathcal{O}_{S}\otimes\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})=\right)\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F}),

where
\mathrm{Res}_{\sigma_{\oslash}}
denotes the residue map
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\Omega_{U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{S}
.

Definition 3.5.1
.

We shall refer to
\mu(\nabla)\left(\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F}))\right)
as the
monodromy operator
of
\nabla
.
(This is essentially the same as the notion of (
0
-th) monodromy operator of the corresponding
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(0)}
-module, in the sense of Definition
3.2.1
.)

For each element
d
of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
,
we denote by
\nabla_{\oslash,d}:\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\rightarrow\Omega_{U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
given by

(238)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\oslash,d}(v):=D(v)-d\cdot v\cdot\frac{dt}{t}

for any local section
v\in\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
, where
D
denotes the universal derivation
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\rightarrow\Omega_{U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
Thus, we obtain a flat line bundle

(239)

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}:=(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,d})

on
U^{\mathrm{log}}_{\oslash}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Note that
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}
is
p
-flat and
isomorphic to the unique extension of

\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,0}

to
t^{-\widetilde{d}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\left(\supseteq\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\right)
.
Also, the equality
\mu(\nabla_{\oslash,d})=-d
holds.

Proposition 3.5.2
.

Let
d
be an element of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.

(i)

The flat bundle
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}

is dormant
and satisfies

(240)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}=\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d,0}^{(m)}.

Moreover,
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}
is the unique (up to isomorphism)
diagonal lifting of
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d,0}^{(m)}
.

(ii)

The following equality holds:

(241)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\oslash,d})=t^{\widetilde{d}}\cdot\left(\sum_{a=0}^{m}p^{m-a}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t^{p^{a+1}}]\!]\right)\left(\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]\right).

Proof.

We shall prove assertion (i) by induction on
m
.
There is nothing to prove for the base step, i.e., the case of
m=0
.
To consider the induction step, we suppose that the assertion with
m
replaced by
m-1
(
m\geq 1
) has been proved.
We shall set
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,d})
.
By the induction hypothesis,

(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,m-1},\nabla_{\oslash,d_{[0,m-1]}})
is the unique diagonal lifting of
(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,0},\nabla_{\oslash,d,0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})
(cf. Proposition
3.3.4
, (iii)).
It follows from Proposition
3.3.1
that

\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}}\left(=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\oslash,d,0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)})\right)=t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,m-1]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{S_{0}}[\![t^{p^{m}}]\!]
.
The section
t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,m-1]}}
of
\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]
is horizontal modulo
p^{m}
with respect to
\nabla_{\oslash,d}
and satisfies

(242)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\oslash,d}(t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,m-1]}})=-p^{m}\cdot(d_{[m]}\cdot t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,m-1]}})\cdot\frac{dt}{t}.

Hence, under the identification

(243)

\displaystyle t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,m-1]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{S_{0}}[\![t^{p^{m}}]\!]=\mathcal{O}_{S_{0}}[\![t]\!]

given by
t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,m-1]}}\cdot h(t^{p^{m}})\leftrightarrow h(t)
(for any
h(t)\in\mathcal{O}_{S_{0}}[\![t]\!]
),
the flat bundle
(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}},\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})
coincides with
(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,0},\nabla_{\oslash,d_{[m]}})
.
By
[
Wak7
, Proposition 3.2.1, (i) and (iv)]
,

\nabla_{\oslash,d,0}^{(m)}
is the unique
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash,0}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla^{(m)}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,0}
satisfying that
\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)}=\nabla_{\oslash,d,0}^{(m)\Rightarrow(m-1)}
and
(\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{F}},\nabla_{\mathscr{F}})=(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,0}^{[m]},(\nabla^{(m)})^{[m]})
(under the identification (
243
)).
In particular,

(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,d})

turns out to be the unique diagonal lifting of
(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,0},\nabla_{\oslash,d,0}^{(m)})
.
This completes the proof of assertion (i).

Also, assertion (ii) follows immediately from the definition of
\nabla_{\oslash,d}
.
∎

The following assertion can be proved immediately.

Proposition 3.5.3
.

Let
d
and
c
be elements of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.

(i)

The canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}}\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
defines an isomorphism of flat bundles

(244)

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,c}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d+c}.

In particular, we have

\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}^{\vee}=\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,-d}
.
Moreover,
the isomorphism (
244
) is compatible with (
221
)
via (
240
).

(ii)

We have

(245)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}(\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d},\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,c})=\left\{\mathrm{mult}_{s}\,|\,s\in\Gamma(U_{\oslash},\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\oslash,c-d}))\right\},

where
\mathrm{mult}_{s}
denotes the endomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
given by multiplication by
s
.
In particular,
there exists a surjection morphism
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,c}
if and only if the equality
d=c
holds.
Moreover,
the equality (
245
) is compatible,
in a natural sense, with (
222
) via the functor
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-)
and the composite

(246)

\displaystyle\Gamma(U_{\oslash},\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\oslash,c-d}))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{mod}\,p}\Gamma(U_{\oslash,0},\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\oslash,c-d})_{0})\xrightarrow{(\ref{YY82})}\Gamma(U_{\oslash,0},\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\oslash,c-d,0}^{(m)})).

Also, by applying Proposition-Definition
3.4.1
(for
\odot=\oslash
),
we obtain the following assertion.

Proposition-Definition 3.5.4
.

Suppose that
S_{0}=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
for a local ring
(R,\mathfrak{m})
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
such that the residue field
k:=R/\mathfrak{m}
is algebraically closed.
Let
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a dormant flat bundle on
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
of rank
n>0
.
Then, there exists an isomorphism of flat bundles

(247)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d_{i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{F}

for some
d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
(This implies that the monodromy operator
\mu(\nabla)
of
\nabla
can be transformed, after choosing a suitable trivialization
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\oplus n}
of
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})
, into the diagonal matrix with diagonal entries
-d_{1},\cdots,-d_{n}
.)
Moreover, the resulting multiset

(248)

\displaystyle e(\nabla):=[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]

depends only on the isomorphism class of
\mathscr{F}
.
We shall refer to
e(\nabla)
as the
exponent
of
\mathscr{F}
.

Proof.

According to Proposition-Definition
3.4.1
,
there exists an isomorphism

(249)

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}:\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d_{i},0}^{(m)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}

for some
d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
Let us choose
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
.
Since the functor
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-)
commutes with the formation of tensor products,
it follows from Propositions
3.3.4
, (i), and
3.5.2
, (i), that

\zeta_{0}
induces an isomorphism

(250)

\displaystyle\zeta_{0,j}:\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d_{i}-d_{j},0}^{(m)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,-d_{j}})\left(={{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(\mathcal{F},\nabla\otimes\nabla_{\oslash,-d_{j}})\right).

If
e_{j}
denotes the image of
1\in\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,0}
via the inclusion into the
j
-th factor
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,0}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\oslash,0}^{\oplus n}
,
then its image
\zeta_{0,j}(e_{j})
is a horizontal section in the codomain of
\zeta_{0,j}
.
By the property
(\beta)_{m}
asserted in Proposition
2.1.1
, (i) (or Corollary
2.1.3
), we can find a lifting of
\zeta_{0,j}(e_{j})
in
\mathcal{F}
that is horizontal with respect to
\nabla\otimes\nabla_{\oslash,-d_{j}}^{(0)}
; this section
corresponds to a morphism of flat bundles

\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,0}\rightarrow(\mathcal{F},\nabla\otimes\nabla_{\oslash,-d_{j}}^{(0)})
.
The tensor produce of this morphism and the identity morphism of

\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d_{j}}
determines
a morphism

\zeta_{j}:\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d_{j}}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}
.
Thus, we obtain a morphism of flat bundles

(251)

\displaystyle\zeta:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\zeta_{j}:\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d_{j}}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}.

Since the reduction of
\zeta
modulo
p
coincides with the isomorphism
\zeta_{0}
(after reducing the level to
0
),
it follows from Nakayama’s lemma that
\zeta
is an isomorphism.
This completes the proof of the former assertion.
The latter assertion can be proved immediately from Proposition
3.5.3
, (ii). ∎

4.
Dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on pointed stable curves

This section deals with higher-level generalizations of (dormant)
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers, which we call (dormant)
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers.
The goal of this section is to show that the moduli category of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers may be represented by a proper Deligne-Mumford stack (cf. Theorem
4.5.3
and Corollary
4.6.2
).

Throughout this section, we shall fix
an element
(l,N)
of
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
l=0
or
N=1
.
Also, denote by
B
the Borel subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
(
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
)
defined as the image, via the natural projection
\mathrm{GL}_{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
,
of the group of invertible upper-triangular
n\times n
matrices.

4.1.
Moduli space of pointed stable curves

Let
(g,r)
be a pair of nonnegative integers with
2g-2+r>0
.
For each commutative ring
R
,
denote by

(252)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,R}\ \left(\text{or simply},\ \overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}\right)

the moduli stack classifying
r
-pointed stable curves of genus
g
over
R
.
Also, denote by
\mathcal{M}_{g,r,R}
the dense open substack of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,R}
classifying nonsingular curves.
The normal crossing divisor defined as the boundary
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,R}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{g,r,R}
on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,R}
determines a log structure;
we shall denote the resulting fs log stack by

(253)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,R}^{\mathrm{log}}\ \left(\text{or simply},\ \overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}^{\mathrm{log}}\right).

Next, let
\mathscr{X}:=(f:X\rightarrow S,\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r})
be an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
over a scheme
S
, where
\sigma_{i}
denotes the
i
-th marked point
S\rightarrow X
.
Recall from
[
FKa
, Theorem 2.6]
that
there exists canonical log structures on
X
and
S
; we shall denote the resulting log schemes by
X^{\mathrm{log}}
and
S^{\mathrm{log}}
, respectively.
(The log structure of
S^{\mathrm{log}}
is obtained as the pull-back from
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}^{\mathrm{log}}
via the classifying morphism
S\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
of
\mathscr{X}
.)

4.2.
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on log curves

Let
S^{\mathrm{log}}
be an fs log scheme whose underlying scheme
S
is
flat over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
, and let

f^{\mathrm{log}}:X^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{log}}
be a log curve over
S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Suppose that
S
is equipped with an
(N-1)
-PD structure that extends to
X
via
f
.
For simplicity, we write
\Omega:=\Omega_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
,
\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
,
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}:=\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
, and
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq j}:=\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\leq j}
(
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
).
In this subsection, we shall
fix an integer
n
with

1<n<p
.

Let us consider a pair

(254)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)

consisting of a
B
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{B}
on
X
and an
(N-1)
-PD stratification on

\mathcal{E}
, where
\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{E}_{B}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{E}
is
the
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle induced from
\mathcal{E}_{B}
via change of structure group by the natural inclusion
B\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
.
Denote by
\nabla_{\phi}
the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on
\mathcal{E}
corresponding, via
the equivalence of categories (
77
), to the
0
-PD stratification induced from
\phi
.

Definition 4.2.1
.

(i)

We shall say that
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
(or a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper of level
N
) on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
if the pair
(\mathcal{E}_{B},\nabla_{\phi})
forms an
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{l}_{n}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
in the sense of
[
Wak6
, Definition 2.1]
.
(Note that the notion of an
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{l}_{n}
-oper can be defined in exactly the same way as
[
Wak6
]
, even though we are working over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
In particular, a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(1)}
-oper
can be formulated in terms of flat connections,
i.e., without fixing a (
0
-)PD structure on
S
.)
If the log curve
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
arises from a pointed stable curve
\mathscr{X}
,
then we will refer to any
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
as a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Also, for simplicity, we shall refer to any
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(1)}
-oper as a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper
.

(ii)

Let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\circ}:=(\mathcal{E}_{\circ,B},\phi_{\circ})

and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\bullet}:=(\mathcal{E}_{\bullet,B},\phi_{\bullet})

be
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Write
\mathcal{E}_{\circ}:=\mathcal{E}_{\circ,B}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
and

\mathcal{E}_{\bullet}:=\mathcal{E}_{\bullet,B}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
.
Then, an
isomorphism of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers
from
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\circ}
to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\bullet}
is defined as an isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{\circ,B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}_{\bullet,B}
of
B
-bundles that defines, via change of structure group by
B\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
, an isomorphism of
(N-1)
-PD stratified
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundles
(\mathcal{E}_{\circ},\phi_{\circ})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{E}_{\bullet},\phi_{\bullet})
.

Moreover, we shall define the notion of a
dormant

\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper, generalizing
[
Moc2
, Chap. II, Definition 1.1]
,
[
Wak6
, Definition 3.15]
, and
[
Wak7
, Definition 4.1.1]
.

Definition 4.2.2
.

Let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)
be a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.

(i)

Suppose that
l=0
, i.e.,
S
is a scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Then, we shall say that
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is
dormant
if
\phi
has vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature.

(ii)

Suppose that
N=1
.
Then, we shall say that
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is
dormant
if the
induced flat
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle
is dormant in the sense of Definition
2.1.4
, (ii).

Remark 4.2.3

(
G
-oper of higher level)
.

In a similar manner to the above definition,
we can extend the definition of a
G
-oper (discussed in
[
Wak6
, Definition 2.1]
) to higher level at least when
G
is a semisimple algebraic group of adjoint type.

Let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)
be a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Suppose that
we are given an fs log scheme
S^{\prime\mathrm{log}}
flat over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
and a morphism of log schemes
s^{\mathrm{log}}:S^{\prime\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{log}}
over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
Then, the pair of base-changes

(255)

\displaystyle s^{*}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}):=(s^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{B}),s^{*}(\phi))

forms a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on the log curve
(S^{\prime\mathrm{log}}\times_{S^{\mathrm{log}}}X^{\mathrm{log}})/S^{\prime\mathrm{log}}
.
Conversely, the formation of a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper has descent with respect to, e.g., the étale topology on
S
.

Let us prove the following fundamental property of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers, which is a generalization of
[
BeDr2
, § 1.3, Proposition]
and
[
Wak6
, Proposition 2.9]
(in the case of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers).

Proposition 4.2.4
.

Any
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
does not have nontrivial automorphisms.

Proof.

We prove the assertion by induction on
l
.
The base step, i.e., the case of
l=0
, follows from

[
Wak6
, Proposition 2.9]
.

Next, to prove the induction step,
we suppose that the assertion with
l
replaced by
l-1
(
l\geq 1
)
has been proved.
We may assume, without loss of generality, that
S
is affine.
Let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)
be a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
and
h:\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
an automorphism of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.
By the induction hypothesis, the reduction of
h
modulo
p^{l}
is equal to the identity morphism.
Denote by
h^{(1)}
the automorphism of the
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper
(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})^{(1)}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\nabla_{\phi})
induced by
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.
According to an argument similar to the argument in
[
Wak6
, § 6.3]
(which deals with the case where the base space
S^{\mathrm{log}}
is a log scheme over a field),

h^{(1)}
coincides with the identify morphism.
Indeed, the space of automorphisms of
(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})^{(1)}
inducing the identity morphism via reduction modulo
p^{l}
has a structure of torsor modeled on

H^{0}(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}))
, where “
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}
” denotes the morphism defined in
[
Wak6
, Eq. (759)]
(for
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{l}_{n}
) associated to
the reduction modulo
p
of
(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})^{(1)}
;
but the equality
H^{0}(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}))=0
holds by
[
Wak6
, Proposition 6.5]
, which implies

h=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}
.
This proves the induction step, and hence we have finished the proof of the assertion.
∎

We shall set
R:=\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
(
l\geq 0
).
Denote by
\mathcal{S}et
the category of (small) sets and by
\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S
the category of flat
R
-schemes
S^{\prime}
equipped with an
R
-morphism
S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
.
We shall
denote by

(256)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\right):\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S\rightarrow\mathcal{S}et

the contravariant functor on
\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S
, which to any
S
-scheme
S^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S)
, assigns the set of isomorphism classes of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers (resp., dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers) on the log curve
(S^{\prime}\times_{S}X^{\mathrm{log}})/(S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\mathrm{log}})
.
By Proposition
4.2.4
above,

\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
)
turns out to be a sheaf with respect to the big étale topology on
\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S
.

4.3.
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on log curves

Next, we shall define the notion of a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper and define a certain equivalence relation in the set of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers.
Fix an integer
n
with

1<n\leq p^{N}
.

Let us consider a collection of data

(257)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j=0}^{n}),

where

•

\mathcal{F}
is a vector bundle on
X
of rank
n
;

•

\nabla
is a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}
;

•

\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j=0}^{n}
is an
n
-step decreasing filtration

(258)

\displaystyle 0=\mathcal{F}^{n}\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{n-1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{0}=\mathcal{F}

on
\mathcal{F}
consisting of subbundles.

Definition 4.3.1
.

(i)

We shall say that
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
is a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
(or a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper of level
N
) on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}

if, for every
j=0,\cdots,n-1
,
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
induced by
\nabla
restricts to an isomorphism

(259)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\leq n-j-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{n-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{F}^{j}.

When
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
arises from a pointed stable curve
\mathscr{X}
, we shall refer to any
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
as a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
(When
N=1
, this definition is the same as the usual definition of a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper, as defined in, e.g.,
[
Wak6
, Definition 4.17]
.)

(ii)

Let
\mathscr{F}_{\circ}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathscr{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ},\{\mathcal{F}_{\circ}^{j}\}_{j})
and
\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathscr{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet},\{\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{j}\}_{j})

be
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
An
isomorphism of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers

from
\mathscr{F}_{\circ}^{\heartsuit}
to
\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}^{\heartsuit}

is an isomorphism
(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet})
of
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-modules preserving the filtration.

Definition 4.3.2
.

Let
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j})
be a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.

(i)

Suppose that
l=0
, i.e.,
S
is a scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Then, we shall say that
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
is
dormant
if
\nabla
has vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature.

(ii)

Suppose that
N=1
.
Then, we shall say that
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
is
dormant

if the flat bundle
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
is dormant in the sense of Definition
2.1.4
, (i).

Example 4.3.3

(
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers for large
N
’s)
.

Despite the fact that
dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(1)}
-opers have been substantially investigated in many references, there are few examples
for higher level
at the time of writing the present paper.
We here give an example of a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper (for a general
N
) constructed recently in
[
Wak8
]
.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
X
a curve embedded in the projective plane
\mathbb{P}^{2}:=\{[t_{0}:t_{1}:t_{2}]\,|\,(t_{0},t_{1},t_{2})\neq(0,0,0)\}
over
k
.
Denote by
\mathrm{Grass}(2,3)
the Grassman variety classifying
2
-dimensional quotient spaces of the
k
-vector space
k^{3}
; it may be identified with the space of
1
-planes in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
.
Recall that the
Gauss map
on
X
is the rational morphism
\gamma:X\dashrightarrow\mathrm{Grass}(2,3)

that assigns to each smooth point
x
the embedded tangent space to
X
at
x
in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Now, suppose that
p>2
and that

X
is the Fermat hypersurface of degree
p^{N}+1
in

\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
i.e., the smooth hypersurface defined by the homogenous polynomial
t_{0}^{p^{N}+1}+t_{1}^{p^{N}+1}+t_{2}^{p^{N}+1}
.
It is well-known that the Gauss map on
X
factors through the
N
-th relative Frobenius morphism
F_{X/k}^{(N)}
.
In particular, we obtain a rank
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}^{\nabla}
on
X^{(N)}
by
pulling-back the universal quotient bundle on
\mathrm{Grass}(2,3)
via the resulting morphism
X^{(N)}\rightarrow\mathrm{Grass}(2,3)
.
If

\{F^{(N)*}_{X/k}(\mathcal{F}^{\nabla})^{j}\}_{j}
denotes the Harder-Narasimhan filtration on

F^{(N)*}_{X/k}(\mathcal{F}^{\nabla})
, then it forms a
2
-step (decreasing) filtration.
Moreover, it follows from
[
Wak8
, Theorem C]
that the collection of data

(260)

\displaystyle(F^{(N)*}_{X/k}(\mathcal{F}^{\nabla}),\nabla^{(N-1)}_{\mathcal{F}^{\nabla},\mathrm{can}},\{F^{(N)*}_{X/k}(\mathcal{F}^{\nabla})^{j}\}_{j})

(cf. (
94
) for the definition of
\nabla_{(-),\mathrm{can}}^{(m)}
) defines a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
X/k
.

Proposition 4.3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(N-1)},\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j})
be a (dormant)
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Also, let
N^{\prime}
be a positive integer with
n<p^{N^{\prime}}
.
The collection

(261)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(N-1)\Rightarrow(N^{\prime}-1)},\{\mathcal{F}_{j}\}_{j})

forms a (dormant)
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N^{\prime})}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the fact that the natural morphism
\mathcal{D}_{\leq n-j-1}^{(N^{\prime}-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq n-j-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}
is an isomorphism for every
j=0,\cdots,n-1
.
∎

Next, we shall define an equivalence relation in the set of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers.
Let
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j})
be
a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
and

\mathscr{L}:=(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})
an invertible
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module.
In particular, we obtain a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla\otimes\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}
on the tensor product
\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}

arising from
\nabla
and
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}
.
One may verify that the collection

(262)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\otimes\mathscr{L}}:=(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L},\nabla\otimes\nabla_{\mathcal{L}},\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\otimes\mathcal{L}\}_{j=0}^{n})

forms
a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
If
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
and
(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})
are dormant, then
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\otimes\mathscr{L}}
is verified to be dormant.

Definition 4.3.5
.

Let
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\circ}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ},\{\mathcal{F}_{\circ}^{j}\}_{j})

and
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\bullet}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet},\{\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{j}\}_{j})

be
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers (resp., dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers) on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
We shall say that

\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\circ}
is
equivalent to
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\bullet}

if
there exists an invertible (resp., a dormant invertible)
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module
\mathscr{L}:=(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})

such that
the
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\circ,\otimes\mathscr{L}}

is isomorphic to

\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}^{\heartsuit}
.
We use the notion “
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\circ}\sim\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\bullet}
”
to indicate the situation that
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\circ}
is equivalent to
\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}^{\heartsuit}
.
(The binary relation “
\sim
” on the set of (dormant)
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
in fact defines an equivalence relation.)
Moreover, for a (dormant)
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
, we shall write
[\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}]
for the equivalence class represented by
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
.

Let
s:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
be an
S
-scheme classified by
\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S
.
The base-change
s^{*}(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit})
of
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
along
s

can be constructed, and the formulation of base-changes preserves the equivalence relation “
\sim
”.
Hence, we obtain the
contravariant functor

(263)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\right):\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S\rightarrow\mathcal{S}et

on
\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S
defined as the sheaf (with respect to the étale topology) associated to
the functor
which, to any
S
-scheme
S^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S)
,
assigns the set of equivalence classes of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers (resp., dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers) on
(S^{\prime}\times_{S}X^{\mathrm{log}})/(S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\mathrm{log}})
.

Proposition 4.3.6
.

Suppose that
p\nmid n
.
Then, the natural morphism of functors

\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}
is injective.
(In the subsequent discussion, we shall regard
\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!
as a subfunctor of
\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}
by using this injection.)

Proof.

Suppose that two dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\circ}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\circ},\nabla_{\circ},\{\mathcal{F}_{\circ}^{j}\}_{j})
and
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\bullet}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\nabla_{\bullet},\{\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{j}\}_{j})

are equivalent in the set of (
non-dormant
)
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
This means that there exists an invertible
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module
\mathscr{L}:=(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})
with
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\circ,\otimes\mathscr{L}}\cong\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\bullet}
.
By taking determinants, we obtain

(264)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}_{\circ}),\mathrm{det}(\nabla_{\circ}))\otimes(\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes n})\cong(\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}),\mathrm{det}(\nabla_{\bullet})).

Since both
\mathrm{det}(\nabla_{\circ})
and
\mathrm{det}(\nabla_{\bullet})
are dormant,
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes n}
is dormant by (
264
).
It follows from Proposition
2.3.1
, (ii), that
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}
turns out to be dormant.
That is to say,
\mathscr{F}_{\circ}^{\heartsuit}
is equivalent to
\mathscr{F}_{\bullet}^{\heartsuit}
in the set of
dormant

\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers.
∎

Suppose further that
n<p
.
Let
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j})
be a

\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper (resp., a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper) on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{F}}

the
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle associated to the vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
via a change of structure group by the projection
\mathrm{GL}_{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
.
The filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j}
determines a
B
-reduction
\mathcal{E}_{\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j}}
of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{F}}
.
Also,
\nabla
induces an
(N-1)
-PD stratification
\phi_{\nabla}
on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{F}}
(cf. Remark
1.3.3
).
The resulting pair

(265)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j}},\phi_{\nabla})

forms a

\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper (resp., a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper) on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
(cf.
[
Wak6
, § 4.4.6]
).
The isomorphism class of
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
depends only on the equivalence class
[\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}]
of
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
.
Hence,
the assignment

[\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}]\mapsto\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
determines a well-defined morphism of functors

(266)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}.\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \Lambda_{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}:\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\right).

4.4.
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristics

We introduce
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristics, generalizing
n
-theta characteristic defined in
[
Wak6
, Definition 4.31]
.
Let
n
be an integer
>1
.

Definition 4.4.1
.

An
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
(or an
n
-theta characteristic of level
N
) of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is a pair

(267)

\displaystyle\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta}),

consisting of a line bundle
\varTheta
on
X
and a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\vartheta}
on the line bundle
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n}
.
Also, we shall say that an
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
is
dormant
if
(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n},\nabla_{\vartheta})
is dormant.

Example 4.4.2

(
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristics arising from theta characteristics)
.

Recall that
a
theta characteristic
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is a line bundle
\varTheta_{0}
on
X
together with an isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}\otimes\varTheta_{0}^{\otimes 2}
.

Now, let
\varTheta_{0}
be a theta characteristic of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Fix an isomorphism
\tau_{\varTheta_{0}}:\mathcal{O}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}\otimes\varTheta_{0}^{\otimes 2}
.
Then, the line bundle
\varTheta_{0}
together with the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{T}\otimes\varTheta_{0}^{\otimes 2}
corresponding to
\nabla_{X,\mathrm{triv}}^{(N-1)}
via
\tau_{\varTheta_{0}}
specifies a
2^{(N)}
-theta characteristic.

More generally, by setting
\varTheta:=\varTheta_{0}^{\otimes(n-1)}
, the line bundle
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n}
admits a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\vartheta_{0}}
corresponding to
\nabla^{(N-1)}_{X,\mathrm{triv}}
via the composite isomorphism

(268)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{\tau_{\varTheta_{0}}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}}(\mathcal{T}\otimes\varTheta_{0}^{\otimes 2})^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes(\varTheta^{\otimes(n-1)}_{0})^{\otimes n}\left(=\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n}\right).

Thus, the resulting pair

(269)

\displaystyle\vartheta_{0}:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta_{0}})

forms an
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Example 4.34]
for the case of
(l,N)=(0,1)
).
Moreover, this
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic is verified to be dormant.

Note that if, for example,
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
arises from a pointed stable curve, then the resulting
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic does not depend on the choice of the isomorphism
\tau_{\varTheta}
; this is because
any automorphism of a line bundle on
X
is given by multiplication by an element of
\Gamma(S,\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\times})\left(=\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})\right)
, which is therefore compatible with any
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-action.

The following assertion is a higher-level generalization of the fact proved in
[
Wak6
, § 4.6.4]
.

Proposition 4.4.3
.

Suppose that
p\nmid n
.
Then, there always exists a dormant
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.

Proof.

Since
(p^{l+N},n)=1
,
one may find a pair of integers
(a,b)
with
a\cdot n+b\cdot p^{N+l}=\frac{n(n-1)}{2}
.
By letting
\varTheta_{a,b}:=\Omega^{\otimes a}
, we have a composite isomorphism

(270)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta_{a,b}^{\otimes n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\left(\frac{n(n-1)}{2}-a\cdot n\right)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes b\cdot p^{N+l}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes b})^{\otimes p^{N+l}}.

Let us take an open covering
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
of
X
together with a collection
\{\tau_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
, where each
\tau_{\alpha}
denotes a trivialization
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes b}|_{U_{\alpha}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{U_{\alpha}}
of
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes b}
over
U_{\alpha}
.
For each
\alpha\in I
, denote by
\nabla_{\alpha}
the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-module structure on
(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes b})^{\otimes p^{N+l-1}}|_{U_{\alpha}}
corresponding to

\nabla_{U_{\alpha},\mathrm{triv}}^{(N-1)}

via
the isomorphism

(271)

\displaystyle\breve{\tau}_{\alpha}:=\tau_{\alpha}^{\otimes p^{N+l-1}}:(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes b})^{\otimes p^{N+l-1}}|_{U_{\alpha}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\left(\mathcal{O}_{U_{\alpha}}^{\otimes p^{N+l-1}}=\right)\mathcal{O}_{U_{\alpha}}.

For each pair
(\alpha,\beta)\in I\times I
with
U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
,
the automorphism
\breve{\tau}_{\alpha}\circ\breve{\tau}_{\beta}^{-1}
of
\mathcal{O}_{U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}}
is given by multiplication by a section of the form
u^{p^{N+l-1}}
for some
u\in\Gamma(U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta},\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})
.
By the local description of
\nabla_{(-),\mathrm{triv}}^{(N-1)}
displayed in (
42
),
this automorphism is verified to preserve the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}|_{U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}}
-module structure.
It follows that the collection
\{\nabla_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
may be glued together to obtain a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes b})^{\otimes p^{N+l}}
;
if
\nabla_{a,b}
denotes the corresponding
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1}{2}}\otimes\varTheta_{a,b}^{\otimes n}
via (
270
),
then the resulting pair
(\varTheta_{a,b},\nabla_{a,b})
specifies a required
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic.
∎

For an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{F}
,
we assume that the tensor product
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}:={{}^{R}\mathcal{D}}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}
(resp.,
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq j}\otimes\mathcal{F}:={{}^{R}\mathcal{D}}^{(N-1)}_{\leq j}\otimes\mathcal{F}
for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
)
is always endowed with
an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure arising from

{{}^{L}\mathcal{D}}^{(N-1)}
(resp.,
{{}^{R}\mathcal{D}}^{(N-1)}
).

Let us fix an
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
, and set

(272)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}:=\mathcal{D}_{\leq n-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta,\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j}:=\mathcal{D}_{\leq n-j-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\ \ (j=0,\cdots,n).

Since
\mathcal{F}^{j}_{\varTheta}/\mathcal{F}^{j+1}_{\varTheta}
(
j=0,\cdots,n-1
) may be identified with
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes(n-j-1)}\otimes\varTheta
,
we obtain the composite isomorphism between line bundles

(273)

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\bigotimes_{j=0}^{n-1}\mathcal{F}^{j}_{\varTheta}/\mathcal{F}^{j+1}_{\varTheta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\bigotimes_{j=0}^{n-1}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes(n-j-1)}\otimes\varTheta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n}.

Definition 4.4.4
.

(i)

A
(dormant)
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is
a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}

such that the collection of data

(274)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\nabla^{\diamondsuit},\{\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j}\}_{j=0}^{n})

forms a (dormant)
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
and
the equality
\mathrm{det}(\nabla)=\nabla_{\vartheta}
holds
under the identification
\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})=\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n}
given by (
273
).
When
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
arises from a pointed stable curve
\mathscr{X}
, we will refer to any (dormant)
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
as a
(dormant)
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.

(ii)

Let
\nabla_{\circ}^{\diamondsuit}

and
\nabla_{\bullet}^{\diamondsuit}

be (dormant)
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-opers on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
We shall say that
\nabla_{\circ}^{\diamondsuit}
is
isomorphic to
\nabla_{\bullet}^{\diamondsuit}

if the associated
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers
\nabla_{\circ}^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
and
\nabla_{\bullet}^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
are isomorphic.

We shall prove the following two propositions concerning
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-opers.

Proposition 4.4.5
.

Let
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j})
be a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.

(i)

There exists a triple

(275)

\displaystyle(\vartheta_{0},\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit},h_{0})

consisting of
an
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta_{0}:=(\varTheta_{0},\nabla_{\vartheta_{0}})
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
,
a
(\mathrm{GL}_{n},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit}
, and
an isomorphism of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-opers
h_{0}:\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
.
(Such an
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic is uniquely determined up to isomorphism in a certain sense.)

(ii)

Suppose that
p\nmid n
.
Then, there exists pair

(276)

\displaystyle(\nabla^{\diamondsuit},\mathscr{L})

consisting of
a
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
and an invertible
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module
\mathscr{L}
with

\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\otimes\mathscr{L}}\cong\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
.
If, moreover, both
\vartheta
and
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
are dormant,
we can choose such
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
and
\mathscr{L}
as being dormant.

Proof.

First, we shall prove assertion (i).
Let us set
\varTheta_{0}:=\mathcal{F}^{n-1}
.
Consider the composite

(277)

\displaystyle h_{0}:\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{0}}\left(=\mathcal{D}_{\leq n-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta_{0}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathcal{F},

where the first arrow arises from the inclusions
\mathcal{D}_{\leq n-1}^{(N-1)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
and
\varTheta_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
.
Since
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
is a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper, this composite turns out to be an isomorphism.
Denote by
\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit}
(resp.,
\nabla_{\vartheta_{0}}
) the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{0}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta_{0}^{\otimes n}\left(=\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{0}})\right)
) corresponding to
\nabla
(resp.,
\mathrm{det}(\nabla)
) via the isomorphism
h_{0}
(resp., the isomorphism
\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{0}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F})
induced by
h_{0}
).
Then, the resulting collection
(\vartheta_{0},\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit},h_{0})
, where
\vartheta_{0}:=(\varTheta_{0},\nabla_{\vartheta_{0}})
, determines the desired triple.

Next, we shall prove assertion (ii).
Write
\mathcal{L}:=\varTheta\otimes\mathcal{F}^{n-1\vee}
, and consider
the composite of isomorphisms

(278)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n})\otimes\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F})^{\vee}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n})\otimes(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes(\mathcal{F}^{n-1})^{\otimes n})^{\vee}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}})^{\vee}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n}\otimes(\mathcal{F}^{n-1\vee})^{\otimes n}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\left((\varTheta\otimes\mathcal{F}^{n-1\vee})^{\otimes n}=\right)\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n},

where the first arrow is the morphism induced by
the isomorphism

\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes(\mathcal{F}^{n-1})^{\otimes n}
in
[
Wak6
, Eq. (501)]
(applied to the
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper determined by
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
).
It follows from Proposition
2.3.1
, (i), that there exists a unique
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}
on
\mathcal{L}
whose
n
-th tensor product corresponds to
\nabla_{\vartheta}\otimes\mathrm{det}(\nabla)^{\vee}
via (
278
).
Let us consider the composite

(279)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\left(=\mathcal{D}_{\leq n-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{D}_{\leq n-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{n-1})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\nabla}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{F}.

Since
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
is a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper,
this composite turns out to be an isomorphism.
Moreover, the equality
\nabla_{\vartheta}=\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{F})
holds under the identification

\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n}=\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{F})
given by (
273
) and
the determinant of (
279
).
Hence, if
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
denotes the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
corresponding to
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\nabla
via (
279
),
then the pair
(\nabla^{\diamondsuit},\mathscr{L})
, where
\mathscr{L}:=(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})
,
defines the desired pair.
Also, the latter assertion follows from Proposition
2.3.2
, (ii).
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

Proposition 4.4.6
.

Suppose that
p\nmid n
and
l=0
.
Let
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\circ}
and
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\bullet}

be
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-opers on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Write
\vartheta^{(1)}
for the
n^{(1)}
-theta characteristic obtained by reducing the level of
\vartheta
to
1
.
Also, for each
\star=\circ,\bullet
,
denote by
\nabla^{(1)\diamondsuit}_{\star}
the
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(1)},\vartheta^{(1)})
-oper induced by
\nabla_{\star}^{\diamondsuit}
.
Suppose that

\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\circ}
is isomorphic to
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\bullet}
and
the equality
\nabla^{(1)\diamondsuit}_{\circ}=\nabla^{(1)\diamondsuit}_{\bullet}
holds.
Then, we have
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\circ}=\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\bullet}
.

Proof.

Let us take an isomorphism of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers

h:\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}_{\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\nabla_{\bullet}^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
.
By the assumption
\nabla^{(1)\diamondsuit}_{\circ}=\nabla^{(1)\diamondsuit}_{\bullet}
,
the isomorphism of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(1)}
-opers

h^{(1)}:\nabla^{(1)\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}_{\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\nabla_{\bullet}^{(1)\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}

induced by
h
defines an automorphism of
\nabla_{\circ}^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
.
According to
[
Wak6
, Proposition 4.25]
,

h
coincides with the automorphism
\mathrm{mult}_{a}:\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
given by multiplication by an element
a
of
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})
.
The multiplication by
a^{n}
defines an isomorphism

(\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}),\mathrm{det}(\nabla_{\circ}^{\diamondsuit}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}),\mathrm{det}(\nabla_{\bullet}^{\diamondsuit}))
; this
may be regarded as an automorphism of
(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n},\nabla_{\vartheta})
via (
273
).
Hence, we have
a^{n}\in\Gamma(X,F_{X/S}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N)}}))
.
This implies
a\in\Gamma(X,F_{X/S}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N)}}))
because of the assumption
p\nmid n
.
Since

\nabla_{\circ}^{\diamondsuit}
is the unique
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
compatible with
\nabla_{\circ}^{\diamondsuit}
itself via
\mathrm{mult}_{a}\left(=h\right)
,
the equality
\nabla_{\circ}^{\diamondsuit}=\nabla_{\bullet}^{\diamondsuit}
must be satisfied.
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

4.5.
Representability of the moduli space

For any morphism
s:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
between flat
R
-schemes, the pair of base-changes

(280)

\displaystyle s^{*}(\vartheta)\ \left(\text{or}\ \vartheta_{S^{\prime}}\right):=(s^{*}(\varTheta),s^{*}(\nabla_{\vartheta}))

specifies an
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic of the log curve
(S^{\prime}\times_{S}X^{\mathrm{log}})/(S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\mathrm{log}})
.
Hence, we obtain the
\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}
-valued contravariant functor

(281)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\right):\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S\rightarrow\mathcal{S}et

on
\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S
which, to any
S
-scheme
s:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
in
\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S)
, assigns the set of isomorphism classes of
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},s^{*}(\vartheta))
-opers (resp., dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},s^{*}(\vartheta))
-opers) on
(S^{\prime}\times_{S}X^{\mathrm{log}})/(S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\mathrm{log}})
.

Suppose that
(1<)n<p
, and
let
\star
denote either the absence or presence of “
\mathrm{Zzz...}
”.
Then, the assignments
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}\mapsto(\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit})^{\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
and
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}\mapsto\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
define morphisms of functors

(282)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit,\vartheta}^{\star}:\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{\star}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{\star}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit,\vartheta}^{\star}:\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{\star}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}^{\star},

respectively.
These morphisms make the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit,\vartheta}^{\star}}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit,\vartheta}^{\star}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}^{\star}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{\star}.}

Moreover, we can obtain the following assertion.

Theorem 4.5.1
.

Suppose that
n<p
, and
let
\star
denote either the absence or presence of “
\mathrm{Zzz...}
”.
In the case of
\star=\mathrm{Zzz...}
,
we assume that the fixed
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta
is dormant.
Then, the morphisms of functors

\Lambda_{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}^{\star}
,

\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit,\vartheta}^{\star}
, and

\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit,\vartheta}^{\star}
are all isomorphisms.

Proof.

The assertion for
\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit,\vartheta}^{\star}

follows immediately from Proposition
2.3.2
, (i) and (ii).
The surjectivity of
\Lambda^{\star}_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit,\vartheta}
is a direct consequence of Proposition
4.4.5
, (ii).
The injectivity of
\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit,\vartheta}^{\star}
can be proved by an argument entirely similar to the argument in the injectivity of “
\Lambda_{\vartheta}^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
” discussed in
[
Wak6
, Proposition 4.40]
.
Moreover, these results together with the commutativity of (
4.5
) deduce the remaining portion, i.e.,
\Lambda_{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}^{\star}
turns out to be an isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 4.5.2
.

Suppose that
n<p
.

(i)

For any (dormant)
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
,
there always exists a (dormant)
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
with
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}\cong\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.

(ii)

Suppose further that the relative characteristic
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
is trivial.
Let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)
be a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
, and write

\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{E}_{B}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
.
Then,
(\mathcal{E},\phi)
is, Zariski locally on
X
, isomorphic to the trivial
(N-1)
-PD stratified
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle
(X\times\mathrm{PGL}_{n},\phi_{\mathrm{triv}})
(cf. Example
1.3.2
).

Proof.

Assertion (i) follows from Proposition
4.4.3
and the surjectivity of
\Lambda^{{}^{(\mathrm{Zzz...})}}_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit,\vartheta}
.
Assertion (ii) follows from assertion (i) and the equivalence (a)
\Leftrightarrow
(b) obtained in Proposition
2.2.4
, (ii).
∎

By applying Theorem
4.5.1
above, we can prove the representability of the functors
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}
and
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
for
l=1
, as follows.

Theorem 4.5.3
.

Let
(g,r)
be a pair of nonnegative integers with
2g-2+r>0
.
Suppose that
n<p
and that
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
arises from an
r
-pointed stable curve
of genus
g
over an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-scheme
S
.
Then, both
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}
and
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
may be represented by (possibly empty) affine schemes of finite type over
S
, and the natural inclusion

\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}
defines a closed immersion between
S
-schemes.
(In the rest of the present paper, we will use the notations
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}
and
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

for writing the schemes representing the functors
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}
and
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
, respectively.)

Proof.

Let us choose a (dormant)
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
(cf. Proposition
4.4.3
).
It induces an
n^{(1)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta^{(1)}
via reduction to level
1
.
