Recall from
[
Wak6
, Theorem 4.66]

that
T:=\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta^{(1)}}
(with “
N
” taken to be
1
) may be represented by an affine
S
-scheme.
Denote by

\mathscr{Y}:=(h:Y\rightarrow T,\{\sigma_{Y,i}\}_{i})
the pointed stable curves classified by the
S
-scheme
T
.
Also, for each
M\in\{0,N-1\}
, we shall set

(287)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{M}:=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{D}_{Y^{\mathrm{log}}/T^{\mathrm{log}},\leq p^{N}-1}^{(M)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{T}},\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{T}}),

where
\varTheta_{T}
denotes the base-change of
\varTheta
over
T
.
Note that
\mathcal{V}_{M}
can be equipped with a filtration induced by the filtrations on both
\mathcal{D}_{Y^{\mathrm{log}}/T^{\mathrm{log}},\leq p^{N}-1}^{(M)}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{T}}
whose
graded pieces are isomorphic to
\Omega_{Y^{\mathrm{log}}/T^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\otimes j}
(for some
j
’s).
Hence, the direct image
h_{*}(\mathcal{V}_{M})
is a vector bundle on
T
.
We shall define
\mathbb{V}(h_{*}(\mathcal{V}_{M}))
to be the relative affine space over
T
associated to
h_{*}(\mathcal{V}_{M})
.
The natural morphism
\mathcal{D}_{Y^{\mathrm{log}}/T^{\mathrm{log}},\leq p^{N}-1}^{(0)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{T}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{Y^{\mathrm{log}}/T^{\mathrm{log}},\leq p^{N}-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{T}}
induces an
S
-morphism

(288)

\displaystyle\nu:\mathbb{V}(h_{*}(\mathcal{V}_{N-1}))\rightarrow\mathbb{V}(h_{*}(\mathcal{V}_{0})).

The formation of this morphism commutes with base-change to
T
-schemes.

Next, the universal
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(1)},\vartheta^{(1)}_{T})
-oper on
\mathscr{Y}

determines an
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-linear morphism

(289)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\diamondsuit}:\mathcal{D}_{Y^{\mathrm{log}}/T^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(0)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{T}}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{T}}.

The restriction of
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
to
\mathcal{D}_{Y^{\mathrm{log}}/T^{\mathrm{log}},\leq p^{N}}^{(0)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{T}}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{Y^{\mathrm{log}}/T^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(0)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{T}}\right)
specifies a global section
\sigma
of

\mathbb{V}(h_{*}(\mathcal{V}_{0}))
.
Note that, for any
T
-scheme
T^{\prime}
, a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{Y^{\prime\mathrm{log}}/T^{\prime\mathrm{log}}}
-module structure (where
T^{\prime\mathrm{log}}:=T^{\prime}\times_{T}T^{\mathrm{log}}
and
Y^{\prime\mathrm{log}}:=T^{\prime}\times_{T}Y^{\mathrm{log}}
) on
a sheaf
is completely
determined by its restriction to
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{Y^{\prime\mathrm{log}}/T^{\prime\mathrm{log}},\leq p^{N}}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{Y^{\prime\mathrm{log}}/T^{\prime\mathrm{log}}}\right)
.
Hence, Proposition
4.4.6
implies
that the set of isomorphism classes of

(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta_{T^{\prime}})
-opers
on
Y^{\prime\mathrm{log}}/T^{\prime\mathrm{log}}
extending (the base-change to
T^{\prime}
of)
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}

may be identified with a subset of
\nu^{-1}(\sigma)(T^{\prime})
.
This identification enables us to consider

\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}

as
a closed subscheme of
\mathbb{V}(h_{*}(\mathcal{V}_{N-1}))
.
In particular,
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}
(
\cong\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}
by Theorem
4.5.1
) may be represented by an affine scheme over
T
, which is affine over
S
.
The assertions for
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
follows immediately from the fact just proved and the definition of
p^{N}
-curvature.
∎

Remark 4.5.4

(Representability for higher rank cases)
.

If the notion of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper can be defined for a large
n
,
then
it will be expected that Theorem
4.5.3
is true even when
p\leq n
.
Indeed, as proved in
[
Wak7
, Theorem B]
,
there exists a canonical duality
between dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers and dormant

\mathrm{PGL}_{p^{N}-n}^{(N)}
-opers.
(Although the discussion in
loc. cit.
only deals with the case where
X
is smooth, we can immediately extend the duality to pointed stable curves in the same manner.)
This result and Theorem
4.5.3
together imply the representability of
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
for
p^{N}-p<n<p^{N}-1
.

Denote by
\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}
the
category of flat
R
-schemes, where
R:=\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
Given a pair of nonnegative integers
(g,r)
with
2g-2+r>0
, we shall
denote by

(290)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r,R},\ \text{or simply}\ \mathcal{O}p_{g,r}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r,R}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},\ \text{or simply}\ \mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}\right)

the category over
\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}
defined as follows:

•

The objects are the pairs
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
, where
\mathscr{X}
denotes an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
over a flat
R
-scheme
S

and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
denotes
a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
(resp., a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper)
on
\mathscr{X}
;

•

The morphisms from
(\mathscr{X}_{\circ},\mathscr{E}_{\circ}^{\spadesuit})
to
(\mathscr{X}_{\bullet},\mathscr{E}_{\bullet}^{\spadesuit})
are morphisms
(\nu_{S},\nu_{X}):\mathscr{X}_{\circ}\rightarrow\mathscr{X}_{\bullet}
of
r
-pointed stable curves (cf.
[
Wak6
, Definition 1.36, (ii)]
) with
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\circ}\cong\nu_{S}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{\bullet}^{\spadesuit})
;

•

The projection
\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r,R}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}

(resp.,
\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r,R}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}
)
is given by assigning, to each pair
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
as above,
the base scheme
S
of
\mathscr{X}
.

By Proposition
4.2.4
,

\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
)
turns out to be fibered in equivalence relations, i.e., specifies a set-valued sheaf on
\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}
.
Forgetting the data of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers yields the projection

(291)

\displaystyle\Pi_{N,g,r,R}\ \left(\text{or simply},\ \Pi_{g,r}\right):\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}.

Also, we obtain
a projective system

(292)

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r,R}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{2,g,r,R}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,g,r,R}

each of whose morphisms is given by reducing the level of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers.

By applying Theorem
4.5.3
to various pointed stable curves,
we obtain the following assertion.

Theorem 4.5.5
.

Suppose that
n<p
and
R=\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Then, the fibered category
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}
may be represented by a (possibly empty) Deligne-Mumford stack of finite type over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and the natural projection
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
is (represented by schemes and) affine.
Moreover,

\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!
may be represented by a closed substack of
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}
.

Let us take a
pair
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
classified by
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,g,r,\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}}
, where
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)
.
Then, the pair

(293)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,0},{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\phi)

consisting of
the reduction
\mathcal{E}_{B,0}
modulo
p
of
\mathcal{E}_{B}
and the diagonal reduction
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\phi
of
\phi
(cf. (
144
)) specifies a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}_{0}
(= the reduction modulo
p
of
\mathscr{X}
).

Definition 4.5.6
.

We shall refer to
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
as the
diagonal reduction
of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.

The resulting assignment
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})\mapsto(\mathscr{X}_{0},{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
defines
a functor

(294)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-):\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,g,r,\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}.

Moreover, the square diagram of categories

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,g,r,\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-)}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}ch_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{flat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}ch_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\mathrm{flat}},}

is
1
-commutative,
where the lower horizontal arrow denotes the functor given by base-change along the closed immersion
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})
.

4.6.
Finiteness of the moduli space

Suppose that
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
arises from
an
r
-pointed stable curve
\mathscr{X}:=(f:X\rightarrow S,\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i})
of genus
g
over an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-scheme
S
.
Also, let us fix a dormant
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
(cf. Proposition
4.4.3
).
We equip
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta
with the left
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure given by left multiplication.
Write

(299)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}

for the quotient of
the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module

\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta
by the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-submodule generated by the image of
\psi_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\varTheta}:\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{N}}\otimes\varTheta\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta
(cf. (
90
) for the definition of
\psi_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
);
this
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}
will be denoted by
\nabla_{\varTheta}
.
By construction,
\nabla_{\varTheta}
has vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature.
For each
j=0,\cdots,p^{N}
, we shall set
\mathcal{P}^{j}_{\varTheta}
to be the subbundle of
\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}
defines as

(300)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{j}_{\varTheta}:=\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N}-j-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\xrightarrow{\mathrm{quotient}}\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}\right).

The natural composite
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}
is injective and restricts, for each
j=0,\cdots,n
, to an isomorphism

(301)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}^{p^{N}-n+j}.

In particular, this isomorphism for
j=0
gives
a short exact sequence

(302)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}/\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}^{p^{N}-n}\longrightarrow 0.

Also, by the definition of
\nabla_{\varTheta}
,
the collection of data

(303)

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}^{\heartsuit}_{\varTheta}:=(\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta},\nabla_{\varTheta},\{\mathcal{P}^{j}_{\varTheta}\}_{j=0}^{p^{N}})

forms a dormant

\mathrm{GL}_{p^{N}}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.

Next, let

\nabla^{\diamondsuit}

be a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
The composite

(304)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\left(=\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{n-1}\right)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\xrightarrow{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}}\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}

factors through the quotient
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}
because
it preserves the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure and

\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
has vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature.
Thus, this composite induces a morphism of
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-modules

(305)

\displaystyle\nu_{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}}:(\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta},\nabla_{\varTheta})\rightarrow(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\nabla^{\diamondsuit}).

This morphism restricts to
the identity morphism of
\varTheta\left(=\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}^{p^{N}-1}=\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{n-1}\right)
,
so it follows from the definition of a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper that
the restriction

(306)

\displaystyle\nu_{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}}|_{\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}^{p^{N}-n}}:\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}^{p^{N}-n}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\left(=\mathcal{F}^{0}_{\varTheta}\right)

of
\nu_{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}}
to
\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}^{p^{N}-n}

is an isomorphism.
In particular,
\nu_{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}}

specifies
a split surjection of (
302
).

By using the surjection
\nu_{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}}
, we prove the following assertion.
(When
N=1
and
\mathscr{X}
is an unpointed smooth curve over a single point, assertion (ii) can be found in
[
JoPa
, Corollary 6.1.6]
.)

Proposition 4.6.1
.

Let us keep the above situation.

(i)

The functor
\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is empty unless
n\leq p^{N}
.

(ii)

Suppose further that
n<p
.
Then, the scheme (representing the functor)
\mathcal{O}p_{\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

is
finite over
S
.
In particular, there are only finitely many isomorphism classes of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on
\mathscr{X}
.

Proof.

Assertion (i)
follows immediately from the fact that
the morphism
\nu_{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}}
defined
for each dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}

is surjective and the vector bundle
\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}
has rank
p^{N}
.

Next, let us consider assertion (ii).
Since we already know that
\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
may be represented by an affine scheme over
S
(cf. Theorems
4.5.1
and
4.5.3
),
the problem is reduced to proving the properness of
\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}/S
.
To this end, we apply the valuative criterion, so it suffices to consider the case of

S=\mathrm{Spec}(A)
, where
A
denotes a valuation ring for a field
K
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Denote by
\mathscr{X}_{K}:=(X_{K}/K,\{\sigma_{K,i}\}_{i})
the generic fiber of
\mathscr{X}
and by
\vartheta_{K}:=(\varTheta_{K},\nabla_{\varTheta_{K}})
the restriction of
\vartheta
to
X_{K}
.
Let us take an arbitrary dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta_{K})
-oper
\nabla_{K}^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathscr{X}_{K}
.
It follows from the discussion preceding this proposition that
\nabla_{K}^{\diamondsuit}

induces an
\mathcal{O}_{X_{K}}
-linear surjection
\nu_{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{K}}:(\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta_{K}},\nabla_{\varTheta_{K}})\twoheadrightarrow(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{K}},\nabla_{K}^{\diamondsuit})
.
By the properness of Quot schemes,
there exists a pair

(\mathcal{F},\nu)
consisting of
an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{F}
flat over
S

and
an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear surjection
\nu:\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{F}
with
(\mathcal{F},\nu)|_{X_{K}}=(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{K}},\nu_{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{K}})
.
The composite

(307)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\xrightarrow{\text{$(\ref{e790})$ for $j=0$}}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}^{p^{N}-n}\hookrightarrow\right)\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}\xrightarrow{\nu}\mathcal{F}

is an isomorphism because it becomes an isomorphism when restricted over
X_{K}
and
the special fiber of
\mathcal{F}
has the same degree and rank as
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{K}}\left(=\mathcal{F}|_{X_{K}}\right)
.
Under the identification
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}=\mathcal{F}
given by (
307
), the surjection
\nu
specifies a split surjection of
(
302
).
In particular, the kernel
\mathrm{Ker}(\nu)
of
\nu
is isomorphic to the vector bundle
\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}/\mathcal{P}_{\varTheta}^{p^{N}-n}
.
Since
\mathrm{Ker}(\nu)|_{X_{K}}\left(=\mathrm{Ker}(\nu_{\nabla_{K}^{\diamondsuit}})\right)
is closed under
\nabla_{\varTheta_{K}}
,

\mathrm{Ker}(\nu)
must be closed under
\nabla_{\varTheta}
.
Hence,
\nabla_{\varTheta}
induces, via
\nu
and (
307
), a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
with vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature.
It is immediately verified that

\nabla^{\diamondsuit}

forms a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
whose generic fiber coincides with
\nabla_{K}^{\diamondsuit}
.
Consequently, we have proved that an
K
-rational point of
\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
(classifying
\nabla_{K}^{\diamondsuit}
as above) extends to an
A
-rational point.
Thus,
\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}/S
turns out to be proper, and the proof of the assertion is completed.
∎

By applying the above result to various pointed stable curves, we obtain
the following theorem,
which is a higher-level generalization of
[
Wak6
, Theorem 3.38, (i)]

for
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers
(or a part of
[
Moc2
, Chap. II, Theorem 2.8]
).

Corollary 4.6.2
.

Suppose that
n<p
and
R=\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Then, the Deligne-Mumford stack
representing
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

is finite over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
(hence also proper over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
).
In particular, for every positive integer
N^{\prime}
with
1\leq N^{\prime}<N
, the morphism of stacks
\Pi_{N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}}:\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N^{\prime},g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}

obtained by reducing the level of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers to
N^{\prime}
is finite.

4.7.
Correspondence with differential equations

We here describe the bijective correspondence between
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-opers and ordinary linear differential operators.
As most of the arguments in this subsection are exactly the same as those made in
[
Wak6
, § 4]
,
the details of the proofs are omitted.
Hereinafter, we shall fix an integer
n
with
1<n\leq p^{N}
, and set

R:=\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
.

Let
S^{\mathrm{log}}
be an fs log scheme whose underlying scheme
S
is flat over
R
and
f^{\mathrm{log}}:X^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{log}}
a log curve over
S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Also, let
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{N}
be
line bundles on
X
.
By an
n
-th order (linear) differential operator
from
\mathcal{L}
to
\mathcal{N}
, we mean
an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{L}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}\otimes\mathcal{D}^{(0)}_{\leq n}
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Remark 4.12]
), where
\mathcal{N}\otimes\mathcal{D}^{(0)}_{\leq n}\left(=\mathcal{N}\otimes{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(0)}_{\leq n}\right)
is equipped with the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure arising from
{{}^{R}}\mathcal{D}^{(0)}_{\leq n}
.
For an
n
-th order differential operator
D
from
\mathcal{L}
to
\mathcal{N}
,
the composite

(308)

\displaystyle\Sigma(D):\mathcal{L}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}\otimes\mathcal{D}^{(0)}_{\leq n}\twoheadrightarrow\left(\mathcal{N}\otimes(\mathcal{D}^{(0)}_{\leq n}/\mathcal{D}^{(0)}_{\leq n-1})=\right)\mathcal{N}\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\otimes n}

is called the
principal symbol
of
D
.
In particular,
D
can have unit principal symbol (i.e.,
\Sigma(D)=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{L}}
) only when
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{L}\otimes\Omega^{\otimes n}
.

Now, we shall fix an
n^{(1)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Let
D^{\clubsuit}
be an
n
-th order differential operator
from
\varTheta^{\vee}
to
\Omega^{\otimes n}\otimes\varTheta^{\vee}
with unit principal symbol.
Denote by
D^{\!{{}^{\rotatebox{180.0}{{\tiny$\clubsuit$}}}}}
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes n}\otimes\varTheta\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq n}^{(0)}\otimes\varTheta
corresponding to
D^{\clubsuit}
via the composite of natural isomorphisms

(309)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}om(\varTheta^{\vee},(\Omega^{\otimes n}\otimes\varTheta^{\vee})\otimes\mathcal{D}^{(0)}_{\leq n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\Omega^{\otimes n}\otimes\varTheta^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{D}_{\leq n}^{(0)}\otimes\varTheta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes n}\otimes\varTheta,\mathcal{D}_{\leq n}^{(0)}\otimes\varTheta).

It induces a left
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}
-module

(\mathcal{D}^{(0)}\otimes\varTheta)/\langle\mathrm{Im}(D^{\!{{}^{\rotatebox{180.0}{{\tiny$\clubsuit$}}}}})\rangle
,
i.e., the quotient
of
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}\otimes\varTheta
by the
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}
-submodule generated by the image
of
D^{\!{{}^{\rotatebox{180.0}{{\tiny$\clubsuit$}}}}}
.
Since
\Sigma(D^{\clubsuit})=1
, the composite

(310)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(0)}\otimes\varTheta\xrightarrow{\mathrm{quotient}}\mathcal{D}^{(0)}\otimes\varTheta/\langle\mathrm{Im}(D^{\!{{}^{\rotatebox{180.0}{{\tiny$\clubsuit$}}}}})\rangle

is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.
The
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}
-action on
(\mathcal{D}^{(0)}\otimes\varTheta)/\langle\mathrm{Im}(D^{\!{{}^{\rotatebox{180.0}{{\tiny$\clubsuit$}}}}})\rangle
determines, via this composite isomorphism,
an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection

(311)

\displaystyle D^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}:\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\rightarrow\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}

on
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
.
The collection of data

(312)

\displaystyle D^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},D^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit},\{\mathcal{F}^{j}_{\varTheta}\}_{j=0}^{n})

forms a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.

Definition 4.7.1
.

We shall say that
D^{\clubsuit}
is an
(n,\vartheta)
-projective connection
on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
if
D^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
specifies a
(\mathrm{GL}_{n},\vartheta)
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.

We shall write

(313)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}i\!f\!f_{\clubsuit,\vartheta}:\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S\rightarrow\mathcal{S}et

for the contravariant functor on
\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S
which, to any
S
-scheme
s:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
in
\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S)
,
assigns the set of

(n,s^{*}(\vartheta))
-projective connections on
(S^{\prime}\times_{S}X^{\mathrm{log}})/(S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\mathrm{log}})

(where
s^{*}(-)
denotes the result of base-changing along
s
).
Then, the assignment
D^{\clubsuit}\mapsto D^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}

determines a morphism of functors

(314)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit,\vartheta}:\mathcal{D}i\!f\!f_{\clubsuit,\vartheta}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}.

Just as in the proof of
[
Wak6
, Proposition 4.28]
, this morphism is verified to be an isomorphism.

Next, let
D
be an
n
-th order differential operator between line bundles on
X
with unit principal symbol.
By taking Proposition
4.4.5
, (i), into account,
we see that
D
forms an
(n,\vartheta^{\prime})
-projective connection for some
n^{(1)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta^{\prime}:=(\varTheta^{\prime},\nabla_{\vartheta^{\prime}})
.

Definition 4.7.2
.

Let us keep the above notation.

(i)

We shall refer to
\vartheta^{\prime}
(or
\nabla_{\vartheta^{\prime}}
) as the
subprincipal symbol
of
D
.

(ii)

Suppose further that the domain of
D
coincides with
\varTheta:=\varTheta_{0}^{\otimes(1-n)}
for a theta characteristic
\varTheta_{0}
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
(cf. Example
4.4.2
).
(In particular,
D
forms a differential operator from
\varTheta^{\vee}
to
\Omega^{\otimes n}\otimes\varTheta^{\vee}
.)
Then, we shall say that
D
has vanishing subprincipal symbol
if its subprincipal symbol coincides with
the
n^{(1)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta_{0}
associated to
\varTheta_{0}
defined in the manner of Example
4.4.2
.
(If such a differential operator
D
has vanishing subprincipal symbol, then it defines an
(n,\vartheta_{0})
-projective connection.)

In the rest of this section,
we suppose that the log structures of
X^{\mathrm{log}}
and
S^{\mathrm{log}}
are trivial (which implies that
X/S
is smooth and
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}=\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(0)}
).
Let
\mathcal{L}
,
\mathcal{N}
be line bundles on
X
and
D
an
n
-th order differential operator from
\mathcal{L}
to
\mathcal{N}
.
Since

D
is
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{X,\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})
-linear,
the kernel
\mathrm{Ker}(D)
forms an
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{X,\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})
-submodule of
\mathcal{L}
.

Definition 4.7.3
.

We shall say that
D
has
a full set of root functions

(or the differential equation associated to
D
has
a full set of solutions
) if the
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{X,\mathrm{triv}}^{(0)})
-module
\mathrm{Ker}(D)
is locally free of rank
n
.

Note that the set of
n
-th order differential operators with a full set of root functions is closed under base-change.
Thus, we obtain the subfunctor

(315)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}i\!f\!f_{\clubsuit,\vartheta}^{\mathrm{full}}

of
\mathcal{D}i\!f\!f_{\clubsuit,\vartheta}
consisting of

(n,\vartheta)
-projective connections
having a full set of root functions.
By an argument similar to the proof of
[
Wak6
, Proposition 4.65]
, we see that
the isomorphism
\Lambda_{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit,\vartheta}
restricts to an isomorphism

(316)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{\mathrm{full}}:\mathcal{D}i\!f\!f_{\clubsuit,\vartheta}^{\mathrm{full}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}.

(In fact, the argument in
[
Wak6
]
can be applied by using the equivalence (a)
\Leftrightarrow
(c) obtained in
Proposition
2.2.4
, (ii), instead of
[
Wak6
, Proposition 4.60]
.)
In particular, by combining with Theorem
4.5.1
,
we obtain the following sequence consisting of isomorphisms between functors:

\textstyle{\mathcal{D}i\!f\!f_{\clubsuit,\vartheta}^{\mathrm{full}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit,\vartheta}^{\mathrm{full}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\heartsuit}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\diamondsuit,\vartheta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit,\vartheta}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\spadesuit}}

(cf.
[
Wak6
, Theorem D]
for the case of
N=1
).

Also,
the following assertion holds.

Corollary 4.7.4
.

Suppose that
n<p
.
Also, let
\varTheta_{0}
be a theta characteristic of
X/S
.
Then, there exists a bijective correspondence between the following two sets:

•

The set of isomorphism classes of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers on
X/S
;

•

The set of
n
-th order differential operators on
\varTheta_{0}^{\otimes(1-n)}
with unit principal symbol and vanishing subprincipal symbol.

Proof.

Denote by
\vartheta_{0}
the
n^{(1)}
-theta characteristic associated to
\varTheta_{0}
defined in the manner of Example
4.4.2
.
Then, the assertion follows from
the isomorphisms
\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit,\vartheta_{0}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
(obtained in Theorem
4.5.1
) and
\Lambda_{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit,\vartheta_{0}}^{\mathrm{full}}
(constructed above).
∎

5.
Radii of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers

This section discusses the radii of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers, which determines a sort of boundary condition to glue together dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on pointed curves along the fibers over the points of attachment.
After proving a certain factorization property of the moduli space

\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}

in accordance with the data of radii,
we obtain the nonemptiness of that space (cf. Corollary
5.5.4
).

Let us fix
a pair of nonnegative integers
(g,r)
with
2g-2+r>0
.
Also, fix a pair of nonnegative integers
(l,m)
with
l=0
or
m=0
.

5.1.
Monodromy/Exponent at a marked point

Let
S
be a flat scheme over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
, and let
\mathscr{X}:=(f:X\rightarrow S,\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i})
be an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
over
S
.
In particular, we obtain a log curve
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
For simplicity, we write
\Omega:=\Omega_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
,
\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
, and
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}:=\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)}
.

Suppose that
r>0
, and
fix
i\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
.
Note that there exists a canonical isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebras

(321)

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}_{i}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{B}_{S}

(cf. (
192
) for the definition of
\mathcal{B}_{S}
)
determined by the following condition:
if
\{\partial^{\langle j\rangle}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
is the local basis of
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
associated, in the manner of §
1.2
, to any local section
t
defining the closed subscheme
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
(which determines a logarithmic coordinate on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
around
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
),
then
\sigma_{i}^{-1}(\partial^{\langle j\rangle})
is mapped to
\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j\rangle}
via (
321
) for every
j
.

Let us take
a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
.
The
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla
induces a
\sigma_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{(m)})
-action
\sigma_{i}^{*}(\nabla)
on

\sigma^{*}_{i}(\mathcal{F})
; this action gives
the composite

(322)

\displaystyle\mu_{i}(\nabla):\mathcal{B}_{S}\xrightarrow{(\ref{e66})^{-1}}\sigma^{*}_{i}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)})\xrightarrow{\sigma^{*}_{i}(\nabla)}\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma^{*}_{i}(\mathcal{F})).

In particular, we obtain

(323)

\displaystyle\mu_{i}(\nabla)^{\langle\bullet\rangle}:=(\mu_{i}(\nabla)^{\langle 1\rangle},\mu_{2}(\nabla)^{\langle p\rangle},\cdots,\mu_{i}(\nabla)^{\langle p^{m}\rangle})\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{\oplus(m+1)}.

(cf. (
194
)).

Definition 5.1.1
.

We shall refer to
\mu_{i}(\nabla)
and
\mu_{i}(\nabla)^{\langle\bullet\rangle}
as the
monodromy operator
of
\nabla
at
\sigma_{i}
.
Also, for each
a=0,\cdots,m
, we shall refer to
\mu_{i}(\nabla)^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}
as the
a
-th monodromy operator
of
\nabla
at
\sigma_{i}
.

Let us take an open subscheme
U
of
X
meeting
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
, and take
a section
t\in\mathcal{O}_{X}
on
U
defining the closed subscheme
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})\cap U
of
U
.
The
t
-adic formal completion
\widehat{U}_{t}
of
U
may be identified with
U_{\oslash}
(cf. (
181
)).
Under this identification
\widehat{U}_{t}=U_{\oslash}
,
the restriction
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}|_{\widehat{U}_{t}}
of
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
to
\widehat{U}_{t}
may be considered as
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
(cf. (
182
)).
The monodromy operator
\mu_{i}(\nabla)
of
\nabla
at
\sigma_{i}
coincides with
that of
the
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}|_{\widehat{U}_{t}}
(in the sense of Definition
3.2.1
)
obtained by restricting
\nabla
.

Moreover, we suppose that
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
is dormant,
\mathcal{F}
is a vector bundle of rank
n>0
, and

S
is connected.
According to Proposition-Definitions
3.4.1
and
3.5.4
,
there exists a well-defined multiset

(324)

\displaystyle e_{i}(\nabla):=[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]

of elements in
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}

satisfying the following condition:
for
any local section
t
as above,
the
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)|_{\widehat{U}_{t}}
obtained by restricting
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
to
\widehat{U}_{t}\left(=U_{\oslash}\right)
has exponent
e_{i}(\nabla)
.
The following definition generalizes
[
Wak7
, Definition 4.1.3]
.

Definition 5.1.2
.

(i)

With the above notation,
we shall refer to
e_{i}(\nabla)
as
the
(local) exponent
of
\nabla
at the marked point
\sigma_{i}
.

(ii)

We shall set
N:=m+1
.
Also, let
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j})
be a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Then, the multiset

(325)

\displaystyle e_{i}(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}):=e_{i}(\nabla)

is called the
(local) exponent
of
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
at
\sigma_{i}
.

The following assertion will be applied in the proofs of Propositions
5.3.5
and
6.6.4
.

Proposition 5.1.3
.

Let us keep the above notation.
Also, let
n
be an integer with
1<n\leq p^{N}
and
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j})
a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Suppose that we are given an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(N-1)}
-modules

(326)

\displaystyle h:(\mathcal{F},\nabla)|_{\widehat{U}_{t}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d_{j}}^{(N-1)}

for some
d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
.
(In particular, the exponent
e_{i}(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit})
of
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
at
\sigma_{i}
coincides with
[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]
.)

(i)

Let
v
be a global section of
\mathcal{F}|_{\widehat{U}_{t}}
generating (formally) the line subbundle
\mathcal{F}^{n-1}|_{\widehat{U}_{t}}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{F}|_{\widehat{U}_{t}}\right)
. We shall write

(327)

\displaystyle(v_{1}(t),\cdots,v_{n}(t)):=h(v)\in\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]^{\oplus n}.

Then, each section
v_{j}(t)
(
j=1,\cdots,n
) belongs to
\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]^{\times}
, or equivalently
v_{j}(0)\in\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\times}
.

(ii)

The elements
d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}
are mutually distinct.

Proof.

Both assertions (i) and (ii) follow from an argument entirely similar to the proof of
[
Wak7
, Proposition 3.4.1]
, so we omit the details of the proofs.
(The case of
N=1
also can be found in
[
Wak6
, Proposition 8.4]
.)
∎

5.2.
Gluing
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules

Let
\mathbb{G}:=({{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG,\{(g_{j},r_{j})\}_{j=1}^{J},\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})
be clutching data of type
(g,r)
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Definition 7.4]
), where
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG:=(V,E,\zeta)
denotes a semi-graph in the sense of
[
Wak6
, Definition 7.1, (i)]
and
J
denotes a positive integer.
For each
j=1,\cdots,J
, suppose that we are given an
r_{j}
-pointed stable curve
{\mathscr{X}_{j}}:=(X_{j}/S_{j},\{\sigma_{j,i}\}^{r_{j}}_{i=1})
of genus
g_{j}
over
S_{j}:=S
.
Also, suppose that
\mathscr{X}
may be obtained by gluing together the
\mathscr{X}_{j}
’s
by means of
\mathbb{G}
in the manner of
[
Wak6
, § 7.2.1]
.
(The discussions in
loc. cit.
only deal with schemes over a
field
, but the gluing construction mentioned there can be applied to a general base space
S
.)
In particular, there exist natural morphisms

(328)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Clut}_{j}:X_{j}\rightarrow X

(
j=1,\cdots,J
) of
S\left(=S_{j}\right)
-schemes.
Here, recall that, for each
j
,
the scheme
S_{j}
(resp.,
X_{j}
) is equipped with the log structure pulled-back from
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{j},r_{j}}^{\mathrm{log}}
(resp.,
the universal family of log curves over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{j},r_{j}}^{\mathrm{log}}
)
via the classifying morphism of
{\mathscr{X}_{j}}
.
Denote by
S_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}
(resp.,
X^{\mathrm{log}}_{j}
) the resulting log scheme.
For simplicity, we shall write

\mathcal{T}_{j}:=\mathcal{T}_{X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}}

and

\mathcal{D}_{j}^{(m)}:=\mathcal{D}_{X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)}
.
Also, write
X^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}_{j}
for the log scheme obtained by equipping
X_{j}
with the log structure pulled-back from
X^{\mathrm{log}}
via
\mathrm{Clut}_{j}
.
The structure morphism
X_{j}\rightarrow S_{j}\left(=S\right)
of
X_{j}/S_{j}
extends to a morphism
X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{log}}
of log schemes.
Moreover, the natural morphisms
S^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow S\left(=S_{j}\right)
and
X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}\rightarrow X_{j}
, respectively,
extend to
morphisms
S^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow S_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}
and
X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}\rightarrow X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}
, which make the following square diagram commute:

\textstyle{X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{S^{\mathrm{log}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}.}

This diagram induces a morphism

X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}\rightarrow X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}\times_{S_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}}S^{\mathrm{log}}

over
S^{\mathrm{log}}

whose
underlying morphism of
S
-schemes coincides with the identity morphism of
X_{j}
.
The differential of this morphism yields isomorphisms of
\mathcal{O}_{X_{j}}
-modules

(333)

\displaystyle\delta_{j}^{\mathcal{T}}:\mathcal{T}_{X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\left(=\mathrm{Clut}_{j}^{*}(\mathcal{T})\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}_{j},\hskip 14.22636pt\delta_{j}^{\mathcal{D}}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\left(=\mathrm{Clut}_{j}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)})\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{j}^{(m)}.

Now,
let
\mathcal{F}
be an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module.
For each
j=1,\cdots,J
, denote by
\mathcal{F}_{j}
the pull-back of
\mathcal{F}
to
X_{j}
.

Definition 5.2.1
.

A
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
on
\mathcal{F}
is a collection

(334)

\displaystyle\{\nabla_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J},

where
each
\nabla_{j}
denotes a
\mathcal{D}_{j}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}_{j}
,
such that, for any closed edge
\{b,b^{\prime}\}\in E^{\mathrm{cl}}
of
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG
with
\zeta(b)=v_{j}
,
\zeta(b^{\prime})=v_{j^{\prime}}
(
j,j^{\prime}\in\{1,\cdots,J\}
),
the equality

(335)

\displaystyle\mu_{\lambda_{j}(b)}(\nabla_{j})\circ\mathrm{sw}=\mu_{\lambda_{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})}(\nabla_{j^{\prime}})\ \left(\Longleftrightarrow\mathrm{sw}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mu_{\lambda_{j}(b)}(\nabla_{j}))^{\langle\bullet\rangle}=\mu_{\lambda_{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})}(\nabla_{j^{\prime}})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}\ \text{by (\ref{dE110})}\right)

holds under the natural identification
\sigma_{j,\lambda_{j}(b)}^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{j})=\sigma_{j^{\prime},\lambda_{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})}^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{j^{\prime}})
.

Let

\nabla:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})
be a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
on
\mathcal{F}
.
For each
j=1,\cdots,J
,
the composite

(336)

\displaystyle\nabla|_{j}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{j}\xrightarrow{(\delta^{\mathcal{D}}_{j})^{-1}}{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Clut}^{*}_{j}(\nabla)}\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F}_{j})

specifies
a
\mathcal{D}_{j}^{(m)}
-module structure
on
\mathcal{F}_{j}
.
We shall refer to
\nabla|_{j}
(resp.,
(\mathcal{F}_{j},\nabla|_{j})
) as the
restriction
of
\nabla
(resp.,
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
) to
\mathcal{F}_{j}
(resp.,
X_{j}
).

Proposition 5.2.2
.

Let
\nabla
be a

\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
on
\mathcal{F}
.

(i)

The collection of its restrictions
\{\nabla|_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}
specifies
a
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
on
\mathcal{F}
.
Moreover, the resulting assignment
\nabla\mapsto\{\nabla|_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}
determines a bijection of sets

(337)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{the set of }\\
\text{$\mathcal{D}^{(m)}$-module structures on $\mathcal{F}$}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{the set of }\\
\text{$\mathbb{G}$-$\mathcal{D}^{(m)}$-module structures on $\mathcal{F}$}\end{pmatrix}.

(ii)

(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
is dormant if and only if
(\mathcal{F}_{j},\nabla|_{j})
is dormant for every
j=1,\cdots,J
.
Moreover, for each
j
,
the formation of restrictions
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)\mapsto(\mathcal{F}_{j},\nabla|_{j})
commutes with diagonal reduction.

Proof.

Assertion (i) follows from Proposition
3.2.2
, (i) and (ii) (cf. the discussion in the proof of
[
Wak6
, Proposition 7.6]
).

Assertion (ii) for
l=0
follows from the commutativity of the following square diagram defined for every
j=1,\cdots,J
:

\textstyle{F_{X_{j}}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{T}_{X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Clut}_{j}^{*}(\psi_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{F_{X_{j}}^{*}(\delta^{\mathcal{T}}_{j})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}|_{X}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{\mathcal{D}}_{j}}
\textstyle{F_{X_{j}}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{T}_{j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{j}^{(m)}.}

Also, the assertion for
m=0
can be proved by applying the assertion for
l=0
together with the definition of the functor
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-)
.
∎

5.3.
Definition of radius

Let
n
be an integer with
1<n<p
.
We shall
discuss the notion of radius associated to a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper of finite level on a pointed stable curve
(cf.
[
Wak7
, Definition 4.3.2]
for the case of pointed smooth curves).
The level
1
case was already defined in
[
Wak6
, Definition 2.32]

under the identification between elements in “
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times n}/\Delta
” (cf. (
342
) below) and certain
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-rational points in the adjoint quotient of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{l}_{n}
.

For convenience, we shall set
N:=m+1
and

L:=l+N
.
Denote by
\Delta
the image of the diagonal embedding
\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}
, which is a group homomorphism.
In particular, we obtain the quotient set
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta
.
Note that the set
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}
is equipped with the action of the symmetric group
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
of
n
letters by permutation;
this action induces a well-defined
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
-action on
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta
.
Hence, we obtain the quotient sets

(342)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n},\hskip 14.22636pt\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta,

and moreover, obtain the natural projection

(343)

\displaystyle\pi_{\Delta}:\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta.

Each element of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}
may be regarded as a multiset of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z}
whose cardinality equals
n
.
Also, we occasionally identify
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}
(resp.,
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta
) with the scheme defined as the disjoint union of copies of
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
indexed by the set
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}
(resp.,
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta
).

Remark 5.3.1

(Radii for
n=2
)
.

Denote by
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}
the set of equivalence classes of elements
a\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z}
, in which

a
and
-a
are identified.
Then, the assignment
a\mapsto[a,-a]
determines a well-defined bijection

(344)

\displaystyle(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{S}_{2}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times 2}/\Delta.

We occasionally identify
\mathfrak{S}_{2}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times 2}/\Delta
with
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}

by using this bijection.
Under this identification, the notion of radius introduced below coincides with the classical notion of radius for torally indigenous bundles
in the sense of

[
Moc2
, Chap. I, Definition 4.1]
(see also
[
Wak7
, Remark 4.3.3]
).

Let us take a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
.
According to Corollary
4.5.2
, (i), we can find a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j})
on
\mathscr{X}
with
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}\cong\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.
For each
i=1,\cdots,r
,
the exponent of
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
at
\sigma_{i}
determines an element

(345)

\displaystyle e_{i}(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}):=[d_{i1},\cdots,d_{in}]\in(\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n})(S),

where
d_{i1},\cdots,d_{in}\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}(S)
.
Its image

(346)

\displaystyle\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}):=\pi_{\Delta}(e_{i}(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}))\in(\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta)(S)

via
\pi_{\Delta}
depends only on the equivalence class
[\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}]
, i.e., the isomorphism class of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.
In fact, if
\mathscr{L}:=(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}})
is a dormant invertible
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module whose exponent at
\sigma_{i}
is
c_{i}\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})(S)
,
then the exponent of

\mathscr{F}_{\otimes\mathscr{L}}^{\heartsuit}

at
\sigma_{i}
coincides with

[d_{i1}+c_{i},\cdots,d_{in}+c_{i}]
.
Since
\pi_{\Delta}([d_{i1},\cdots,d_{in}])=\pi_{\Delta}([d_{i1}+c_{i},\cdots,d_{in}+c_{i}])
, we see that

\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
is a well-defined element associated to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.

Definition 5.3.2
.

(i)

With the above notation,
we shall refer to
\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
as the
radius
of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
at
\sigma_{i}
.

(ii)

Let
\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}
be an element of
(\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta)^{\times r}
.
We shall say that a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is
of radii
\rho
if
\rho_{i}=\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
for every
i=1,\cdots,r
.
When
r=0
, we will refer to any
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper as being
of radii
\emptyset
.

The following two propositions describe some basic properties of the radius of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper.

Proposition 5.3.3
.

Let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
be a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
of radii
\rho\in(\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta)^{\times r}
.
For each integer
L^{\prime}
with
1\leq L^{\prime}\leq L
, we denote by

(347)

\displaystyle q_{L\Rightarrow L^{\prime}}:(\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta)^{\times r}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta)^{\times r}

the surjection induced from the natural quotient
\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}/p^{L^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z}
.

(i)

Suppose further that
l=0
(hence
N=L
).
Then, for each positive integer
N^{\prime}\leq N
, the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N^{\prime})}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
obtained by reducing the level of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
to
N^{\prime}
is of radii
q_{N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}}(\rho)
.

(ii)

Suppose further that
N=1
(hence
l+1=L
).
Then, for each nonnegative integer
l^{\prime}\leq l
,
the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}_{l^{\prime}}
(:= the reduction of
\mathscr{X}
modulo
p^{l^{\prime}+1}
) obtained by reducing
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
modulo
p^{l^{\prime}+1}
is of radii
q_{l+1\Rightarrow l^{\prime}+1}(\rho)
.

Proof.

Assertion (i) follows from Proposition
3.3.4
, (iii).
Also, assertion (ii) follows from the definition of the radius of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper.
∎

Proposition 5.3.4
.

We shall fix
i\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
,
a_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z}
, and
\rho_{0}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta
.
Note that (since
n<p
) there exists a unique
multiset
e_{0}:=[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}
with
\pi_{\Delta}(e_{0})=\rho_{0}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}=a_{0}
.
Also, let
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
be a dormant
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
such that the exponent of
\nabla_{\vartheta}
at
\sigma_{i}
is
a_{0}
, and let
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
be a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Denote by
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper corresponding to
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
via
\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit,\vartheta}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
.
Then, the radius of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
at
\sigma_{i}
is
\rho_{0}
if and only if the exponent of
\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
at
\sigma_{i}
is
e_{0}
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the various definitions involved.
∎

Denote by

\widetilde{\Xi}_{n,L}

the subset of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}
consisting of multisets
[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]

such that the elements

d_{1[0]},\cdots,d_{n[0]}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
determined by
d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}

are mutually distinct.
(In particular,
\widetilde{\Xi}_{n,L}
may be identified with a set of subsets of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z}
with cardinality
n
.)
Also, we denote
the image of
\widetilde{\Xi}_{n,L}
via the projection
\pi_{\Delta}:\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta
by

(348)

\displaystyle\Xi_{n,L}\ \left(\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta\right).

Under the identification
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}=\mathfrak{S}_{2}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times 2}/\Delta
given by (
344
),

\Xi_{2,L}
may be identified with the subset
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}
of
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}
.

Proposition 5.3.5
.

Let
\rho
be
an element of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta
such that
there exists a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
whose radius at
\sigma_{i}
(for some
i\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
) coincides with
\rho
.
Then,
\rho
belongs to
\Xi_{n,L}
.

Proof.

Let us choose a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
whose radius at
\sigma_{i}
coincides with
\rho
.
There exists a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j})
on
\mathscr{X}
with
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}\cong\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
(cf. Corollary
4.5.2
, (i)).
The exponent
e_{i}(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit})
of
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
at
\sigma_{i}
can be described as a multiset
[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]
(
d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z}
).
Now, let us consider the case where
l=0
, or equivalently
L=N
(resp.,
N=1
, or equivalently
L=l+1
).
By
Propositions
4.3.4
and
5.3.3
, (i) (resp., Proposition
5.3.3
, (ii)),
the radius at
\sigma_{i}
of the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper obtained by reducing the level of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
to
1
(resp., by reducing
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
modulo
p
)
coincides with the image of
[d_{1[0]},\cdots,d_{n[0]}]
in
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta
.
Then, it follows from
Proposition
5.1.3
, (ii) (or

[
Wak7
, Proposition 3.4.1]
), that
d_{1[0]},\cdots,d_{n[0]}
are mutually distinct.
This means that
\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})\in\Xi_{n,L}
, and hence completes the proof of this proposition.
∎

Write
R:=\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
For each

\rho:=(\rho_{1},\cdots,\rho_{r})\in(\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta)^{\times r}
(where
\rho:=\emptyset
if
r=0
),
we shall write

(349)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r,R}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},\ \text{or simply}\ \mathcal{O}p_{\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},

for the full subcategory of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r,R}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
consisting of pairs
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
such that the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is of radii
\rho
.
The projection
\Pi_{g,r}
restricts to a morphism

(350)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\rho,g,r}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}.

It follows from Proposition
5.3.5
that

\mathcal{O}p_{\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is empty unless
\rho\in\Xi_{n,L}^{\times r}
, and that

\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!
and
\Pi_{g,r}
decompose as the disjoint unions

(351)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}=\coprod_{\rho\in\Xi_{n,L}^{\times r}}\mathcal{O}p_{\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Pi_{g,r}=\coprod_{\rho\in\Xi_{n,L}^{\times r}}\Pi_{\rho,g,r},

respectively, where
\Xi_{n,L}^{\times r}:=\{\emptyset\}
if
r=0
.
Also, the functor
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-)
(cf. (
294
)) restricts to a functor

(352)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-):\mathcal{O}p_{1,\rho,g,r,\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}.

That is to say, a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(1)}
-oper on a pointed stable curve classified by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}}
is of radii
\rho
if and only if its diagonal reduction is of radii
\rho

(cf. Definition
4.5.6
).

Theorem 5.3.6
.

Suppose that
l=0
.
Then,
for each
\rho\in\Xi_{n,L}^{\times r}
,
the category
\mathcal{O}p_{\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
may be represented by a (possibly empty) proper Deligne-Mumford stack over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and the projection
\Pi_{\rho,g,r}

is finite.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the decompositions displayed in (
351
) together with Theorem
4.5.5
and Corollary
4.6.2
.
∎

5.4.
Gluing dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers

In this subsection, we shall discuss a gluing procedure for dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers by means of clutching data.

For each
d:=(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n})\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}
, we write

d^{\veebar}:=(-d_{1},\cdots,-d_{n})
.
The assignment
d\mapsto d^{\veebar}
induces a well-defined involution

(353)

\displaystyle(-)^{\veebar}:\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta

on the set
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta
.
Also, for each
\vec{\rho}:=(\rho_{1},\cdots,\rho_{r})\in(\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times n}/\Delta)^{\times r}
,
we set
\rho^{\veebar}:=(\rho_{1}^{\veebar},\cdots,\rho_{r}^{\veebar})
.
Note that
(-)^{\veebar}
restricts to an involution on
\Xi_{n,L}
(hence also on
\Xi_{n,L}^{\times r}
).

Let

\mathbb{G}
and

\mathscr{X}_{j}
(
j=1,\cdots,J
) be as in §
5.2
.

Definition 5.4.1
.

A
set of
\mathbb{G}
-
\Xi_{n,L}
-radii
is an ordered set

(354)

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathbb{G}}:=\{\rho^{j}\}_{j=1}^{J},

where each
\rho^{j}:=(\rho^{j}_{i})_{i=1}^{r_{j}}
is an element of

\Xi_{n,L}^{\times r_{j}}

such that for
every closed edge
\{b,b^{\prime}\}\in E^{\mathrm{cl}}
of
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG
with
\zeta(b)=v_{j}
,
\zeta(b^{\prime})=v_{j^{\prime}}
(for some
j
,
j^{\prime}\in\{1,\cdots,J\}
),
the equality
\rho^{j}_{\lambda_{j}(b)}=(\rho^{j^{\prime}}_{\lambda_{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})})^{\veebar}
holds.

Definition 5.4.2
.

(i)

A
dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
on
\mathscr{X}
is a collection

(355)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\{\phi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}),

where
\mathcal{E}_{B}
denotes a
B
-bundle on
X
and each
\phi_{j}
(
j=1,\cdots,J
) denotes an
(N-1)
-PD stratification
on

(\mathcal{E}_{B}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{n})_{j}\left(:=\mathcal{E}_{B,j}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}\right)/X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}
, satisfying the following conditions:

–

For each
j=1,\cdots,J
, the pair
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G},j}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,j},\phi_{j})
forms a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}_{j}
.

–

For each closed edge
\{b,b^{\prime}\}\in E^{\mathrm{cl}}
of
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG
with
\zeta(b)=v_{j}
,
\zeta(b^{\prime})=v_{j^{\prime}}
(for some
j,j^{\prime}\in\{1,\cdots,J\}
), the equality
\rho_{\lambda_{j}(b)}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G},j})=(\rho_{\lambda_{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G},j^{\prime}}))^{\veebar}
holds.

(ii)

Let
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G},\circ}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,\circ},\{\phi_{\circ,j}\}_{j=1}^{J})
and

\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G},\bullet}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,\bullet},\{\phi_{\bullet,j}\}_{j=1}^{J})

be dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on
\mathscr{X}
.
An
isomorphism of dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers
from

\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G},\circ}^{\spadesuit}
to
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G},\bullet}^{\spadesuit}
is defined as an isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{B,\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}_{B,\bullet}
of
B
-bundles such that, for every
j=1,\cdots,J
, the induced isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{B,\circ,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}_{B,\bullet,j}
defines an isomorphism of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G},\circ,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G},\bullet,j}^{\spadesuit}
.

Definition 5.4.3
.

Let
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}:=\{\rho^{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
be a set of
\mathbb{G}
-
\Xi_{n,L}
-radii and

\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\{\phi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})
a dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Then, we shall say that
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\spadesuit}
is
of radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
if
for any
j=1,\cdots,J
, the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G},j}
is of radii
\rho^{j}
.

Proposition 5.4.4
.

(i)

Any dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
does not have nontrivial automorphisms.

(ii)

Suppose that
N=1
, and we set
N^{\prime}:=l+1
.
Denote by

\mathscr{X}_{0}

the reduction of
\mathscr{X}
modulo
p
.
Also, let
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\{\phi_{j}\}_{j})
be a dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(1)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Then, the collection of data

(356)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,0},\{{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\phi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})

forms a dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N^{\prime})}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}_{0}
.
(We shall refer to
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\spadesuit}
as the
diagonal reduction
of
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\spadesuit}
.)
If, moreover,
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
is a set of
\mathbb{G}
-
\Xi_{n,N}
-radii,
then
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is of radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
if and only if
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\spadesuit}
is of radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
.

Proof.

Assertion (i) follows from Proposition
4.2.4
.
Also, assertion (ii) follows from the various definitions involved and the functor
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-)
(cf. (
352
)).
∎

Proposition 5.4.5
.

(i)

There exists an equivalence of categories

(357)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of dormant}\\
\text{$\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}$-opers on $\mathscr{X}$}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of dormant}\\
\text{$\mathbb{G}$-$\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}$-opers on $\mathscr{X}$}\end{pmatrix}.

Moreover, this equivalence commute with the formation of diagonal reductions.

(ii)

Let
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}:=\{\rho^{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}
be a set of
\mathbb{G}
-
\Xi_{n,L}
-radii.
Then, there exists an equivalence of categories

(358)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of dormant}\\
\text{$\mathbb{G}$-$\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}$-opers of radii $\rho_{\mathbb{G}}$ on $\mathscr{X}$}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of dormant}\\
\text{$\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}$-opers of radii $\rho^{j}$ on $\mathscr{X}_{j}$}\end{pmatrix}.

Moreover, this equivalence commute with the formation of diagonal reductions.

Proof.

First, we shall prove assertion (i).
Choose a dormant
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Also, let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)
be a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
By Theorem
4.5.1
,
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
defines a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
via the isomorphism
\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit,\vartheta}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
.
It follows from Proposition
5.2.2
that the collection
\{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}|_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}
(cf. (
336
)) forms a
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
.
For each
j=1,\cdots,J
, denote by
\mathcal{E}_{B,j}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{j}
) the restriction of
\mathcal{E}_{B}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{E}_{B}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
) to
X_{j}
, and by

\phi_{j}
the
(N-1)
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}_{j}/X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}
induced from
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}|_{j}
via projectivization.
Then,
the pair
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G},j}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,j},\phi_{j})
forms a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}_{j}
.
Let
\{b,b^{\prime}\}\in E^{\mathrm{cl}}
be
a closed edge of
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG
with
\zeta(b)=v_{j}
,
\zeta(b^{\prime})=v_{j^{\prime}}
(for some
j,j^{\prime}\in\{1,\cdots,J\}
), and
write
i:=\lambda_{j}(b)
,
i^{\prime}:=\lambda_{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})
.
Then, Proposition
3.3.2
implies the equality

(359)

\displaystyle e_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}|_{j})=e_{i^{\prime}}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}|_{j^{\prime}})^{\veebar}\ \left(\Longrightarrow\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G},j}^{\spadesuit})=(\rho_{i^{\prime}}(\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G},j^{\prime}}^{\spadesuit}))^{\veebar}\right).

That is to say,
the collection
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\emptyset\Rightarrow\mathbb{G}}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\{\phi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})
specifies a dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
The resulting assignment
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\emptyset\Rightarrow\mathbb{G}}
defines a functor of the form (
357
).
Also,
this functor is fully faithful by Propositions
4.2.4
and
5.4.4
, (i).

In what follows,
we shall prove the essential surjectivity of this functor.
To this end,
we are always free to replace
S
with its covering in the étale topology because (
357
) is fully faithful
.
Let
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\{\phi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})
be a dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Note that, for each
j=1,\cdots,J
, the pair of restrictions
\vartheta_{j}:=(\varTheta_{j},\nabla_{\vartheta}|_{j})
, where
\varTheta_{j}:=\mathrm{Clut}_{j}^{*}(\varTheta)
, forms a dormant
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic of
X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}
.
Denote by
\nabla_{j}^{\diamondsuit}
(
j=1,\cdots,J
) the dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta_{j})
-oper on
\mathscr{X}_{j}
corresponding to
\mathscr{E}_{j}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,j},\phi_{j})
.
For each
i=1,\cdots,r_{j}
,
let us fix an identification of
U_{\oslash}
(cf. (
181
)) with the formal neighborhood
\widehat{U}_{j,i}
of

\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{j,i})
in
X_{j}
.
(We can choose such an identification thanks to the italicized fact described above.)
Also, we shall fix an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(N-1)}
-modules

(360)

\displaystyle h_{j,i}:(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{j}},\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{j})|_{\widehat{U}_{j,i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\bigoplus_{s=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d_{i,s}^{j}}^{(N-1)},

where
[d_{i,1}^{j},\cdots,d_{i,n}^{j}]
are the exponent of
\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}_{j}
at
\sigma_{j,i}
.
This isomorphism restricts to an isomorphism
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(h_{j,i}):\sigma_{j,i}^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{j}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\oplus n}\left(=\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{\oplus n})\right)
.
Here, let us take a closed edge
\{b,b^{\prime}\}\in E^{\mathrm{cl}}

of
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG
with
\zeta(b)=v_{j}
,
\zeta(b^{\prime})=v_{j^{\prime}}
(for some
j,j^{\prime}\in\{1,\cdots,J\}
).
Then, the equalities
e_{i}(\nabla_{\vartheta}|_{j})=-e_{i^{\prime}}(\nabla_{\vartheta}|_{j^{\prime}})
and
\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{j})=\rho_{i^{\prime}}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{j^{\prime}})^{\veebar}
hold (cf. Propositions
5.2.2
, (i), and
5.4.4
).
It follows from Proposition
5.3.4
that, after possibly rearranging the indexes of elements of the
e_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{j})
’s,
we obtain the equality
d^{j}_{i,s}=-d_{i^{\prime},s}^{j^{\prime}}
for every
s=1,\cdots,n
.
In particular, the equality

(361)

\displaystyle\mu_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{j})\circ\mathrm{sw}=\mu_{i^{\prime}}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{j^{\prime}})

holds under the identifications given by
h_{j,i}
and
h_{j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}
(cf. (
211
)).
Also,
according to Proposition
5.1.3
, (i), we may assume,
after possibly composing
h_{j,i}
with an automorphism of
\bigoplus_{s=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d^{j}_{i,s}}^{(N-1)}
determined by an element of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
,
that the equality

\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(h_{j,i})(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{j}}^{n-1})=\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(h_{j^{\prime},i^{\prime}})(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{j^{\prime}}}^{n-1})
holds in
\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\oplus n}
.
By the definition of a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper,
this equality implies

(362)

\displaystyle\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(h_{j,i})(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{j}}^{s})=\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(h_{j^{\prime},i^{\prime}})(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{j^{\prime}}}^{s})

for every
s=0,\cdots,n
.
By (
361
) and (
362
),
the collections
(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{j}},\nabla_{j}^{\diamondsuit},\{\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{j}}^{s}\}_{s=0}^{n})
(
j=1,\cdots,J
) may be glued together to obtain
a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
(cf. Proposition
3.2.2
, (ii)).
If
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
denotes the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper induced from
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
,
then the dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\emptyset\Rightarrow\mathbb{G}}
given by its image via (
357
) is isomorphic to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
by construction.
This means that the functor (
357
) is essentially surjective, and hence it is an equivalence of categories.
The latter assertion of (i) follows from the definitions of (
357
) and
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-)
.

Next, we shall prove assertion (ii).
The assignment
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G}}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\{\phi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})\mapsto(\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G},j}^{\spadesuit})_{j=1}^{J}
(where
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G},j}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,j},\phi_{j})
) defines a functor of the form (
358
).
This functor is immediately verified to commute with the formation of diagonal reductions.
Also, it is fully faithful because of Propositions
4.2.4
and
5.4.4
, (i), so the remaining portion is the essential surjectivity.

Suppose that we are given a collection
(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{j})_{j=1}^{J}
, where each
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{j}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,j},\phi_{j})
(
j=1,\cdots,J
) is a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper of radii
\rho^{j}
on
\mathscr{X}_{j}
.
Denote by
{{}^{\dagger}}\mathcal{E}_{B}
the
B
-bundle defined in
[
Wak6
, Eq. (605)]
.
If we write
({{}^{\dagger}}\mathcal{E}_{B})_{j}
(
j=1,\cdots,J
) for the restriction of
{{}^{\dagger}}\mathcal{E}_{B}
to
X_{j}
, then
there exists an isomorphism of
B
-bundles
\mathcal{E}_{B,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}({{}^{\dagger}}\mathcal{E}_{B})_{j}
for every
j
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Proposition 4.55]
).
We obtain an
(N-1)
-PD stratification
{{}^{\dagger}}\phi_{j}
on
({{}^{\dagger}}\mathcal{E}_{B})_{j}/X_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}/S_{j}^{\mathrm{log}}
corresponding to
\phi_{j}
via this isomorphism.
The resulting collection
({{}^{\dagger}}\mathcal{E}_{B},\{{{}^{\dagger}}\phi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})
forms a dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper of radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
on
\mathscr{X}
which is mapped to
(\mathscr{E}_{j}^{\spadesuit})_{j=1}^{J}
via (
358
).
This proves the essential surjectivity of (
358
), and hence we have finished the proof of assertion (ii).
∎

Remark 5.4.6

(The choice of an
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic)
.

Despite the fact that a specific
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta
is fixed in the proof of assertion (i),
the resulting functor (
357
) does not depend on the choice of
\vartheta
.

Just as in the discussion of
[
Wak6
, § 7.1.3]
,
we obtain the morphism between moduli stacks

(363)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Clut}_{\mathbb{G}}:\prod_{j=1}^{J}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{j},r_{j}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r},

given by assigning
\{\mathscr{X}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}\mapsto\mathscr{X}
,
where the product “
\prod
” in the domain is taken over
R
.

Let
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}:=\{\rho^{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}
be a set of
\mathbb{G}
-
\Xi_{n,L}
-radii and

\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G}}
be a dormant
\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
of radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
.
By
the equivalence of categories (
357
) resulting from
Proposition
5.4.5
, (i),

\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G}}

corresponds to
a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}\Rightarrow\emptyset}^{\spadesuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
.
The radii of
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}\Rightarrow\emptyset}^{\spadesuit}
can be determined by
\rho^{j}
(
j=1,\cdots,J
) in accordance with the clutching data
\mathbb{G}
; we denote the resulting radii of
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}\Rightarrow\emptyset}^{\spadesuit}
by

(364)

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathbb{G}\Rightarrow\emptyset}\in\Xi_{n,L}^{\times r}.

On the other hand, it follows from Proposition
5.4.5
, (ii), that
any point of
\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{O}p_{\rho^{j},g_{j},r_{j}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
classifies a unique (up to isomorphism) dormant

\mathbb{G}
-
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper of radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
.
Hence, the assignment
(\{\mathscr{X}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbb{G}})\mapsto(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}_{\mathbb{G}\Rightarrow\emptyset}^{\spadesuit})
determines
a well-defined morphism

(365)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Clut}_{\mathbb{G},\rho_{\mathbb{G}}}:\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{O}p_{\rho^{j},g_{j},r_{j}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\rho_{\mathscr{G}\Rightarrow\emptyset},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}.

Theorem 5.4.7
.

Let us keep the notation.
Also, let
\rho
be an element of
\Xi_{n,L}^{\times r}
(where
\rho:=\emptyset
if
r=0
).
Then, the following commutative square diagram is Cartesian:

\textstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{\mathbb{G}}:=\{\rho^{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho^{j},g_{j},r_{j}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{\mathbb{G}}}\mathrm{Clut}_{\mathbb{G},\rho_{\mathbb{G}}}}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{\mathbb{G}}}\prod_{j}\Pi_{\rho^{j},g_{j},r_{j}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,g,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{\rho,g,r}}
\textstyle{\prod_{j=1}^{J}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{j},r_{j}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Clut}_{\mathbb{G}}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r},}

where the products “
\prod
” are taken over
R
and
the disjoint union on the upper-left corner runs over the sets of
\mathbb{G}
-
\Xi_{n,L}
-radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
with
\rho_{\mathbb{G}\Rightarrow\emptyset}=\rho
.
Finally, this diagram is compatible with the functors
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!(-)
(cf. (
352
)) in a natural sense.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Proposition
5.4.5
, (i) and (ii).
∎

5.5.
Nonemptiness of the moduli space

Let
S
be as before.
Denote by
\mathbb{P}
the projective line over
S
, i.e.,
\mathbb{P}:=\mathcal{P}roj(\mathcal{O}_{S}[x_{1},x_{2}])
.
Also, denote by

[0]
,
[1]
, and
[\infty]
the
S
-rational points of
\mathbb{P}
determined by the values
0
,
1
, and
\infty
, respectively.
After ordering the three points
[0],[1],[\infty]
, we obtain
a unique (up to isomorphism)
3
-pointed stable curve

(370)

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}:=(\mathbb{P}/S,\{[0],[1],[\infty]\})

of genus
0
over
S
.
In particular, we obtain a log curve
\mathbb{P}^{\mathrm{log}}
over
S\left(=S^{\mathrm{log}}\right)
.

We shall write
\mathcal{L}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(-1)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}([0]+[1]+[\infty])
, and write
\kappa
for the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}
-linear injection
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(-1)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{\oplus 2}
given by
w\mapsto(wx_{1},wx_{2})
for each local section

w\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(-1)
.
Also, let
\mathcal{F}
be a vector bundle on
\mathbb{P}
which makes the following square diagram cocartesian:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(-1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{\oplus 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}.}

The trivial
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
(\nabla_{\mathbb{P},\mathrm{triv}}^{(N-1)})^{\oplus 2}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{\oplus 2}
extends uniquely to a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}^{(N-1)}
on
\mathcal{F}
.
Note that the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}
-linear composite

(375)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}^{(N-1)\Rightarrow(0)}}\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F}\twoheadrightarrow\Omega\otimes(\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{L})

is nonzero.
Since
\mathbb{P}/S
is a smooth curve and
\Omega\otimes(\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{L})
is a line bundle, this composite must be injective.
Moreover, the following equalities hold:

(376)

\displaystyle\mathrm{deg}(\Omega\otimes(\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{L}))=\mathrm{det}(\Omega)+\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{F})-\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{L})=1+3-2=2\left(=\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{L})\right).

It follows that (
375
) turns out to be an isomorphism.
That is to say, the triple

(377)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{N,S}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}^{(N-1)},\mathcal{L})

forms a
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{P}
.
Also,
we shall write

(378)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N,S}:=\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}_{N,S},

which defines a
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{P}
.
Both
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{0,S}
and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0,S}
are dormant
and
satisfy

(379)

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{0,S}=\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{N,S_{0}},\hskip 28.45274pt{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0,S}=\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N,S_{0}}.

Denote by

(380)

\displaystyle\rho_{L,\circledcirc}\ \left(\text{or simply,}\ \rho_{\circledcirc}\right)\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}

the element defined as the image of
\frac{1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z}
via the natural quotient

\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}
.
(By the bijection (
344
), we occasionally regard it as an element of
\mathfrak{S}_{2}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times 2}/\Delta
.)
The exponent of
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{N,S_{0}}
at every marked point coincides with
[1,0]
, so we have
\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N,S_{0}})=\rho_{\circledcirc}
for every
i=1,\cdots,r
.

Here, we shall describe the definition of a totally degenerate curve over a general base space
S
(cf., e.g.,
[
Wak6
, Definition 7.15]
for the case where
S
is the spectrum of an algebraically closed field).

Definition 5.5.1
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
over
S
.
Then, we shall say that
\mathscr{X}
is
totally degenerate

if there exists clutching data

\mathbb{G}:=({{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG,\{(g_{j},r_{j})\}_{j=1}^{J},\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})
of type
(g,r)
satisfying the following conditions:

•

{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG

coincides with the dual semi-graph of
\mathscr{X}
(cf.
[
Wak6
, § 7.1.2]
) and
(g_{j},r_{j})=(0,3)
for every
j=1,\cdots,J
;

•

\mathscr{X}
may be obtained by gluing together
J
copies of the
3
-pointed projective line
\mathscr{P}
by means of
\mathbb{G}
in the manner of
[
Wak6
, § 7.2.1]
.

If
\mathscr{X}
is totally degenerate and
\mathbb{G}
is clutching data satisfying the above conditions, then we shall say that
\mathbb{G}
is
compatible with
\mathscr{X}
.

Theorem 5.5.2
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be an
r
-pointed totally degenerate curve of genus
g
over

S
.
We shall set

(381)

\displaystyle\rho_{\circledcirc}^{\times r}:=(\rho_{\circledcirc},\cdots,\rho_{\circledcirc})\in((\mathbb{Z}/p^{L}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\})^{\times r},

where
\rho_{\circledcirc}^{\times r}:=\emptyset
if
r=0
.
Then,
there exists a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
of radii
\rho_{\circledcirc}^{\times r}
.
In particular, the category
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho_{\circledcirc}^{\times r},g,r}
(hence also
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
) for
n=2
is nonempty.

Proof.

Let us take clutching data

\mathbb{G}
that is compatible with

\mathscr{X}
.
In particular,
\mathscr{X}
may be obtained by gluing together
3
-pointed projective lines
\mathscr{X}_{j}\left(\cong\mathscr{P}\right)
(
j=1,\cdots,J
) by means of
\mathbb{G}
.
Since
\rho_{\circledcirc}=\rho_{\circledcirc}^{\veebar}
,
the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N,S}
defined on the respective components
\mathscr{X}_{j}
may be glued together to form a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
(cf. Proposition
5.4.5
, (i) and (ii)).
The resulting dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper is verified to be of radii
\rho_{\circledcirc}^{\times r}
, so this completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

Next, let
S^{\mathrm{log}}
and
f^{\mathrm{log}}:X^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{log}}
be as in §
4.2
.
Also, let us take a
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j=0}^{2})
on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
For an integer
n
with
1<n<p
,
the
(n-1)
-st symmetric product
S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})
of
\mathcal{F}
over
\mathcal{O}_{X}

forms a rank
n
vector bundle.
It admits a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
S^{n-1}(\nabla)
induced naturally by
\nabla
.
Moreover,
S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})
is equipped with an
n
-step decreasing filtration
\{S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})^{j}\}_{j=0}^{n}
induced from
\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j}
;
to be precise, we set
S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})^{0}:=S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})
,
S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})^{n}:=0
, and
S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})^{j}
(for each
j=1,\cdots,n-1
) is defined as
the image of
(\mathcal{F}^{1})^{\otimes j}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\otimes(n-1-j)}
via the natural quotient
