\mathcal{F}^{\otimes(n-1)}\twoheadrightarrow S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})
.
This filtration satisfies that

(382)

\displaystyle S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})^{j}/S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})^{j+1}\cong\mathcal{F}^{n-1}\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\otimes(n-1-j)}

for every
j=0,\cdots,n-1
.
By the assumption
n<p
, the collection

(383)

\displaystyle S^{n-1}(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}):=(S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F}),S^{n-1}(\nabla),\{S^{n-1}(\mathcal{F})^{j}\}_{j=0}^{n})

is verified to form a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
If
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
is dormant, then so is
S^{n-1}(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit})
.

Since the formation of
S^{n-1}(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit})
commutes with base-change over
S
-schemes and preserves the equivalence class (cf. Definition
4.3.5
),
the assignment
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}\mapsto S^{n-1}(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit})

defines a morphism of functors

(384)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit,n=2}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit,n=2}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\right),

where
\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit,n=2}
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit,n=2}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
) denotes the functor
\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
) in the case of
n=2
.
By using this morphism, we obtain the following assertion.

Proposition 5.5.3
.

(i)

Suppose that

S
is an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-scheme (i.e.,
l=0
) and that
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
arises from
an unpointed, geometrically connected, proper, and smooth curve of genus
g>1
.
Then, the fiber of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\spadesuit}\!
over each geometric point of
S
is nonempty.

(ii)

Suppose that
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
arises from an unpointed totally degenerate curve of genus
g>1
.
Then, the fiber of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\spadesuit}\!
over each
S
-scheme in
\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{S}ch_{R}^{\mathrm{flat}}/S)
is nonempty.

Proof.

First, we shall prove assertion (i).
To this end, we may assume, without loss of generality,
that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
for an algebraically closed field
k
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Under this assumption,
\mathcal{O}p_{\heartsuit,n=2}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is known to be nonempty by
[
Wak4
, Theorem 7.5.2]
.
Hence,
by using the morphism (
384
), we see that
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\heartsuit}\!\neq\emptyset
, which implies
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\spadesuit}\!\neq\emptyset
via the isomorphism
\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
(cf. Theorem
4.5.1
).

Also, assertion (ii) is a direct consequence of Theorem
5.5.2
.
∎

Corollary 5.5.4
.

Suppose that
R=\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Also, let
n
and
N
be positive integers with
1<n<p
.

(i)

The fibers of the projection
\Pi_{g,r}

over the geometric points of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
classifying totally degenerate curves are nonempty.
In particular,
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
is nonempty.

(ii)

Suppose further that
r=0
(and
g>1
).
Then,
the fibers of the projection
\Pi_{g,r}

over the geometric points of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0}
classifying smooth curves is nonempty.
In particular,
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,0}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0}}\mathcal{M}_{g,0}
is nonempty and
\Pi_{g,0}
is surjective.

Proof.

Assertions (i) and the former assertion of (ii) (as well as the nonemptiness of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0}}\mathcal{M}_{g,0}
) follow from Proposition
5.5.3
.
Also, the former assertion of (ii) implies the surjectivity of
\Pi_{g,0}
because
\mathcal{O}p_{g,0}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is proper over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0}
(cf. Corollary
4.6.2
) and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0}
is irreducible.
∎

Remark 5.5.5

(The nonemptiness assertion)
.

The nonemptiness of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}\!\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}}\mathcal{M}_{g,r}
(including the case of
r>0
) will be proved in Corollary
6.4.4
by a different approach.

6.
Deformation theory of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers

This section focuses on the study of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers in characteristic
p
from a deformation point of view.
By applying cohomological descriptions of their deformation spaces,
we prove that the moduli space

\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
for
n=2

is smooth (cf. Corollary
6.3.6
, (i)) and the projection onto
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
is generically étale (cf. Theorem
6.7.1
).

In this section,
we suppose that
p>2
.
Let
B
denote the Borel subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
consisting of the images of invertible upper triangular
2\times 2
matrices via the quotient
\mathrm{GL}_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
.
Also, denote by
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{b}
the Lie algebras of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
and
B
, respectively.
We shall fix a positive integer
N
.
By using the bijection (
344
),
we identify
\Xi_{2,N}
with the set
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}
.

For a morphism of sheaves
\nabla:\mathcal{K}^{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{1}
,
we denote by
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla]

the complex defined to be
\nabla
regarded as a complex concentrated at degrees
0
and
1
(hence
\mathcal{K}^{0}[\nabla]:=\mathcal{K}^{0}
and
\mathcal{K}^{1}[\nabla]:=\mathcal{K}^{1}
).
Also, given an integer
n
and a sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
S
,
we define
the complex
\mathcal{F}[n]
to be
\mathcal{F}
(considered as a complex concentrated at degree
0
) shifted down by
n
, so that
\mathcal{F}[n]^{-n}=\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}[n]^{i}=0
(
i\neq-n
).

Finally, let us fix
a pair of nonnegative integers
(g,r)
with
2g-2+r>0
.
In the rest of the present paper,
we denote by
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
(or
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}
if there is a fear of confusion) the Deligne-Mumford stack associated to this pair
(g,r)
representing
the category displayed in the resp’d portion of (
290
) in the case where
(n,R)
is taken to be
(2,\mathbb{F}_{p})
(cf. Theorem
4.5.5
).

6.1.
Adjoint bundle associated to a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper

Let

S
be a scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{p}

and
\mathscr{X}:=(f:X\rightarrow S,\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r})
an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
over
S
.
In particular, we obtain a log curve
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
For simplicity, we write
\Omega:=\Omega_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
,
\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
, and
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}:=\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.

Let
\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}
be an element of
((\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\})^{\times r}
(where
\rho:=\emptyset
if
r=0
).
For each
i=1,\cdots,r
, we denote by
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}
the
unique integer with
0\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\leq\frac{p-1}{2}
that mapped to

\rho_{i}
via the natural quotient
\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}
.
Also, denote by
\lambda_{i}
the image of
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}
via
\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
.

Next, let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)

be a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
of radii
\rho
, and let

\mathcal{E}
be the
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-bundle induced by
\mathcal{E}_{B}
.
In particular, we obtain the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
corresponding to
\mathcal{E}
(cf. the discussion following Remark
1.3.3
).
Denote by
\pi
the structure morphism
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow X
of
\mathcal{E}
.
By pulling-back the log structure of
X^{\mathrm{log}}
via
\pi
,
one may obtain a log structure on
\mathcal{E}
; we denote the resulting log scheme by
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}
.
The
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-action on
\mathcal{E}
induces a
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-action on the direct image
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})
of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
We shall set

(385)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}:=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})^{\mathrm{PGL}_{2}},

i.e.,
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
is the subsheaf of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-invariant sections of
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})
.
Denote by
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
(resp.,
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}
) the adjoint vector bundle of
\mathcal{E}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{B}
), i.e., the vector bundle on
X
associated to
\mathcal{E}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{B}
) via change of structure group by the adjoint representation
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}(\mathfrak{g})
(resp.,
B\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}(\mathfrak{b})
).
We regard
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule of
\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})
under the natural identification
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}/X})^{\mathrm{PGL}_{2}}\left(=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/X^{\mathrm{log}}})^{\mathrm{PGL}_{n}}\right)
.
Differentiating
\pi
yields a short exact sequence of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

(386)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\xrightarrow{d_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{T}\longrightarrow 0

(cf.
[
Wak6
, § 1.2.5]
).

Note that
\phi
induces a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure

(387)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})

on
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
To be precise, if
\phi^{\natural\natural}
denotes the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
corresponding to
\phi
(cf. Remark
1.3.4
), then
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi}
is
given by
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi}(D)(v)=[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),v]
for any local sections
D\in\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
and
v\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
In particular, we obtain a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module
(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})
, which has vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature.

Recall from
[
Wak6
, § 2.1.2]
that
there exists a canonical
3
-step decreasing filtration

(388)

\displaystyle 0=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{1}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{-1}=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}

on
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
together with natural identifications

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{i}/\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{i+1}=\Omega^{\otimes i}
(
i=-1,0,1
) and
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{0}=\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}
.
Then, we can prove the following assertion.

Proposition 6.1.1
.

The collection of data

(389)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}},\{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{j-1}\}_{j=0}^{3})

forms a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{3}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Moreover, for each
i=1,\cdots,r
, the exponent of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\heartsuit}
at
\sigma_{i}
coincides with
[0,\lambda_{i},-\lambda_{i}]
.

Proof.

Let us fix a dormant
2^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
The dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
corresponds to a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j})
on
\mathscr{X}
via the isomorphism
\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
(cf. Theorem
4.5.1
).
Let
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})
denote the sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear endomorphisms of
\mathcal{F}
with vanishing trace.
This sheaf has
a decreasing filtration

(390)

\displaystyle 0=\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{2}\subseteq\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{0}\subseteq\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{-1}=\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F})

determined by the condition that

\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{1}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})
) consists of local sections
h
with
h(\mathcal{F}^{1})=0
(resp.,
h(\mathcal{F}^{1})\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{1}
).
Also,
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})
admits a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}
induced naturally from
\nabla
.
One may verify that the resulting collection

(391)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F}),\nabla_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}},\{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{j-1}\}_{j=1}^{3})

forms a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{3}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
On the other hand, there exists a canonical identification
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
, by which
\{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{j-1}\}_{j}
and
\nabla_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}
correspond to
\{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{j-1}\}_{j}
and
\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}
, respectively.
This implies that
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\heartsuit}
is a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{3}^{(N)}
-oper.
This proves the former assertion.

Next, to prove the latter assertion, we fix
i\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
.
Let
[d_{i1},d_{i2}]
be the exponent of
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
at
\sigma_{i}
.
Since
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}\cong\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
,
the equality
d_{i1}-d_{i2}=\lambda_{i}
holds.
We see that the exponent of
\nabla_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}
at
\sigma_{i}
is given by
[0,d_{1}-d_{2},d_{2}-d_{1}]=[0,\lambda_{i},-\lambda_{i}]
(cf. Proposition
3.3.4
, (i)), which completes the proof of the latter assertion.
∎

Proposition 6.1.2
.

Let us fix

a\in\{0,1,\cdots,N\}
, and write
\Omega^{(a)}:=\Omega_{X^{(a)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
Recall that
(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})
induces a flat module

(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[a]},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[a]}\rightarrow\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[a]})
with vanishing
p
-curvature (cf. (
99
), Proposition
1.6.1
, (i) and (ii)).

(i)

The
following equalities of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules hold:

(392)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}_{\phi}])=f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}))=\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}_{\phi}))=0.

(ii)

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}_{\phi}])

is a vector bundle on
S
of rank
6g-6+3r
.
Also,

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}))
and
f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}_{\phi}))
) are
vector bundles on
S
of
rank
3g-3+r
and
3g-3+2r
, respectively.
In particular,
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))
is a vector bundle of rank
3g-3+r
.

Proof.

We shall prove both assertions (i) and (ii) by induction on
a
.
The base step. i.e.,
the case of
a=0
, was already proved in
[
Wak6
, Propositions 6.5, (ii), and 6.18]
.
To consider the induction step, we shall suppose that the assertions with
a
replaced by
a-1
(
a\geq 1
) have been proved.
For simplicity, we write

\mathcal{F}^{[a]}:=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[a]}
and
\nabla^{[a]}:=\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}
.

First, we shall consider assertion (i).
Since
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})\subseteq\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a-1]})
,
the equality

(393)

\displaystyle f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))=0

holds because of the induction hypothesis
f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))=0
.
On the other hand, let us consider the Hodge to de Rham spectral sequence

(394)

{}^{\prime}E_{1}^{q_{1},q_{2}}:=\mathbb{R}^{q_{2}}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{q_{1}}[\nabla^{[a]}])\Rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}+q_{2}}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])

associated to the complex
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}]
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Eq. (755)]
).
Since the relative dimension
\mathrm{dim}(X/S)
of
X/S
is
1
, the equality
\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})=0
holds.
It follows that
the sequence

(395)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\nabla^{[a]})}\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])\longrightarrow 0

induced by
(
394
) is exact.
By the comment following
[
Ogu1
, Proposition 1.2.4]
,

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]})
is isomorphic to

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))
.
But, the equality
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))=0
holds by
the induction hypothesis, so the exactness of (
395
) implies

(396)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])=0.

Moreover, consider
the conjugate spectral sequence

(397)

{}^{\prime\prime}E_{2}^{q_{1},q_{2}}:=\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}}f_{*}(\mathcal{H}^{q_{2}}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}]))\Rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}+q_{2}}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])

associated to
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}]
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Eq. (757)]
).
Since the equality
\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))=0
holds because of
\mathrm{dim}(X/S)=1
,
the morphism

\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))

induced by (
397
) is surjective.
Hence, it follows from (
396
) that

(398)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))=0.

By (
393
), (
396
), and (
398
),
the proof of assertion (i) is completed.

Next, we shall consider assertion (ii).
It follows from Proposition
1.6.2
, (i), that
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})
is a relatively torsion-free sheaf on
X^{(a+1)}
.
Also, since

\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]})
is isomorphic to
\Omega^{(a+1)}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})
(cf. the comment following
[
Ogu1
, Proposition 1.2.4]
),

\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]})
is a relatively torsion-free sheaf on
X^{(a+1)}
.
By the equality
\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))=0
and (
398
),
both
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))
and
f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))

turn out to be vector bundles (cf.
[
Har
, Chap. III, Theorem 12.11, (b)]
).
Moreover, the exactness of
the sequence

(399)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow f_{*}(\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]})\left(=f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))\right)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})\left(=\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))\right)\rightarrow 0

induced by (
394
) and the induction hypothesis together imply that

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])
is a vector bundle.

In what follows, we shall compute the ranks of the vector bundles under consideration.
To this end, we may assume that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
for an algebraically closed field
k
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Denote by
\mathscr{X}^{(a)}
the pointed stable curve obtained as the
a
-th Frobenius twist of
\mathscr{X}
over
k
, and by
D^{(a)}
the reduced effective divisor on
X^{(a)}
determined by the union of the marked points of
\mathscr{X}^{(a)}
.
For each flat module
(\mathcal{V},\nabla_{\mathcal{V}})
on
X^{(a)\mathrm{log}}/k^{\mathrm{log}}
,
we write
{{}^{c}}\mathcal{V}:=\mathcal{V}(-D^{[a]})
and write
{{}^{c}}\nabla_{\mathcal{V}}
for the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on
{{}^{c}}\mathcal{V}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{V}\right)
obtained by restricting
\nabla_{\mathcal{V}}
.
By
[
Wak6
, Corollary 6.16]
in the case where “
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
” is taken to be
(\mathcal{F}^{[a]},\nabla^{[a]})
,
there exists a canonical isomorphism of
k
-vector spaces

(400)

\displaystyle H^{1}(X^{(a)},\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{0}(X^{(a)},\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))^{\vee}.

In particular,
we have

(401)

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})))=h^{0}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]})).

Next, the Killing form on
\mathfrak{g}
induces an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-modules

(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee},(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{\vee})
.
This isomorphism restricts to an isomorphism of flat modules

(402)

\displaystyle({{}^{c}}\mathcal{F}^{[a]},{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}({{}^{c}}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee})^{[a]},{{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]}).

This isomorphism gives the equality
\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})=\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]})
, which implies

(403)

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}))=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]})).

Hence,
the following sequence of equalities holds:

(404)

\displaystyle h^{0}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{ddE1})}}{{=}}h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})))

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]}))-R_{a}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{dE155})}}{{=}}h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}))-R_{a}

\displaystyle=(h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+r+R_{a})-R_{a}

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+r

(cf. (
408
) for the definition of
R_{a}
),
where the second and fourth equalities follow from Lemma
6.1.3
described below.
On the other hand,
there exists a short exact sequence

(405)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow H^{1}(X^{(a)},\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))\longrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}(X^{(a)},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])\longrightarrow H^{0}(X^{(a)},\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))\longrightarrow 0

induced by (
397
).
This implies

(406)

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+h^{0}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{dim}(\mathbb{H}^{1}(X^{(a)},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}]))

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))+h^{0}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))

\displaystyle=(3g-3+r)+(3g-3+2r)

\displaystyle=6g-6+3r,

where the second equality follows from the exactness of (
399
) and the third equality follows from the induction hypothesis.
Consequently, it follows from
(
404
) and (
406
) that

(407)

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}))=3g-3+r,\ \ \ h^{0}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}))=3g-3+2r.

This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

The following lemma was applied in the proof of the above proposition.

Lemma 6.1.3
.

We shall keep the notation in the proof of the above proposition.
Let
a\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
, and
suppose that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
for an algebraically closed field
k
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Also, we shall set

(408)

\displaystyle R_{a}:=\sharp\left\{i\,\Big|\,1\leq i\leq r,\ (\lambda_{i})_{[a]}\in\{0,p-1\}\left(\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}\right)\right\}

(cf. §
3.3
for the definition of
(-)_{[a]}
).
Then,
the following two equalities hold:

(409)

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+r+R_{a},

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]}))

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})))+R_{a}.

Proof.

To begin with, we prepare some notations.
Let
U_{\oslash}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(N-1)}
be as in (
181
) and (
182
), respectively.
Let
\mathscr{V}:=(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
be a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module (resp., a
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(N-1)}
-module) such that
\psi(\nabla)=0
and
\mathcal{V}
is a vector bundle of rank
n>0
.
In the non-resp’d portion,
({{}^{c}}\mathcal{V}^{[a]},{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})
is defined as in the proof of the above proposition.
Also,
in the resp’d portion, we set
{{}^{c}}\mathcal{V}^{[a]}:=t^{p^{a}}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{[a]}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{[a]}\right)
, which has an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}
obtained by restricting
\nabla^{[a]}
.
Then, the inclusion

{{}^{c}}\mathcal{V}^{[a]}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}^{[a]}
restricts to an inclusion

(410)

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathscr{V}}^{[a]}:\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}).

On the other hand, the natural morphism

{{}^{c}}(\mathcal{V}^{\vee})^{[a]}\rightarrow{{}^{c}}(\mathcal{V}^{[a]\vee})

restricts to a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(a+1)}}
-modules

(411)

\displaystyle\beta_{\mathscr{V}}^{[a]}:\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})).

In what follows, we shall examine the morphisms
\alpha_{\mathscr{V}}^{[a]}
and
\beta_{\mathscr{V}}^{[a]}
in the case where
\mathscr{V}
is taken to be the
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(N-1)}
-module
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}^{(N-1)}:=(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(N-1)})
for

d\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
(cf. (
207
)).
For simplicity, write
\mathscr{O}_{d}:=\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}^{(N-1)}
and
\nabla_{d}:=\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(N-1)}
.
First,
a straightforward calculation shows

(412)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}_{d})=\begin{cases}t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{(a+1)}_{\oslash}&\text{if $d_{[a]}\neq 0$};\\
t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}+p^{a+1}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{(a+1)}_{\oslash}&\text{if $d_{[a]}=0$}.\end{cases}

Hence, it follows from Proposition
3.3.1
that
\alpha_{\mathscr{O}_{d}}^{[a]}
is injective and its cokernel satisfies

(413)

\displaystyle\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}}(\mathrm{Coker}(\alpha^{[a]}_{\mathscr{O}_{d}}))=\begin{cases}0&\text{if $d_{[a]}\neq 0$};\\
1&\text{if $d_{[a]}=0$}.\end{cases}

Also, by putting

c:=-d
, we see (from
d\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
)
that

(414)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla_{d}^{\vee})^{[a]})\left(=\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla_{c}^{[a]})\right)=\begin{cases}t^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}&\text{if
$c_{[a]}\neq 0\left(\Leftrightarrow d_{[a]}\neq p-1\right)$};\\
t^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a]}+p^{a+1}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}&\text{if
$c_{[a]}=0\left(\Leftrightarrow d_{[a]}=p-1\right)$}.\end{cases}

and that

(415)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla_{d}^{[a]})^{\vee})=t^{-\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}+p^{a+1}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}\left(=t^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}\right).

Hence,

\beta_{\mathscr{O}_{d}}^{[a]}
is injective and its cokernel satisfies

(416)

\displaystyle\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}}(\mathrm{Coker}(\beta^{[a]}_{\mathscr{O}_{d}}))=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $d_{[a]}=p-1$};\\
0&\text{if $d_{[a]}\neq p-1$}.\end{cases}

The same arguments with
d
replaced by
c
give

(417)

\displaystyle\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}}(\mathrm{Coker}(\alpha^{[a]}_{\mathscr{O}_{-d}}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0&\text{if $d_{[a]}\neq p-1$};\\
1&\text{if $d_{[a]}=p-1$},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}}(\mathrm{Coker}(\beta^{[a]}_{\mathscr{O}_{-d}}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $d_{[a]}=0$};\\
0&\text{if $d_{[a]}\neq 0$}.\end{cases}

Now, let us go back to our situation.
We shall set
(\mathcal{F},\nabla):=(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})
.
Both
\alpha^{[a]}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}
and
\beta^{[a]}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}
become isomorphisms
when restricted to
X\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
.
Given each
d\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
, we shall set

(418)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{d}^{[a]}:=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}/(t^{p^{a+1}})&\text{if $d_{[a]}\in\{0,p-1\}$};\\
0&\text{if $d_{[a]}\notin\{0,p-1\}$}.\end{cases}

For each
i=1,\cdots,r
, denote by
\widehat{U}_{i}
the formal neighborhood of
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
in
X
, and by

\mathrm{incl}_{i}:\widehat{U}_{i}\rightarrow X
the natural morphism.
It follows from Proposition
6.1.1
that
the restriction
(\mathcal{F}|_{\widehat{U}_{i}},\nabla|_{\widehat{U}_{i}})
of
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
to
\widehat{U}_{i}

is isomorphic to

(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{\oplus 3},\nabla_{\oslash,0}^{(N-1)}\oplus\nabla_{\oslash,\lambda_{i}}^{(N-1)}\oplus\nabla_{\oslash,-\lambda_{i}}^{(N-1)})
via a fixed identification
\widehat{U}_{i}=U_{\oslash}
.
By (
413
) and the first equality of (
417
),
the cokernel of
\alpha_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}^{[a]}
restricted to
\widehat{U}_{i}
is isomorphic
to
\mathcal{Q}^{[a]}_{\lambda_{i}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}/(t^{p^{a+1}})
.
This implies that
\alpha^{[a]}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}
fits into the short exact sequence

(419)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})\xrightarrow{\alpha^{[a]}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}}\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{incl}_{i*}(\mathcal{Q}^{[a]}_{\lambda_{i}}\oplus\mathcal{O}^{(a+1)}_{\oslash}/(t^{p^{a+1}}))\longrightarrow 0.

Here, we shall write
H^{j}(-):=H^{j}(X^{(a+1)},-)
(
j\geq 0
) for simplicity.
Since
H^{0}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))=0
(cf. (
393
)),
the sequence (
419
) yields a short exact sequence of
k
-vector spaces

(420)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}H^{0}(\mathrm{incl}_{i*}(\mathcal{Q}^{[a]}_{\lambda_{i}}\oplus\mathcal{O}^{(a+1)}_{\oslash}/(t^{p^{a+1}})))\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}))\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))\rightarrow 0.

This sequence implies

(421)

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}))

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{dim}(H^{0}(\mathrm{incl}_{i*}(\mathcal{Q}^{[a]}_{\lambda_{i}}\oplus\mathcal{O}^{(a+1)}_{\oslash}/(t^{p^{a+1}}))))

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+\sum_{i=1}^{r}(\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}}(\mathcal{Q}^{[a]}_{\lambda_{i}})+1)

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+R_{a}+r.

Thus, we have proved the first equality in (
409
).
Moreover, (
416
) and the second equality of (
417
) together imply

(422)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{\vee[a]})\xrightarrow{\beta^{[a]}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}}\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]\vee})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{incl}_{i*}(\mathcal{Q}^{[a]}_{\lambda_{i}})\longrightarrow 0.

Similarly to the above argument,
we obtain from (
422
) the equality

(423)

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]}))=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})))+R_{a},

which is the second equality in (
409
).
Thus, we have finished the proof of this lemma.
∎

6.2.
Horizontal sections of the adjoint bundle

Just as in the case of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
,
we can consider

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodules of
\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})
.
We shall write

(424)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}\right)

for the subsheaf of

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
) consisting of local sections
v
such that the equality
[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),v]=0
(resp.,
[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),v]-\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,d_{\mathcal{E}}(v)])=0
) holds for any
D\in\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
.
In particular, we have
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})
.
Both
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
may be regarded as
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N)}}
-modules via the underlying homeomorphism of

F^{(N)}_{X/S}:X\rightarrow X^{(N)}
.
Denote by

(425)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\phi}:\mathcal{T}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}

the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on
\mathcal{E}
obtained by reducing the level of
\phi
to
0
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Definition 1.17]
and (
93
)).

The following lemma will be applied in the proof of Lemma

6.2.3
described later.

Lemma 6.2.1
.

(i)

The inclusion

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
restricts to an injective morphism

(426)

\displaystyle\mathrm{incl}_{\mathscr{E}^{\blacklozenge}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi},

and the image of
\nabla_{\phi}
is contained in
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}\left(\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\right)
.
In particular,
the sequence

(427)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{incl}_{\mathscr{E}^{\blacklozenge}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}\longrightarrow 0

is exact and split by the morphism
\nabla_{\phi}
.

(ii)

The
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})
forms a vector bundle.

Proof.

We shall consider assertion (i).
The first assertion follows immediately from the definitions of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
.
Moreover,
for any local sections
v\in\mathcal{T}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
, we have

(428)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),\nabla_{\phi}(v)]-\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,d_{\mathcal{E}}(\nabla_{\phi}(v))])=[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),\phi^{\natural\natural}(v)]-\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,v])=0.

This implies the inclusion relation
\mathrm{Im}(\nabla_{\phi})\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
.
The last assertion of (i) follows from the first two assertions.

Next, we shall prove assertion (ii).
By Proposition
1.6.2
, (i),

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}\left(=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})\right)
is flat over
S
.
Since the equality
\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})=0
holds,

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})
turns out to be a vector bundle (cf.
[
Har
, Chap. III, Theorem 12.11, (b)]
).
On the other hand, the short exact sequence (
427
) induces the short exact sequence

(429)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{T})\longrightarrow 0.

Both
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})
and
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{T})
are vector bundles, so the exactness of this sequence implies that
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})
is a vector bundle.
This completes the proof of assertion (ii).
∎

Next, denote by

(430)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}

the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on the vector bundle
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}

obtained by reducing the level of
\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}
to
0
.
In other words,
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi}
is obtained from
\nabla_{\phi}
via
change of structure group by the adjoint representation
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}(\mathfrak{g})
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Eq. (745)]
).
Also, denote by

(431)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\rightarrow\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}

the unique
f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-linear morphism determined by the condition that

(432)

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{1},\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi}(\partial_{2})\rangle=[\nabla_{\phi}(\partial_{1}),\partial_{2}]-\nabla_{\phi}([\partial_{1},d_{\mathcal{E}}(\partial_{2})])

for any local sections
\partial_{1}\in\mathcal{T}
and
\partial_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
, where
\langle-,-\rangle
denotes the pairing
\mathcal{T}\times(\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
arising from the natural pairing
\mathcal{T}\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
The restriction of
\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi}
to
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\left(\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\right)
coincides with
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi}
.
Also, we obtain the composite

(433)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi}}\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
denotes the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module obtained from the
B
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{B}
in the same manner as
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
The pair of the natural inclusion

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}

and the identity morphism of
\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
defines
an inclusion of complexes

(434)

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}:\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}].

On the other hand, since
\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}
becomes the zero map when restricted to
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}\left(\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\right)
, we obtain
an inclusion

(435)

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}[0]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}].

For each
i\geq 0
, we shall set

(436)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{i}:=\mathrm{Ker}\left(\mathbb{R}^{i}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi,B}])\oplus\mathbb{R}^{i}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{R}^{i}f_{*}(\gamma_{1}\oplus(-\gamma_{2}))}\mathbb{R}^{i}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}])\right).

Then, we obtain the composite

(437)

\displaystyle D_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}:\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi,B}])\oplus\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi,B}])

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{T}),

where the last arrow arises from
d_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}_{B}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
.

The following two lemmas will be applied in the proof of Proposition
6.2.4
described below.

Lemma 6.2.2
.

Denote by
\xi
the composite
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}/\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}
.
Then,
\xi
is injective and the direct image of its cokernel
f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))
is a vector bundle on
S
.

Proof.

First, we shall prove the injectivity of
\xi
.
Let us take a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j=1}^{2})
on
\mathscr{X}
with
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}\cong\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
(cf. Corollary
4.5.2
, (i)).
Denote by
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{\nabla}
) the subsheaf of
\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{F})
consisting of endomorphisms with vanishing trace (resp., endomorphisms with vanishing trace preserving
\nabla
).
Under the natural identification
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
,
the sheaf
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{\nabla}
corresponds to
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}
.
Also, the surjection
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}/\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}
corresponds to the morphism
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{F}^{1},\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{F}^{1})
induced by composing with
\mathcal{F}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{F}^{1}
.
Thus,
\xi
may be identified with the composite

(438)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{\nabla}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{F}^{1},\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{F}^{1}).

Let
U
be an open subscheme of
X
and
h
an element of
\Gamma(U,\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F})^{\nabla})

which is mapped to the zero map via (
438
).
The inclusion relation
h(\mathcal{F}^{1}|_{U})\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{1}|_{U}
holds,
and the endomorphism of
\mathcal{F}^{1}|_{U}
induced by
h
is given by multiplication by some element
a\in\Gamma(U,\mathcal{O}_{X})
.
Since
the morphism (
259
)
associated to
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
is an isomorphism and
h
preserves the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure,
the endomorphism
h
itself is given by multiplication by
a
.
However,
h
has vanishing trace, so we have
a=0
.
That is to say, the equality
h=0
holds.
This proves the injectivity of
\xi
.

Next, we shall prove the remaining assertion.
Note that the formation of
\xi
is functorial with respect to base-change over
S
-schemes.
Hence, the above discussion implies that
\xi
is universally injective relative to
S
.
By
[
Mat
, p. 17, Theorem 1]
,
\mathrm{Coker}(\xi)
turns out to be flat over
S
.
In particular,
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))
is a vector bundle on
S
because the relative dimension of
X/S
equals
1
(cf.
[
Har
, Chap. III, Theorem 12.11, (b)]
).
Also, by the equality
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}=\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})
together with Proposition
1.6.2
, (i),
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}
is flat over
S
.
This implies that
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})
is a vector bundle.
The short exact sequence
0\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}\xrightarrow{\xi}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}/\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Coker}(\xi)\rightarrow 0
yields an exact sequence of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules

(439)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}/\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}})\left(\cong\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{T})\right)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))\rightarrow 0,

where the exactness at
f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))
follows from the fact that
f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}/\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}})\cong f_{*}(\mathcal{T})=0
.
Since the sheaves appearing in this sequence except for
f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))

are all vector bundles,

f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))
turns out to be a vector bundle.
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

Lemma 6.2.3
.

We shall write

(440)

\displaystyle\gamma:=\gamma_{1}\oplus(-\gamma_{2}):\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\oplus\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}[0]\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}].

Also, write
\mathrm{Im}(\gamma)
for the subcomplex of
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]
defined as the image of
\gamma
.
Then, the complex
\mathrm{Coker}(\xi)[0]
fits into the following short exact sequence:

(441)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\gamma)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\longrightarrow\mathrm{Coker}(\xi)[0]\longrightarrow 0.

Proof.

Denote by
\nabla_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}:\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}\rightarrow\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
the morphism obtained by restricting
the domain of
\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}
(i.e.,
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
) to
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}
.
Then, we obtain the following diagram of complexes:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\gamma}_{1}\oplus(-\overline{\gamma}_{2})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi[0]}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}/\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where

•

the second (resp., third) upper horizontal arrow denotes the inclusion into the first factor (resp., the projection onto the second factor);

•

\overline{\gamma}_{1}
(resp.,
\overline{\gamma}_{2}
) denotes the natural inclusion

\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]
(resp.,
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}[0]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]
);

•

the third lower horizontal arrow denotes the morphism induced by the natural surjection
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}/\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}
and the zero map
\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow 0
.

Since the two horizontal sequences are exact,
it follows from the snake lemma that
there exists an isomorphism

(446)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Coker}(\overline{\gamma}_{1}\oplus(-\overline{\gamma}_{2}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Coker}(\xi)[0].

Next, let us consider the following diagram:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl.}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\gamma}_{1}\oplus(-\overline{\gamma}_{2})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\oplus\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{1}\oplus\delta_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma\left(=\gamma_{1}\oplus(-\gamma_{2})\right)}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}[0]\oplus\mathcal{T}[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\oplus(-\mathrm{id})}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where

•

\delta_{1}
is the morphism induced from
d_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
and the zero map
\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow 0
;

•

\delta_{2}
is the morphism induced from the composite of the inclusion
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
and
d_{\mathcal{E}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{T}
;

•

the third lower horizontal arrow denotes the morphism induced from
d_{\mathcal{E}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{T}
and the zero map
\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow 0
.

The two horizontal sequences in this diagram are exact.
Here, denote by

\varsigma_{1}
the morphism
\mathcal{T}[0]\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]
determined by
\nabla_{\phi}^{(0)}
, and by
\varsigma_{2}
the morphism
\mathcal{T}[0]\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}[0]
determined by the split injection of (
427
) resulting from Lemma
6.2.1
, (i).
Since the direct sum
\varsigma_{1}\oplus\varsigma_{2}
specifies a split injection of the upper horizontal sequence in (
6.2
),
it follows from the snake lemma again that there exists an isomorphism

(451)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Coker}(\overline{\gamma}_{1}\oplus(-\overline{\gamma}_{2}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Coker}(\gamma).

Thus, the assertion follows from (
446
) and (
451
).
∎

Proposition 6.2.4
.

The equality
\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{i}=0
holds except for
i=1
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1}
is a vector bundle on
S
and the formation of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1}
commutes with base-change over
S
-schemes.

Proof.

First, we shall prove the assertion for
i=0
.
Let us consider
the short exact sequence of complexes

(452)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]\xrightarrow{(d_{\mathcal{E}},0)}\mathcal{T}[0]\longrightarrow 0.

As already proved in
[
Wak6
, Proposition 6.5]
, the equality
\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}])=0
holds.
Hence,
by applying the functor
\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(-)
to (
452
), we obtain

(453)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}])\left(=f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}))\right)=0.

Both
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]

and
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}[0]
are subcomplexes of

\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}]
, so
(
453
) implies

(454)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}])=\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})=0.

Since
\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{0}
is a subsheaf of the direct sum

\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}])\oplus\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})
, we have
\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{0}=0
.

Next, we shall prove the assertion for
i=1
.
Note that the short exact sequence (
441
) obtained in Lemma
6.2.3
induces
an exact sequence of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules

(455)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma))\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}])\longrightarrow f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma))\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}])\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))\longrightarrow 0.

The exactness at
\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma))
in this sequence and (
453
) together imply the equality

(456)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{0}f_{*}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma))=0.

The sheaves appearing in this sequence except for
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma))

are all vector bundles (cf. Lemma
6.2.2
),
so
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma))
turns out to be a vector bundle.
Also, let us consider the short exact sequence

(457)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ker}(\gamma)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi,B}]\oplus\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}[0]\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathrm{Im}(\gamma)\longrightarrow 0.

Since
\gamma
is an isomorphism at degree
1
, the complex
\mathrm{Ker}(\gamma)
is concentrated at degree
0
only.
This implies
\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\gamma))=0
, and hence (
457
) induces
the following short exact sequence

(458)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\gamma))\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi,B}])\oplus\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma))\longrightarrow 0,

where the exactness at
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\gamma))
follows from (
456
).
Since
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi,B}])
,
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})
, and
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma))

are vector bundles (cf.
[
Wak6
, Eq. (788)]
and Lemma
6.2.1
, (ii)), we see that
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\gamma))
is a vector bundle.
Here, consider the following diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\gamma))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi,B}])\oplus\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}_{\phi,B}])\oplus\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}])}

where the right-hand and left-hand vertical arrows are the natural morphisms, and the upper horizontal sequence is (
458
).
By the snake lemma applied to this diagram and the exactness of (
455
), we obtain the short exact sequence

(463)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\gamma))\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}\longrightarrow f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))\longrightarrow 0.

Since both
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\gamma))
and
f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))
are vector bundles on
S
(cf. Lemma
6.2.2
),
\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1}
turns out to be a vector bundle.
Moreover, it follows from the various definitions involved that the formations of
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\gamma))
and

f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\xi))
commute with base-change over
S
-schemes.
Hence, the exactness of (
463
) implies the required commutativity of the formation of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1}
.
This completes the proof of the assertion for
i=1
.

Finally, the assertion for
i\geq 2
follows immediately from the fact that
\mathbb{R}^{i}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}])=\mathbb{R}^{i}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})=0
for every
i\geq 2
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Lemma 6.6]
).
∎

6.3.
Deformation space of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper

In this subsection, we suppose that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
for an algebraically closed field
k
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
We will regard
\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{i}
(
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) as
k
-vector spaces.
Denote by

\mathcal{A}rt_{/k}
the category of local Artinian
k
-algebras with residue field
k
.
For each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{A}rt_{/k})
, we write
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
for the maximal ideal of
R
.

Definition 6.3.1
.

Let
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{A}rt_{/k})
.
A
deformation of
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})

over
R
is a pair

(464)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{X}_{R},\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}),

where

•

\mathscr{X}_{R}
denotes an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
over
R
equipped with an isomorphism
\nu:\mathscr{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}k\times_{R}\mathscr{X}
between
\mathscr{X}
and the reduction
k\times_{R}\mathscr{X}_{R}
of
\mathscr{X}_{R}
modulo
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
;

•

\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R}
denotes a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}_{R}
whose reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
corresponds to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
via

\nu
.

The notion of an isomorphism between two deformations of
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})

can be defined in a natural manner (so the precise definition is left to the reader).

Recall that any deformation of the pointed stable curve
\mathscr{X}
(or equivalently, the log curve
X^{\mathrm{log}}/k^{\mathrm{log}}
) is locally trivial (cf.
[
Wak6
, § 6.1.2]
).
Also, the local triviality of deformations holds for a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper, as shown in the following proposition.

Proposition 6.3.2
.

Let
R
be an element of
\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{A}rt_{/k})
and

(\mathscr{X}_{R},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R})
, where
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R}:=((\mathcal{E}_{B})_{R},\phi_{R})
, a deformation of
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
over
R
.
We shall write
\mathcal{E}_{R}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B})_{R}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
.
Then, the
(N-1)
-PD stratified
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-bundle
(\mathcal{E}_{R},\phi_{R})
is, Zariski locally on
X
, isomorphic to the trivial deformation of
(\mathcal{E},\phi)
(i.e., the base-change of
(\mathcal{E},\phi)
over
R
).

Proof.

We shall prove the assertion by double induction on
N
and
l:=\mathrm{dim}(R)
.
There is nothing to prove when
l=1
, i.e.,
R=k
.
Also, the assertion for
N=1
can be proved by an argument entirely similar to the proof of
[
Wak6
, Corollary 6.12]
.

Next, we shall consider the induction step.
Suppose that
N\geq 2
,
l\geq 2
, and that we have proved the assertion in the case where the level of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
and the dimension of
R
are smaller than
N
and
l
, respectively.
To clarify the level of PD stratifications,
we write
\phi^{(N-1)}:=\phi
,
\phi_{R}^{(N-1)}:=\phi_{R}
, and use the notation
(-)^{(N^{\prime})}
(
N^{\prime}<N
) to denote the result of reducing the level of an
(N-1)
-PD stratification
(-)
to
N^{\prime}
.
Also, we use the notation
R\times_{k}(-)
to denote the result of base-changing objects over
k
via
k\rightarrow R
.

Let us take a nonzero element
\varepsilon
of
R
with
\varepsilon^{2}=0
,
and write
R_{0}:=R/(\varepsilon)
.
By the induction hypothesis,
each point
q
of
X
has an open neighborhood
U\left(\subseteq X\right)

on which

((\mathcal{E}_{B})_{R},\phi^{(N-2)}_{R})
is isomorphic to the base-change
(R\times_{k}\mathcal{E}_{B},R\times_{k}\phi^{(N-2)})
of the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N-1)}
-oper
(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi^{(N-2)})
.
We shall fix an identification

(465)

\displaystyle((\mathcal{E}_{B})_{R}|_{U},\phi^{(N-2)}_{R}|_{U})=(R\times_{k}\mathcal{E}_{B}|_{U},R\times_{k}\phi^{(N-2)}|_{U}),

by which we regard
\phi_{R}^{(N-1)}|_{U}
as an
(N-1)
-PD stratification on
(R\times_{k}\mathcal{E}|_{U})/U^{\mathrm{log}}/k^{\mathrm{log}}
(where
U^{\mathrm{log}}:=U\times_{X}X^{\mathrm{log}}
) satisfying the equality

(466)

\displaystyle\phi_{R}^{(N-2)}|_{U}=R\times_{k}\phi^{(N-2)}|_{U}.

If

\phi_{R_{0}}^{(N-1)}

denotes the reduction modulo
\varepsilon
of

\phi_{R}^{(N-1)}
,
then the induction hypothesis asserts that
(R_{0}\times_{k}\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi_{R_{0}}^{(N-1)})
is locally isomorphic to the base-change
(R_{0}\times_{k}\mathcal{E}_{B},R_{0}\times_{k}\phi^{(N-1)})
of
(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi^{(N-1)})
over
R_{0}
.
Hence, after possibly shrinking
U
, we may assume that
there exists an isomorphism

(467)

\displaystyle(R_{0}\times_{k}\mathcal{E}_{B}|_{U},\phi_{R_{0}}^{(N-1)}|_{U})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(R_{0}\times_{k}\mathcal{E}_{B}|_{U},R_{0}\times_{k}\phi^{(N-1)}|_{U}).

By Proposition
4.2.4
,
this isomorphism coincides, via reducing the level to
N-2
, with the identity morphism of

(R_{0}\times_{k}\mathcal{E}_{B}|_{U},R_{0}\times_{k}\phi^{(N-2)}|_{U})
under the identification (
465
).
This implies the equality

(468)

\displaystyle\phi_{R_{0}}^{(N-1)}|_{U}=R_{0}\times_{k}\phi^{(N-1)}|_{U}.

It follows that there exists a well-defined
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism

h:\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}|_{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})|_{U}
satisfying

(469)

\displaystyle\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural(N-1)}|_{U}=R\times_{k}\phi^{\natural\natural(N-1)}|_{U}+\varepsilon\cdot h

(cf. Remark
1.3.4
for the definition of
(-)^{\natural\natural}
).
According to (
466
),
the composite

(470)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{(N-2)}_{\leq p^{N-1}-1}|_{U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq p^{N-1}-1}|_{U}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)}|_{U}\xrightarrow{h}\mathcal{E}nd_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})|_{U}

coincides with the zero map.
Hence,
h
induces
an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism

(471)

\displaystyle\overline{h}:F^{(N-1)*}_{X/k}(\mathcal{T}^{(N-1)})|_{U}\left(=\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{N-1}}|_{U}=(\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)}/\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}-1}^{(N-1)})|_{U}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})|_{U},

where
\mathcal{T}^{(N-1)}:=\mathcal{T}_{X^{(N-1)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
Since
\varepsilon^{2}=0
and
h
is
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-equivariant (in an evident sense),
the image of
\overline{h}
lies in

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\left(=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}/X})^{\mathrm{PGL}_{2}}\right)
.
Moreover, (
466
) implies that
\overline{h}
restricts to a morphism

(472)

\displaystyle\overline{h}^{\nabla}:\mathcal{T}^{(N-1)}|_{U}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]}|_{U}.

We shall use the same notation
\overline{h}^{\nabla}
to denote the induced
\mathcal{O}_{U^{(N-1)}}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]}|_{U}\rightarrow\Omega^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]}|_{U}
(where
\Omega^{(N-1)}:=\mathcal{T}^{(N-1)\vee}
) via the natural morphism
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N-1)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]})
.
If
\nabla_{R}
(resp.,
R\times_{k}\nabla
) denotes the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on the
\mathcal{O}_{U^{(N-1)}}
-module
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}|_{U}
induced by
\phi_{R}^{(N-1)}|_{U}
(resp.,
R\times_{k}\phi^{(N-1)}|_{U}
), then
(
469
) implies

\nabla_{R}=R\times_{k}\nabla+\varepsilon\cdot\overline{h}^{\nabla}
.
Since both
\nabla_{R}
and
R\times_{k}\nabla
have vanishing
p
-curvature (cf. Proposition
1.6.1
, (ii)),
it follows from Lemma
6.3.3
described below that
we can find, after possibly shrinking
U
, an element
v\in\Gamma(U,\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]})
satisfying

(473)

\displaystyle(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{[N-1]}(v)=\overline{h}^{\nabla}.

Denote by
h_{v}
the automorphism of
R\times_{k}\mathcal{E}
corresponding to the automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{R\times_{k}\mathcal{E}}
described as
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{R\times_{k}\mathcal{E}}}-\varepsilon\cdot v
.
Also, denote by
h_{v}^{*}(R\times_{k}\phi^{\natural\natural}|_{U})^{(N-1)}
the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}_{R\times_{k}\mathcal{E}|_{U}}
defined as the pull-back of
R\times_{k}\phi^{(N-1)\natural\natural}|_{U}
by
h_{v}
.
Since
v
belongs to
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]}
,
we have

(474)

\displaystyle h_{v}^{*}(R\times_{k}\phi^{\natural\natural}|_{U})^{(N-2)}=\left(R\times_{k}\phi^{\natural\natural(N-2)}|_{U}=\right)\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural(N-2)}|_{U}.

On the other hand, the equality (
473
) together with
the same argument as
[
Wak6
, Remark 6.2]
shows that

(475)

\displaystyle h_{v}^{*}(R\times_{k}\phi^{\natural\natural}|_{U})^{[N-1]}=\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural[N-1]}|_{U}.

By (
474
) and (
475
), we can apply Lemma
2.1.2
to obtain
the equality
h_{v}^{*}(R\times_{k}\phi^{\natural\natural}|_{U})^{(N-1)}=\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural(N-1)}|_{U}
.
This means that
\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural(N-1)}|_{U}
can be transformed into the trivial deformation of
\phi^{\natural\natural(N-1)}
via
a suitable trivialization
\mathcal{E}_{R}|_{U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}R\times_{k}\mathcal{E}|_{U}
.
By applying this argument to various points
q
of
X
,
we finish the proof of this proposition.
∎

The following lemma was used in the proof of the above proposition.

Lemma 6.3.3
.

Let us keep the notation in the proof of Proposition
6.3.2
.
Then, the
p
-curvature of
\nabla_{R}
satisfies the equality

(476)

\displaystyle\psi(\nabla_{R})=-\varepsilon\cdot C_{(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]},(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{[N-1]})}(\overline{h}^{\nabla})

under the composite of natural inclusions

(477)

\displaystyle\Gamma(U^{(N)},\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N]})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{U^{(N)}}}(\mathcal{T}^{[N]}|_{U^{(N)}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N]}|_{U^{(N)}})

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{U^{(N)}}}(\mathcal{T}^{[N]}|_{U^{(N)}},\mathcal{E}nd_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})|_{U^{(N)}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{U^{(N-1)}}}(F_{X^{(N-1)}/k}^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{[N]})|_{U^{(N-1)}},\mathcal{E}nd_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})|_{U^{(N-1)}}).

In particular, if the
p
-curvature of
\nabla_{R}
vanishes, then
\overline{h}^{\nabla}
may be expressed, Zariski locally on
X
, as
\overline{h}^{\nabla}=(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{[N-1]}(v)
for some local section
v
of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]}
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from computations similar to those carried out in the proof of
[
Wak6
, Proposition 6.11]
.
So we omit the details of the proof.
∎

Remark 6.3.4

(Higher rank cases)
.

The same assertion as Proposition
6.3.2
also holds for
a general rank
n\left(<p\right)
not only the case of
n=2
.
In fact, it can be proved by a similar argument
because the result of Proposition
4.2.4
, which was essentially used in the proof, can be applied to
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers.

For each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{A}rt_{/k}
), we shall set

(478)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R):=\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes}\\
\text{of deformations of $(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})$ over $R$}\end{matrix}\right).

If
\sigma:R^{\prime}\rightarrow R
is a morphism in
\mathcal{A}rt_{/k}
,
then the base-change along the induced morphism
\mathrm{Spec}(R)\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(R^{\prime})
gives a map of sets
\sigma^{*}:\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)
.
Thus, the assignments
R\mapsto\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)

and
\sigma\mapsto\sigma^{*}
determines a functor

(479)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}:\mathcal{A}rt_{/k}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}et.

Theorem 6.3.5
.

The functor
\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}
is a rigid deformation functor with good deformation theory, in the sense of
[
Wak5
, § 6.1]
.
Moreover, the triple

(480)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})},\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1},\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{2})

forms a deformation triple, in the sense of
[
Wak5
, Definition 6.1.1]
, i.e.,

\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}
has a tangent-obstruction theory by putting

\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1}
as the tangent space and

\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{2}
as the obstruction space.
In particular,
\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}
is smooth and pro-representable
by
k[\![t_{1},\cdots,t_{\mathrm{dim}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1})}]\!]
(cf.
[
Wak5
, Remark 6.1.3, (i)]
).

Proof.

First, we shall consider the tangent space of
\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}
.
Let
\mathfrak{e}:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\rightarrow R_{0}\rightarrow 0)
be a small extension in

\mathcal{A}rt_{/k}
and
(\mathscr{X}_{R},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R})
(where
\mathscr{X}_{R}:=(X_{R}/R,\{\sigma_{R,i}\}_{i})
and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R}:=((\mathcal{E}_{B})_{R},\phi_{R})
) a deformation of
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
over
R
.
Set
\mathcal{E}_{R}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B})_{R}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
.
Also, let us take an element
v
of
M\otimes_{k}\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1}
, which may be described as
v=(v_{1},v_{2})
for
v_{1}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}])
,
v_{2}\in H^{1}(X,M\otimes_{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/k^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})
,
where
\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}
denotes the tensor product

M\otimes_{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}^{\mathrm{log}}/k^{\mathrm{log}}}\rightarrow M\otimes_{k}(\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})

of
\mathrm{id}_{M}
and
\widetilde{\nabla}_{\phi,B}^{\mathrm{ad}(0)}
.
Note here that

H^{1}(X,M\otimes_{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/k^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})

can be realized as the cohomology of the Čech complex of

M\otimes_{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/k^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
associated with
an affine covering.
That is to say, we can find an affine open covering
\mathcal{U}:=\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
(where

I
is a finite index set) of
X
, and
v
may be represented
by
a Čech
1
-cocycle

(481)

\displaystyle v_{2}=\{\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}\}_{(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}}\in\check{C}^{1}(\mathcal{U},M\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/k^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})

where
I_{2}
denotes the subset of
I\times I
consisting of pairs
(\alpha,\beta)
with
U_{\alpha\beta}:=U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
, and
\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}\in\Gamma(U_{\alpha\beta},M\otimes_{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/k^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})
.
For each
(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}
,
we set
\partial_{\alpha\beta}:=d_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}(\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta})\in\Gamma(U_{\alpha\beta},M\otimes_{k}\mathcal{T})
.
Also, we shall set
U_{R,\alpha}^{\mathrm{log}}:=X_{R}^{\mathrm{log}}|_{U_{\alpha}}
for each
\alpha\in I
and set

U_{R,\alpha\beta}^{\mathrm{log}}:=X_{R}^{\mathrm{log}}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}
for each
(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}
.
The element
\partial_{\alpha\beta}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}
) may be regarded as a
k
-linear morphism

(482)

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha\beta}:\mathcal{O}_{U_{\alpha\beta}}\rightarrow M\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{\alpha\beta}}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}}\rightarrow M\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}}\right)

satisfying
the Leibniz rule.
Then,

\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}}+\partial_{\alpha\beta}
(resp.,
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{R}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}}}+\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}
) defines a well-defined automorphism
\partial^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
) of
U_{R,\alpha\beta}^{\mathrm{log}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\mathrm{log}}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}
).
By means of the
\partial_{\alpha\beta}^{\sharp}
’s (
(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}
), we can glue together the
U_{R,\alpha}^{\mathrm{log}}
’s (
\alpha\in I
) to
obtain a log curve
X^{\mathrm{log}}_{R,v}
over
R\times_{k}S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
It determines a deformation
\mathscr{X}_{R,v}
of
\mathscr{X}
over
R
(cf.
[
FKa
, Theorem 4.1]
).
Also, the automorphism
\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
is
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-equivariant, and
the following square diagram is commutative and Cartesian:

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\mathrm{log}}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\mathrm{log}}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{U_{R,\alpha\beta}^{\mathrm{log}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}}
\textstyle{U_{R,\alpha\beta}^{\mathrm{log}}.}

For each local section
D\in\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}^{\mathrm{log}}/R^{\mathrm{log}}}
,
the following sequence of equalities holds:

(487)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt(\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta})^{*}(\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}})(D)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id}+\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta})\circ\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}(D-[\partial_{\alpha\beta},D])\circ(\mathrm{id}+\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id}+\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta})\circ\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}(D-[\partial_{\alpha\beta},D])\circ(\mathrm{id}-\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta})

\displaystyle=\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}(D)-\left([\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}(D),\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}]-\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}([D,\partial_{\alpha\beta}])\right)

\displaystyle=\phi_{R}^{\natural\natural}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}(D)

(cf.
[
Wak6
, Remark 6.2]
for the first equality), where the last equality follows from
\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}\in M\otimes_{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/k^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
.
This means that
\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
preserves the
(N-1)
-PD stratification
\phi_{R}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}
.
Hence,
the pairs
(\mathcal{E}_{R}|_{U_{R,\alpha}},\phi_{R}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}})

may be glued together by means of
the
\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
’s;
we denote the resulting
(N-1)
-PD stratified
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-bundle by
(\mathcal{E}_{R,v},\phi_{R,v})
.
By construction,
\phi_{R,v}
has vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature.

On the other hand,
according to
[
Wak6
, Eq. (792)]
,
the element
v_{1}
determines a deformation
((\mathcal{E}_{B})_{R,v},(\nabla_{\phi})_{R,v})
over
R
of the
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper
(\mathcal{E}_{B},\nabla_{\phi})
.
Since
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
agree under the maps
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\gamma_{1})
and
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\gamma_{2})
,
we see that
(\mathcal{E}_{B})_{R,v}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{2}\cong\mathcal{E}_{R,v}
and that the
k^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on
\mathcal{E}_{R,v}
induced by
\phi_{R,v}
coincides with
(\nabla_{\phi})_{R,v}
.
Hence,

\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R,v}:=((\mathcal{E}_{B})_{R,v},\phi_{R,v})
forms
a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on

\mathscr{X}_{R,v}
,
and the pair
(\mathscr{X}_{R,v},\mathscr{E}_{R,v}^{\spadesuit})
specifies an element of
\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)
.
By Proposition
6.3.2
and the bijection displayed in
[
Wak6
, Eq. (792)]
,
the assignment

(\mathscr{X}_{R},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R})\mapsto(\mathscr{X}_{R,v},\mathscr{E}_{R,v}^{\spadesuit})
(for every
v\in M\otimes_{k}\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
)
defines a transitive action of
M\otimes_{k}\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
on
the fibers of
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}(\sigma):\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R_{0})
.
This action is, by construction, verified to be free if
R=k
.
Consequently,
\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
is isomorphic to the tangent space of
\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}
.

The
assertion concerning an obstruction space can be proved by a routine argument using
the Čech cohomological descriptions of relevant deformations as discussed above,
so the proof is left to the reader.
(Similar discussions can be found in the proofs of
[
Wak5
, Propositions 6.4.3 and 6.8.2]
.)
Finally, the last assertion
follows from
[
Sch
, Theorem 2.11]
together with
the fact that
\mathrm{dim}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1})<\infty
and
\mathcal{G}^{2}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}=0
(cf. Proposition
6.2.4
).
∎

As a consequence of the above theorem, we obtain the following assertion.

Corollary 6.3.6
.

(i)

The Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
​ is smooth over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.

(ii)

Let

k
be an algebraically closed field over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
,

\mathscr{X}
an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
over
k
, and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Denote by
s:\mathrm{Spec}(k)\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
(resp.,
\widetilde{s}:\mathrm{Spec}(k)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
)
the morphism classifying
\mathscr{X}
(resp.,
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
).
Also, denote by
T_{s}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
(resp.,
T_{\widetilde{s}}\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
)
the
k
-vector space defined as the tangent space of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
) at
s
(resp.,
\widetilde{s}
).
Then, there exists a canonical isomorphism of
k
-vector spaces

(488)

\displaystyle\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}:T_{\widetilde{s}}\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1}

which makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{T_{\widetilde{s}}\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})}}
\scriptstyle{d\Pi_{g,r}}
\textstyle{T_{s}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{KS}_{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(X,\mathcal{T}),}

where
\mathrm{KS}_{\mathscr{X}}
denotes the Kodaira-Spencer morphism (cf., e.g.,
[
Wak6
, §6̇.1.1]
) and the upper horizontal arrow
d\Pi_{g,r}
denotes the differential of the projection
\Pi_{g,r}
(cf. (
291
)).

Proof.

The assertion follows from Theorem
6.3.5
and
the definition of
D_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
.
∎

6.4.
Dimension of the moduli space

Let
S
,
\mathscr{X}
, and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
be as introduced at the beginning of §
6.1
.
Suppose further that the
