\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper induced from
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is normal, in the sense of
[
Wak6
, Definition 4.53]
.
(According to
[
Wak6
, Proposition 4.55]
, any
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper is isomorphic to a normal one.)
Consider the composite surjection

(493)

\displaystyle d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}/\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}/\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}\left(=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{-1}/\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{0}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T},

where the second and third arrows are the isomorphisms defined in

[
Wak6
, Eq. (163)]
and
[
Wak6
, Eq. (617)]
, respectively.
By applying the functor
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(-)
to the composite

(494)

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}=\right)\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\hookrightarrow\right)\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\xrightarrow{d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{T},

we obtain an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-linear morphism

(495)

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}:\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{T}).

Next, let us consider the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear endomorphism of
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
defined as

(496)

\displaystyle\chi:=\mathrm{id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}}-\nabla_{\phi}^{(0)}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}-\nabla^{(0)}_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}.

Lemma 6.4.1
.

The automorphism
\chi
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
is involutive (i.e.,
\chi\circ\chi=\mathrm{id}
), and
the following equalities hold:

(497)

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\chi=-d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}},\hskip 28.45274ptd^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\chi=-d_{\mathcal{E}}.

Proof.

Since we have assumed that the
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper induced from
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is normal,
the equality
d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\nabla_{\phi}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{T}}
holds (cf.
[
Wak6
, Example 2.7 and Remark 2.16]
).
Hence, the following sequence of equalities holds:

(498)

\displaystyle\chi\circ\chi

\displaystyle=\left(\mathrm{id}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)\circ\left(\mathrm{id}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{id}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\left(\mathrm{id}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\left(\mathrm{id}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{id}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}+\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}+\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}+\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}+\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{id}.

Moreover, we have

(499)

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\chi

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{E}}\circ(\mathrm{id}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}-\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}})

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{E}}-d_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}}-d_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{E}}-d_{\mathcal{E}}-d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle=-d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}.

A similar calculation shows
d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\chi=-d_{\mathcal{E}}
.
This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Moreover, we can prove the following assertion.

Proposition 6.4.2
.

The automorphism
\chi
restricts to an automorphism
\chi_{\phi}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
, and restricts to an isomorphism
\chi_{B}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
That is to say, we obtain the following commutative square diagrams:

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\chi_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\chi}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}},}

\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\chi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\chi}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}.}

Proof.

By the definition of
\nabla_{\phi}
,
the equality

(508)

\displaystyle\phi^{\natural\natural}(D)=\nabla_{\phi}(D)\left(\in\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})\right)

holds for every local section
D\in\mathcal{T}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\right)
.
For local sections
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
, the following sequence of equalities holds:

(509)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),\chi(v)]-\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,d_{\mathcal{E}}(\chi(v))])

\displaystyle=[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),v-(\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}})(v)-(\nabla\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}})(v)]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,d_{\mathcal{E}}(v-(\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}})(v)-(\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}})(v))])

\displaystyle=\cancel{[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),v]}-[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),(\nabla_{\phi}\circ d_{\mathcal{E}})(v)]-[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),(\nabla_{\phi}\circ d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}})(v)]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\cancel{\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,d_{\mathcal{E}}(v)])}+\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,d_{\mathcal{E}}(v)])+\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}(v)])

\displaystyle=-[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),\phi^{\natural\natural}(d_{\mathcal{E}}(v))]-[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),\phi^{\natural\natural}(d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}(v))]+\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,d_{\mathcal{E}}(v)])+\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}(v)])

\displaystyle=0,

where the third equality follows from (
508
) together with the equality
[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),v]-\phi^{\natural\natural}([D,d_{\mathcal{E}}(v)])=0
, and the fourth equality follows from the fact that
\phi^{\natural\natural}
preserves the Lie bracket operator
[-,-]
.
This implies that
\chi(v)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
, and hence
\chi
restricts to an automorphism of
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
.

Moreover,
since
\mathrm{Ker}(d_{\mathcal{E}})=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\mathrm{Ker}(d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}})=\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}_{B}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
,
the equality
d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\chi=-d_{\mathcal{E}}
asserted in Lemma
6.4.1
implies that

\chi
restricts to an isomorphism
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

Proposition 6.4.3
.

There exists an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules

(510)

\displaystyle\Xi:\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1},

which makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\Xi}
\scriptstyle{D^{\prime}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{T}).}

Proof.

By Proposition
6.4.2
, we obtain the following commutative square diagram:

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})\oplus\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\chi_{B}\oplus\chi_{\phi})}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\delta_{1}\oplus(-\delta_{2}))}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})\oplus\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\gamma_{1}\oplus(-\gamma_{2}))}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\chi}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}),}

where
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
denote the natural inclusions
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
, respectively.
This induces an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules

(519)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}(\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\delta_{1}\oplus(-\delta_{2})))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1}.

On the other hand,
it follows from Lemma
6.2.1
, (i), that the commutative square diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\delta_{1})}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\delta_{2})}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})}

is Cartesian, where the upper horizontal arrow (resp., the left-hand vertical arrow) arises from the natural inclusion
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
(resp.,
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\phi}
).
Hence,
this diagram yields an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules

(524)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Ker}(\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\delta_{1}\oplus(-\delta_{2}))).

By composing (
519
) and (
524
), we obtain an isomorphism

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{G}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{1}
, as desired.
∎

Corollary 6.4.4
.

The Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
is smooth over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and equidimensional of dimension
3g-3+r
.
Moreover,
the projection
\Pi_{g,r}
is
faithfully flat.
In particular,
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}}\mathcal{M}_{g,r}
is nonempty.

Proof.

The former assertion follows from Corollary
6.3.6
, Propositions
6.1.2
, (ii), and
6.4.3
.

Next, suppose that the projection
\Pi_{g,r}
is not surjective.
Since
\Pi_{g,r}
is proper (cf. Corollary
4.6.2
) and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
is irreducible,
the image
\mathrm{Im}(\Pi_{g,r})
of
\Pi_{g,r}
does not coincides with the entire space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
and has dimension
<3g-3+r
.
In particular, we have
\mathrm{dim}(\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r})-\mathrm{dim}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r})>0
.
This implies that each fiber of
\Pi_{g,r}
has positive dimension (cf.
[
Har
, Chap. II, Exercise 3.22]
); it contradicts the finiteness of
\Pi_{g,r}
asserted in Corollary
4.6.2
.
Thus,
\Pi_{g,r}
turns out to be surjective.

The flatness of
\Pi_{g,r}
follows immediately from

[
Har
, Chap. III, Exercise 10.9]
because both
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
,
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
are smooth over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
\Pi_{g,r}
is finite.
Thus, we have finished the proof of the assertion.
∎

6.5.
Ordinariness

Let
S
,
\mathscr{X}
, and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
be as before.

Definition 6.5.1
.

We shall say that
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is
ordinary
if the morphism
D_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
(cf. (
437
)) is an isomorphism.

The following assertion is a direct consequence of Proposition
6.4.3
.

Proposition 6.5.2
.

\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is ordinary if and only if the morphism
D^{\prime}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
(cf. (
495
)) is an isomorphism.

Remark 6.5.3

(Previous definition of ordinariness)
.

When

\mathscr{X}
is unpointed and smooth,
it follows from Proposition
6.5.2
that the definition of ordinariness
introduced here is equivalent to that of the corresponding
indigenous
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},B)
-bundle
, in the sense of
[
Wak5
, Definition 6.7.1]
.

Denote by

(525)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r,\mathrm{ord}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},\ \text{or simply},\mathcal{O}p_{g,r,\mathrm{ord}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},

the open substack of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
(i.e.,
\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
) classifying
ordinary
dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers.
According to Corollary
6.3.6
,
this stack coincides with
the étale locus of
\mathcal{O}p_{g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
relative to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
.

Proposition 6.5.4
.

The projection
\Pi_{N\Rightarrow 1}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,g,r}
obtained by reducing the level of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers to
1
restricts to a morphism

\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r,\mathrm{ord}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,g,r,\mathrm{ord}}
.

Proof.

As shown in
[
Moc2
, Chap. II, Theorem 2.8]
,
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,g,r}
is irreducible.
Hence, by an argument similar to the proof of Corollary
6.4.4
,
the projection
\Pi_{N\Rightarrow 1}
turns out to be flat.
If the projection
\Pi_{N,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
is unramified (or equivalently, étale) at a geometric point
q
of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}
,
then

\Pi_{N\Rightarrow 1}

is unramified (or equivalently, étale) at the same point.
It follows that the differential of the projection
\Pi_{1,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}

at
\Pi_{N\Rightarrow 1}(q)
yields an isomorphism between the respective tangent spaces.
This means that
\Pi_{1,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
is étale at
\Pi_{N\Rightarrow 1}(q)
.
So the proof of the assertion is completed.
∎

Next,
suppose that
\mathscr{X}
is unpointed (i.e.,
r=0
) and smooth (which implies
X^{\mathrm{log}}=X
and
S^{\mathrm{log}}=S
).
We shall write
\mathscr{G}:=(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi})
for simplicity.
Since the equivalence of categories (
95
) commutes the formation of duals,
the identification
(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}\vee})
induced from the Killing form on
\mathfrak{g}

gives
an identification
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi}^{\vee}
.
Under these identifications,
the diagram (
1.7
) where “
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
” is taken to be
\mathscr{G}
defines a commutative square diagram

\textstyle{f_{*}(\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$C$}\hskip-1.42262pt}_{\mathrm{Coker}}}\left(=f_{*}(C_{\mathscr{G}})\right)}
\scriptstyle{\int_{\mathscr{G}}^{(0)}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{f_{*}^{(N)}(\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\int_{\mathscr{G}}^{(N)}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$C$}\hskip-1.42262pt}_{\mathrm{Ker}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}^{(N)}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})^{\vee}.}

Let us consider the composite

(530)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\otimes 2}\hookrightarrow\Omega\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{C_{\mathscr{G}}}\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi},

where the first arrow denotes the morphism induced by the inclusion
\mathfrak{g}^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
under the natural identification
\left(\mathfrak{g}^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}/\mathfrak{g}^{2}_{\mathcal{E}}=\right)\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{1}=\Omega
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Eq. (617)]
).
By applying the functor
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(-)
to this composite,
we obtain an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-linear morphism

(531)

\displaystyle D^{\prime\prime}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}:f_{*}(\Omega^{\otimes 2})\rightarrow f_{*}(\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E},\phi})).

Proposition 6.5.5
.

The following square diagram is commutative up to multiplication by an invertible factor in
\mathbb{F}_{p}
:

where
\int
denotes the isomorphism obtained by applying
Grothendieck-Serre duality to
\Omega
.
In particular,
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is ordinary if and only if
D^{\prime\prime}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider
the diagram

where the upper and lower horizontal arrows arise from the isomorphisms
\Omega\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{1}\left(\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)
and
\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\twoheadrightarrow\right)\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}/\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}
, respectively, defined in
[
Wak6
, Eq. (617)]
.
By the definition of the Killing form on
\mathfrak{g}
(i.e.,
(a,b)\mapsto\frac{1}{4}\cdot\mathrm{tr}(\mathrm{ad}(a)\cdot\mathrm{ad}(b))\left(=\mathrm{tr}(ab)\right)
for
a,b\in\mathfrak{g}
), this diagram
is commutative up to multiplication by an invertible factor.
Hence, the assertion follows from the commutativity (in this sense) of this diagram and that of (
6.5
).
The latter assertion follows from the former assertion and Proposition
6.5.2
.
∎

6.6.
Dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers on a
3
-pointed projective line

The goal of this subsection is to show that the stack
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,0,3}
defined for each
\rho\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm\}
is, if it is nonempty, isomorphic to
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
(cf. Theorem
6.6.5
).
Since we have already obtained the finiteness and smoothness of the moduli space (cf. Theorem
5.3.6
and Corollary
6.3.6
, (i)),
the problem is reduced to proving
that
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,0,3}
consists of exactly one point in the set-theoretic sense.
So the desired assertion follows directly from
[
Wak7
, Proposition 6.4.1]
.
However, in this subsection, we give an alternative (somewhat simpler) proof of “
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,0,3}\cong\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
” without applying the finiteness and smoothness, which were actually proved by technically complicated arguments.
We do so by slightly generalizing the discussion in
[
Wak7
]
.

We shall consider the case where
\mathscr{X}
is
the
3
-pointed projective line
\mathscr{P}:=(\mathbb{P}/S,\{[0],[1],[\infty]\})
, introduced in (
370
).
Let
k
be an algebraically closed field over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and
suppose that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
for
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{A}rt_{/k})
.
To clarify the base space “
\mathrm{Spec}(R)
”,
we occasionally write
\mathbb{P}_{R}
and
\mathscr{P}_{R}
instead of
\mathbb{P}
and
\mathscr{P}
, respectively.

Let us take a triple
(\rho_{0},\rho_{1},\rho_{\infty})
of elements of
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}
.
Then, there exists a triple of integers
(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{\infty})
satisfying the following conditions:

•

2\cdot\rho_{x}=\lambda_{x}
as elements of
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}
and

0<\lambda_{x}<p^{N}

for every
x=0,1,\infty
;

•

The sum
\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{\infty}
is odd
<2\cdot p^{N}
.

Set
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{+}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\lambda_{0}\cdot[0]+\lambda_{1}\cdot[1]+\lambda_{\infty}\cdot[\infty])
.
Note that we can find a unique
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla^{+}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{+}
whose restriction to
U:=\mathbb{P}\setminus\{[0],[1],[\infty]\}
coincides with the trivial
\mathcal{D}_{U/S}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{+}|_{U}=\mathcal{O}_{U}
.
Also, let
\mathcal{L}
be a line bundle on
\mathbb{P}
of relative degree
\frac{\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{\infty}+1}{2}
, which is uniquely determined up to isomorphism because
R
is a local ring.
Let us fix an identification
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{+}
.
Under this identification, the pair
\vartheta:=(\mathcal{L},\nabla^{+})
form a dormant
2^{(N)}
-theta characteristic of
\mathbb{P}^{\mathrm{log}}/S
.

Now, let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
be a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{P}
of radii
(\rho_{0},\rho_{1},\rho_{\infty})
, i.e., whose radius at
[x]
(
x\in\{0,1,\infty\}
) coincides with
\rho_{x}
.
We shall write

(540)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla,\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j=0}^{2})

for
the dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper determined by
a unique (up to isomorphism)
dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathscr{P}

corresponding to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
via the isomorphism
\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
(cf. Theorem
4.5.1
).
That is to say, we set
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}:=\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
(hence
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\nabla=\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
).
In particular, since
\mathcal{F}^{1}=\mathcal{L}
, we can regard
\mathcal{L}
as a line subbundle of
\mathcal{F}
.

Definition 6.6.1
.

We shall refer to
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
as the
canonical dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
associated to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.
(Note that the formation of
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}
depends on the choice of
(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{\infty})
, but its isomorphism class does not depend on the choice of the identification
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{+}
.)

Remark 6.6.2

(Previous works on canonical
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers)
.

The construction of the canonical
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
-opers was discussed in
[
Moc2
, Chap. IV, § 2.1]
(for
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
and
N=1
) and
[
Wak7
, § 6.3]
(for
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
) to
establish a bijective correspondence between dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers on
\mathscr{P}
and certain tamely ramified coverings on
\mathscr{P}
(i.e., dynamical Belyi maps).

We shall write
\mathcal{F}^{\flat}:=F^{(N)*}_{\mathbb{P}/S}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla))
and write

(541)

\displaystyle\tau:\mathcal{F}^{\flat}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}

for the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}
-linear injection
extending the

\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N)}}
-linear inclusion
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
;
this morphism commutes with the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structures
\nabla^{(N-1)}_{\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla),\mathrm{can}}
(cf. (
94
)) and
\nabla
.
Next, we shall set
\mathcal{L}^{\flat}:=\mathcal{L}\cap\mathrm{Im}(\tau)
.
Since the restriction of
\tau
to
U:=\mathbb{P}\setminus\{[0],[1],[\infty]\}
is an isomorphism (cf. (
95
)),
the quotient sheaf
\mathcal{L}/\mathcal{L}^{\flat}
is a torsion sheaf supported on
\{[0],[1],[\infty]\}
.

Lemma 6.6.3
.

Let us keep the above notation.

(i)

\mathcal{L}/\mathcal{L}^{\flat}
is flat over
S
, and
the stalk of
\mathcal{L}/\mathcal{L}^{\flat}
at the marked point
x\in\{[0],[1],[\infty]\}
is
of rank
\lambda_{x}
(as a free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module).
Moreover, the injection

(542)

\displaystyle\iota:\mathcal{L}/\mathcal{L}^{\flat}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Coker}(\tau)

induced from the inclusion
\mathcal{L}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
is an isomorphism.

(ii)

There exists an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{(N)}}
-linear isomorphism
\gamma:\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{(N)}}^{\oplus 2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)
.

Proof.

In what follows, we shall use the notation
(-)_{k}
to denote the result of reducing objects and morphisms over
R
modulo
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
.

First, let us consider assertion (i).
By Proposition
6.3.2
(and the isomorphism
\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
),
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
is locally
isomorphic to the base-change of
(\mathcal{F}_{k},\nabla_{k})
over
R
.
Hence,
\mathrm{Coker}(\tau)
can be locally identified with the base-change of
\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{k})
.
This implies that
\mathrm{Coker}(\tau)
is flat over
S
.
Recall from
[
Wak7
, Lemma 6.3.2]
that
the reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
of
(
542
)
is an isomorphism.
By Nakayama’s lemma, the morphism
(
542
)
turns out to be an isomorphism.
In particular,
\mathcal{L}/\mathcal{L}^{\flat}
is flat over
S
, and the rank of its stalk at
x
(as a free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module)
coincides with the rank of its reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
, i.e.,
\lambda_{x}
.
This completes the proof of assertion (i).

Next, we shall consider assertion (ii).
Since
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
is locally isomorphic to the base-change of
(\mathcal{F}_{k},\nabla_{k})
(as mentioned above),
the vector bundle
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)
on
\mathbb{P}^{(N)}
is locally isomorphic to the base-change of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{k})
.
Hence,
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)
specifies a deformation of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{k})
over
R
.
Here, let us observe the following fact from well-known generalities of deformation theory: if
we are given a small extension in
\mathcal{A}rt_{/k}
of the form
0\rightarrow k\rightarrow R_{1}\rightarrow R_{0}\rightarrow 0
and a deformation

\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{k})_{R_{0}}
of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{k})
over
R_{0}
, then
the set of deformations of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{k})_{R_{0}}
over
R_{1}
has a structure of torsor modeled on
H^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(N)},\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{k})))
.
On the other hand,
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{k})
is isomorphic to

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(N)}}^{\oplus 2}
as proved in

[
Wak7
, Lemma 6.3.3]
,
so we have

(543)

\displaystyle H^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(N)},\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{k})))\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{(N)}_{k},\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(N)}}^{\oplus 2}))\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(N)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(N)}})^{\oplus 4}=0.

This implies that
any deformation of
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{k})
over
R
is trivial.
In particular, there exists an isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{(N)}}^{\oplus 2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla)
.
∎

The following assertion is a slight generalization of
[
Wak7
, Proposition 6.4.1]
.
(The proof is entirely similar.)

Proposition 6.6.4
.

Let
\rho:=(\rho_{0},\rho_{1},\rho_{\infty})
be an element of
((\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\})^{\times 3}
.
Then, any two dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers on
\mathscr{P}
of radii
\rho
are isomorphic.

Proof.

Suppose that we are given two dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers
\mathscr{E}_{\circ}^{\spadesuit}
,
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\bullet}
on
\mathscr{P}
of radii
\rho
.
For each
\star\in\{\circ,\bullet\}
,
denote by
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\star}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\star},\nabla_{\star},\{\mathcal{F}^{j}_{\star}\}_{j})
the canonical dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper associated to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\star}
.
As mentioned previously,
the inclusion
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\star})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\star}
extends to
an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}
-linear injection
\tau_{\star}:F^{(N)*}_{\mathbb{P}/S}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\star}))\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\star}
, and we obtain

\mathcal{L}_{\star}^{\flat}:=\mathcal{L}_{\star}\cap\mathrm{Im}(\tau_{\star})
by regarding
\mathcal{L}_{\star}:=\mathcal{L}
as a line subbundle of
\mathcal{F}_{\star}
.
The morphism
\iota_{\star}:\mathcal{L}_{\star}/\mathcal{L}_{\star}^{\flat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\star})
induced by
\mathcal{L}_{\star}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\star}
is an isomorphism (cf. Lemma
6.6.3
, (i)).
Write
\nabla_{\star}^{(0)}
for the
\mathcal{D}^{(0)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}_{\star}
induced from
\nabla_{\star}
.
Then, the collection
\mathscr{F}_{\star}^{\heartsuit(1)}:=(\mathcal{F}_{\star},\nabla_{\star}^{(0)},\{\mathcal{F}_{\star}^{j}\}_{j})
defines a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(1)},\vartheta^{(1)})
-oper, where
\vartheta^{(1)}
denotes the
2^{(1)}
-theta characteristic obtained by reducing the level of
\vartheta
to
1
.
Recall from
[
Moc2
, Chap. I, Theorem 4.4]
that dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers on
\mathscr{P}
are completely determined by their radii.
In particular,
since
(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit(1)}_{\circ})^{\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
and
(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit(1)}_{\bullet})^{\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
have the same radii (cf. Proposition
5.3.3
, (i)),
we have
(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit(1)}_{\circ})^{\Rightarrow\spadesuit}\cong(\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit(1)}_{\bullet})^{\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
.
It follows that there exists an isomorphism

(544)

\displaystyle\alpha:\mathscr{F}^{(1)\heartsuit}_{\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{F}^{(1)\heartsuit}_{\bullet}

(cf. Theorem
4.5.1
).
This isomorphism
restricts to an isomorphism
\alpha|_{\mathcal{L}_{\circ}}:\mathcal{L}_{\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{L}_{\bullet}
, and hence
induces an isomorphism
\alpha|_{\mathcal{L}_{\circ}/\mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{\circ}}:\mathcal{L}_{\circ}/\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{\flat}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{L}_{\bullet}/\mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{\bullet}
.
Thus, we obtain the composite isomorphism

(545)

\displaystyle\alpha|_{\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\circ})}:=\iota_{\bullet}\circ\alpha|_{\mathcal{L}_{\circ}/\mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{\circ}}\circ\iota_{\circ}^{-1}:\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\circ})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\bullet}).

In what follows, we shall prove the commutativity of the following square diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\circ}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\bullet}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\circ})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\alpha|_{\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\circ})}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\bullet}),}

where
\pi_{\star}
(
\star\in\{\circ,\bullet\}
) denotes the natural projection
\mathcal{F}_{\star}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\star})
.
Let us take
\star\in\{\circ,\bullet\}
and
x\in\{[0],[1],[\infty]\}
.
Also, choose a local function
t
on
\mathbb{P}
defining
x
.
The formal neighborhood
\widehat{U}_{x}
of
x
in
\mathbb{P}
may be identified with
U_{\oslash}
(cf. (
181
)).
By Proposition
3.3.1
and Lemma
6.6.3
,
one may verify that the restriction
(\mathcal{F}_{\star},\nabla_{\star})|_{\widehat{U}_{x}}
of
(\mathcal{F}_{\star},\nabla_{\star})
to
\widehat{U}_{x}

is isomorphic to
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,\overline{\lambda}_{x}}^{(N-1)}\oplus\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,0}^{(N-1)}
, where
\overline{\lambda}_{x}
denotes the image of
\lambda_{x}
via the natural quotient
\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
.
Let us fix
an identification
(\mathcal{F}_{\star},\nabla_{\star})|_{\widehat{U}_{x}}=\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,\overline{\lambda}_{x}}^{(N-1)}\oplus\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,0}^{(N-1)}
.
Under this identification,
the restriction of
\alpha
to
\widehat{U}_{x}
determines an automorphism

\alpha_{\widehat{U}_{x}}
of

\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,\overline{\lambda}_{x}}^{(N-1)}\oplus\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,0}^{(N-1)}
.
Here, we shall use the notation
\mathrm{mult}_{(-)}
to denote the endomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
given by multiplication by
(-)
.
Since
\overline{\lambda}_{x}\neq 0
,
the automorphism
\alpha|_{\widehat{U}_{x}}
may be expressed as
\mathrm{mult}_{v}\oplus\mathrm{mult}_{w}
for some
v,w\in\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{\times}
after possibly replacing the fixed identification

(\mathcal{F}_{\star},\nabla_{\star})|_{\widehat{U}_{x}}=\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,\overline{\lambda}_{x}}^{(N-1)}\oplus\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,0}^{(N-1)}
with another (cf. Proposition
3.3.4
, (ii)).
By Proposition
5.1.3
, (i),
the inclusion
\mathcal{L}_{\star}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\star}
corresponds, after choosing a suitable trivialization

\Gamma(\widehat{U}_{x},\mathcal{L}_{\star}|_{\widehat{U}_{x}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
,
to the
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{\oplus 2}
given by

1\mapsto(u_{\star},1)
for some
u_{\star}\in\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{\times}
.
Then, the restriction of
\alpha|_{\mathcal{L}_{\star}}
to
\widehat{U}_{x}
may be expressed as
\mathrm{mult}_{w}
, and the equality
v\cdot u_{\circ}=w\cdot u_{\bullet}
holds.
Hence, for each
(g,h)\in\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{\oplus 2}\left(=\mathcal{F}_{\circ}|_{\widehat{U}_{x}}\right)
,
we have

(550)

\displaystyle(\pi_{\bullet}\circ\alpha)((g,h))

\displaystyle=\pi_{\bullet}((v\cdot g,w\cdot h))

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{v\cdot g}{u_{\bullet}}\cdot u_{\bullet},\frac{v\cdot g}{u_{\bullet}}\cdot 1\right)\text{mod}\ \mathrm{Im}(\tau_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=\iota_{\bullet}\left(\frac{v\cdot g}{u_{\bullet}}\ \mathrm{mod}\ \mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{\bullet}\right)

\displaystyle=(\iota_{\bullet}\circ\alpha|_{\mathcal{L}_{\circ}/\mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{\circ}})\left(\frac{v\cdot g}{wu_{\bullet}}\ \mathrm{mod}\ \mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{\circ}\right)

\displaystyle=(\alpha|_{\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\circ})}\circ\iota_{\circ})\left(\frac{g}{u_{\circ}}\ \mathrm{mod}\ \mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{\circ}\right)

\displaystyle=\alpha|_{\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\circ})}\left(\left(\frac{g}{u_{\circ}}\cdot u_{\circ},\frac{g}{u_{\circ}}\cdot 1\right)\text{mod}\ \mathrm{Im}(\tau_{\circ})\right)

\displaystyle=(\alpha|_{\mathrm{Coker}(\tau_{\circ})}\circ\pi_{\circ})((g,h)).

This shows the desired commutativity of (
6.6
).

Moreover, the commutativity of (
6.6
) just proved implies that
\alpha
is restricted, via
\iota_{\circ}
and
\iota_{\bullet}
, to an isomorphism

(551)

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}:F^{(N)*}_{\mathbb{P}/S}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\circ}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{(N)*}_{\mathbb{P}/S}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\bullet})).

Since
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\circ})\cong\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\bullet})\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{(N)}}^{\oplus 2}
(cf. Lemma
6.6.3
, (ii)), the morphism

(552)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{(N)}}}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\circ}),\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\bullet}))\rightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}}(F^{(N)*}_{\mathbb{P}/S}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\circ})),F^{(N)*}_{\mathbb{P}/S}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\bullet})))

arising from pull-back by
F^{(N)}_{\mathbb{P}/S}
is bijective.
In particular,
\alpha^{\prime}
comes from an isomorphism
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\circ})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\bullet})
, and hence
\alpha^{\prime}
is compatible with the respective
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-actions
\nabla^{(N-1)}_{\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\circ}),\mathrm{can}}
,
\nabla^{(N-1)}_{\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\bullet}),\mathrm{can}}
.
Since
\nabla_{\star}
(
\star\in\{\circ,\bullet\}
) is the unique
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}_{\star}
extending
\nabla^{(N-1)}_{\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\star}),\mathrm{can}}
via
\tau_{\star}
,
the isomorphism
\alpha
, being an extension of
\alpha^{\prime}
, preserves the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure.
It follows that
\alpha
defines an isomorphism of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers
\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{F}^{\heartsuit}_{\bullet}
.
Thus, we obtain
\mathscr{E}_{\circ}^{\spadesuit}\cong\mathscr{E}_{\bullet}^{\spadesuit}
, as desired.
∎

Theorem 6.6.5
.

Let
\rho\in((\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\})^{\times 3}
.
Then, the stack
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,0,3}
is either empty or isomorphic to
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
In particular, any dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{P}
is ordinary, i.e., we have
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{0,3,\mathrm{ord}}=\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{0,3}
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Proposition
6.6.4
.
∎

Remark 6.6.6

(When does
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,0,3}
become nonempty?)
.

It is an important problem to determines for what elements
\rho\in((\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\})^{\times 3}
the associated moduli stacks
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,0,3}
are nonempty.
The complete answer to this problem for
N=1
was given in
[
Moc2
, Introduction, Theorem 1.3]
(see also
[
Wak6
, § 7.8.1]
).
But, little is known about the general case at the time of writing the present paper.
In a subsequent study in this series of papers, we will investigate that problem (cf.
[
Wak9
]
).

Corollary 6.6.7
.

For each
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, denote by
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r})

the set of connected components of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}
.
Let us consider the projective system of sets

(553)

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r})\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{2,g,r})\rightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,g,r})

arising from (
292
).
Then, the projective limit

(554)

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\pi_{0}(\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r})

of this system is nonempty.

Proof.

Let us take an
r
-pointed totally degenerate curve
\mathscr{X}
of genus
g
over an algebraically closed field
k
in characteristic
p
.
Choose
clutching data
\mathbb{G}:=({{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG,\{(g_{j},r_{j})\}_{j=1}^{J},\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})

that is compatible with
\mathscr{X}
(cf. Definition
5.5.1
).
In particular,

\mathscr{X}
may be obtained by gluing together
J
copies of
\mathscr{P}
by means of
\mathbb{G}
.
Denote by
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}:=\{\rho^{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}
the set of
\mathbb{G}
-
\Xi_{2,N}
-radii defined by
\rho^{j}:=\rho_{N,\circledcirc}^{\times 3}
(cf. (
381
)).
By Theorem
6.6.5
and the equivalence of categories (
358
),
there exists a unique (up to isomorphism) dormant
\mathbb{G}\text{-}\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper of radii
\rho_{\mathbb{G}}
on
\mathscr{X}
;
we shall denote by
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathscr{X},N}
the corresponding dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
via the equivalence of categories (
357
).
Also, denote by
\mathfrak{I}_{N}
the connected component of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}
containing the point
s_{N}
classifying
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathscr{X},N})
.
For each
N^{\prime}\leq N
, the equality
q_{N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}}(\rho_{N,\circledcirc}^{\times 3})=\rho_{N^{\prime},\circledcirc}^{\times 3}
holds (cf. (
347
) for the definition of
q_{N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}}
).
This implies that the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N^{\prime})}
-oper obtained from
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathscr{X},N}
by reducing its level is isomorphic to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathscr{X},N^{\prime}}
.
In other words, the projection
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N^{\prime},g,r}
sends
s_{N}
to
s_{N^{\prime}}
.
Since the stacks
\mathfrak{I}_{N}
are irreducible because of their smoothness proved in Corollary
6.3.6
, (i),
the projective system (
292
) restricts to a projective system

(555)

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow\mathfrak{I}_{N}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\mathfrak{I}_{2}\rightarrow\mathfrak{I}_{1}.

In particular, this system defines an element of the set
\varprojlim_{N}\pi_{0}(\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r})
, so we conclude that
\varprojlim_{N}\pi_{0}(\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r})\neq\emptyset
, as desired.
∎

6.7.
Generic étaleness of the moduli space

When
N=1
,
the generic étaleness of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}/\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
was already shown in
[
Moc2
, Chap. II, Theorem 2.8]
.
By using Theorem
6.6.5
, we can prove the following higher-level generalization.

Theorem 6.7.1
.

The stack

\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r,\mathrm{ord}}
is a dense open substack of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
containing all the points lying over the points in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
classifying
totally degenerate curves.
In particular, the projection
\Pi_{g,r}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}\!\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
is generically étale.

Proof.

First, we shall prove the former assertion.
Let
k
be an algebraically closed field over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
\rho
an element of
\Xi_{2,N}^{\times r}
.
Also, let us take an arbitrary
r
-pointed totally degenerate curve
\mathscr{X}

of genus
g
over
k
;
there exists
clutching data

\mathbb{G}=({{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{-20.0}{$\vartriangle$}}}\hskip-7.39772ptG,\{(g_{j},r_{j})\}_{j=1}^{J},\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J})
that is compatible with
\mathscr{X}
.
In particular, the equality
(g_{j},r_{j})=(0,3)
holds for every
j=1,\cdots,J
, and the point
q_{\mathbb{G}}
of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
determined by the morphism

(556)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Clut}_{\mathbb{G}}:\left(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{F}_{p})=\right)\prod_{j=1}^{J}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{j},r_{j}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}

(cf. (
363
)) classifies
\mathscr{X}
.
By applying Theorem
5.4.7
,
we obtain the Cartesian square diagram (
5.4.7
) for
\mathbb{G}
and
\rho
.
It follows from Theorem
6.6.5
that
the left-hand vertical arrow in this diagram is unramified.
Hence, the projection
\Pi_{\rho,g,r}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\rho,g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
, i.e., the right-hand vertical arrow in (
5.4.7
), is unramified over the point
q_{\mathbb{G}}
.
By the flatness of
\Pi_{g,r}
obtained in Corollary
6.4.4
,
\Pi_{\rho,g,r}
is moreover étale over the same point.
This completes the proof of the former assertion.

Also, since the projection
\Pi_{g,r}

is finite and faithfully flat, one may verify that any irreducible component of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
contains a point lying over the point of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
classifying a totally degenerate curve.
Thus, the latter assertion follows from the former assertion together with the open nature of étaleness.
∎

Remark 6.7.2
.

(Divisor defined by the nonordinary locus)
The complement of the ordinary locus forms a divisor on
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}
.
Indeed, let us consider
the morphism
D_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
(cf. (
437
)) in the case where the pair “
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
” is taken to be the universal object
over
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
.
This morphism determines a morphism

(557)

\displaystyle\bigwedge^{3g-3+r}D_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}:\bigwedge^{3g-3}\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}})\rightarrow\bigwedge^{3g-3+r}\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{T})

between line bundles on
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
(cf. Propositions
6.1.2
, (ii),
6.2.4
, and
6.4.3
), which corresponds to a global section of the line bundle

(558)

\displaystyle\left(\bigwedge^{3g-3+r}\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{T})\right)\otimes\left(\bigwedge^{3g-3+r}\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{1}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}})\right)^{\vee}.

The morphism
D_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
is an isomorphism exactly on the complement of the divisor

\mathcal{D}_{\cancel{\mathrm{ord}}}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}\right)
associated to this section.
Thus, the nonordinary locus in
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{g,r}
coincides with the support of
\mathcal{D}_{\cancel{\mathrm{ord}}}
.

Remark 6.7.3

(Generic étaleness for higher rank cases)
.

For a (sufficiently small) general
n>1
,
the projection
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}

is generically étale (cf.
[
Wak6
, Theorem G]
).
The generic étaleness
is essential in the proofs of Joshi’s conjecture described in
[
Wak1
]
and
[
Wak6
]
.
Indeed, we used this geometric property to lift relevant moduli spaces to characteristic
0
and then compared them with
certain Quot schemes over
\mathbb{C}
; this argument enables us to compute explicitly the number of all possible dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers on a general curve.
To develop the enumerative geometry of higher-level dormant opers in such a way that this argument works, we expect that the following conjecture is true:

Suppose that
p
is sufficiently large relative to
n
,
g
, and
r
.
Then, the stack
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
associated to such a collection
(p,n,g,r)
is generically étale over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
, i.e., any irreducible component of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
that dominates
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
admits a dense open subscheme which is étale over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
.

For each positive integer
N^{\prime}<N
, we shall write

\Pi_{N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}}
for the morphism
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N^{\prime},g,r}
obtained by reducing the level of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers to
N^{\prime}
.
We shall set

(559)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}:=\bigcap_{N^{\prime}\leq N}\Pi_{N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N^{\prime},g,r,\mathrm{ord}}),

which is a dense open substack of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}
(cf. Theorem
6.7.1
).
That is to say,

\mathcal{J}
classifies dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
such that
the morphisms
D_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit(N^{\prime})}}
are isomorphisms for all
N^{\prime}\leq N
, where
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit(N^{\prime})}
denotes the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N^{\prime})}
-oper induced from
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.
Hence, we obtain the dense open substack

(560)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,N\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}

of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
defined as the complement of the image of the natural projection
\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\setminus\mathcal{J}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
.

If
\mathscr{X}
is a pointed stable curve over a geometric point classified by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,N\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}
,
then all the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers on
\mathscr{X}
and their reductions to level
N^{\prime}\leq N
are ordinary.
In particular, the set of isomorphism classes of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers on
\mathscr{X}
has cardinality equal to the degree of the projection
\Pi_{g,r}
.

Definition 6.7.4
.

An
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
is called
(dormant-)
N
-ordinary
if it is classified by the open substack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,N\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}
.
(Since
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,N\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}
is dense in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
, a general pointed stable curve is
N
-ordinary.)

The following assertion describes the relationship between
N
-ordinariness and
1
-ordinariness.

Proposition 6.7.5
.

The inclusion relation
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,N\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,1\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}
holds.
That is to say, if
\mathscr{X}
is

N
-ordinary, then it is also
1
-ordinary.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Proposition
6.5.4
.
∎

Remark 6.7.6

(
N
-ordinariness vs.
N^{\prime}
-ordinariness)
.

For the same reason as above,
the inclusion relation
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,N\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,N^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}
(where
N^{\prime}\leq N
) holds if the projection
\Pi_{N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N^{\prime},g,r}
is surjective.
In particular, if we can show the connectedness of the stacks
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}

(which implies the surjectivity of the
\Pi_{N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}}
’s), there exists a sequence of open immersions

(561)

\displaystyle\cdots\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,N\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,2\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,1\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}.

It will be natural to ask what the intersection
\bigcap_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,N\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}
is.
However, at the time of writing the present paper, the author does not know the existence of a
closed
point of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
classifying a pointed curve that is
N
-ordinary for every
N
.

7.
Canonical diagonal liftings

In this final section, we construct the canonical diagonal lifting of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper, as well as a Frobenius-projective structure, on a general hyperbolic curve.
A key observation for doing this is that the ordinariness introduced in the previous section
enables us to connect
various deformation spaces involved.
As a consequence, we obtain an effective way to discuss the counting problem of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers in characteristic
p^{N}
via reduction modulo
p
.

Let us fix an odd prime
p
and a positive integer
N
.

7.1.
Canonical diagonal liftings of dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-opers

Let
R
be
a flat
\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
-algebra and
X
a geometrically connected, proper, and smooth curve over
S:=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
of genus
g>1
.
For each positive integer
N^{\prime}\leq N
, we shall denote by a subscript “
N^{\prime}-1
” the result of reducing an object over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
modulo
p^{N^{\prime}}
.
For simplicity, we write
\Omega_{0}:=\Omega_{X_{0}/S_{0}}
and
\Omega^{(N^{\prime})}_{0}:=\Omega_{X_{0}^{(N^{\prime})}/S_{0}}
.

We shall fix a dormant
2^{(1)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
X/S
(cf. Definition
4.4.1
and Proposition
4.4.3
) and fix an integer
N^{\prime}
with
1\leq N^{\prime}<N
.
The diagonal reduction of
\vartheta_{N^{\prime}-1}
defines a

2^{(N^{\prime})}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta_{0}^{(N^{\prime})}
of
X_{0}/S_{0}
.

Also, let

\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{N^{\prime}-1}
be
a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(1)},\vartheta_{N^{\prime}-1})
-oper on
X_{N^{\prime}-1}/S_{N^{\prime}-1}
, and
write
\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F}_{N^{\prime}-1},\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit})
, where
\mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
.
The diagonal reduction of
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{N^{\prime}-1}
defines a
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N^{\prime})},\vartheta_{0}^{(N^{\prime})})
-oper
\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}
on
X_{0}/S_{0}
.
The
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(1)},\vartheta_{0})
-oper on
X_{0}/S_{0}
induced by
\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}
via level reduction will be denoted by
\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(1)}
.

We shall write

(562)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit})_{\vartheta}

for the set of isomorphism classes of
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(1)},\vartheta_{N^{\prime}})
-opers
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{N^{\prime}}
on
X_{N^{\prime}}/S_{N^{\prime}}
with
(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{N^{\prime}})_{N^{\prime}-1}=\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{N^{\prime}-1}
.
Then,
\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit})_{\vartheta}
is nonempty
and has a canonical structure of torsor modeled on the
R
-module
H^{0}(X_{0},\Omega_{0}^{\otimes 2})
(cf.
[
Moc1
, Chap. I, Corollary 2.9]
).

Now, let us consider
the composite

(563)

\displaystyle H^{0}(X_{0},\Omega_{0}^{\otimes 2})\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\Omega_{0}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{0}))\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[(\nabla^{\diamondsuit(1)}_{0})_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}])

(cf. (
163
) for the definition of
(-)_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}
),
where the first arrow denotes the canonical inclusion (cf.
[
Wak6
, § 4.9.5]
) and
the second arrow denotes the morphism induced by the natural inclusion of complexes
(\Omega_{0}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{0}))[-1]\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[(\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(1)})_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}]
.
Then,
the assignment
\nabla_{N^{\prime}}^{\diamondsuit}\mapsto(\mathcal{F}_{N^{\prime}},\nabla_{N^{\prime}}^{\diamondsuit})
defines a map of sets

(564)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit})_{\vartheta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}

where
\mathscr{L}:=(\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}),\nabla_{\vartheta})
(cf. (
164
) for the definition of
\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}
).
Recall from the discussion preceding Proposition
2.4.2
that
\mathrm{Lift}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathscr{L}}
has a structure of
\mathbb{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[(\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(1)})_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}])
-torsor.
The map (
564
) is verified to be compatible with the respective torsor structures via the morphism (
563
) (cf.
[
Wak6
, § 6.3.2]
).
By combining this fact and Proposition
2.4.3
,
we obtain the following assertion.

Proposition 7.1.1
.

Taking the diagonal reductions
\nabla_{N^{\prime}}^{\diamondsuit}\mapsto{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\nabla_{N^{\prime}}^{\diamondsuit}

yields a map of sets

(565)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit})_{\vartheta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})})_{\mathscr{L}}

(cf. (
159
) for the definition of the codomain of this map),
and this map commutes with the respective torsor structures via the composite

(566)

\displaystyle D^{\prime\prime}_{\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}}:H^{0}(X_{0},\Omega_{0}^{\otimes 2})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(\ref{fE14})}\mathbb{H}^{1}(X_{0},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[(\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(1)})_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}])

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(\ref{fE8})}H^{0}(X_{0}^{(N^{\prime})},\Omega^{(N^{\prime})}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}))).

In particular,
the map (
565
) becomes bijective when
D^{\prime\prime}_{\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}}
is bijective.

Denote by

(567)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit})_{\vartheta}^{\psi}

the subset of

\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit})_{\vartheta}
consisting of
dormant

(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(1)},\vartheta_{N^{\prime}})
-opers.
Propositions
2.4.4
and
7.1.1
together imply the following proposition.

Proposition 7.1.2
.

(i)

Taking the diagonal reductions
\nabla_{N^{\prime}}^{\diamondsuit}\mapsto{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\nabla_{N^{\prime}}^{\diamondsuit}

yields a map of sets

(568)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit})_{\vartheta}^{\psi}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})})^{\psi},

and the following commutative square diagram is Cartesian:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit})_{\vartheta}^{\psi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{fE29})}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})})^{\psi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit})_{\vartheta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{fE24})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Lift}(\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}).}

(ii)

Suppose that
D^{\prime\prime}_{\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}}
is bijective.
Then, the map
(
568
)
is bijective.
In particular, for each dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N^{\prime}+1)},\vartheta^{(N^{\prime}+1)}_{0})
-oper
\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime}+1)}
of
X_{0}/S_{0}

that induces
\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}
by reducing its level,
there exists a unique (up to isomorphism) dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(1)},\vartheta_{N^{\prime}})
-oper
\nabla_{N^{\prime}}^{\diamondsuit}
on
X_{N^{\prime}}/S_{N^{\prime}}
with
(\nabla_{N^{\prime}}^{\diamondsuit})_{N^{\prime}-1}=\nabla_{N^{\prime}-1}^{\diamondsuit}
and
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\nabla_{N^{\prime}}^{\diamondsuit}=\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime}+1)}
.

Corollary 7.1.3
.

Let
\nabla^{\diamondsuit(N)}_{0}
be a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta_{0}^{(N)})
-oper on
X_{0}/S_{0}
.
Suppose that
the morphism
D^{\prime\prime}_{\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}}
is bijective for every
N^{\prime}\leq N
, where
\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}
denotes the dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N^{\prime})},\vartheta^{(N^{\prime})}_{0})
-oper obtained by reducing the level of
\nabla^{\diamondsuit(N)}_{0}
to
N^{\prime}
.
Then, there exists a unique (up to isomorphism)
dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(1)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
on
X/S
whose diagonal reduction coincides with
\nabla^{\diamondsuit(N)}_{0}
.

Proof.

The assertion can be proved by applying assertion (ii) of Proposition
7.1.2
successively with respect to
N^{\prime}
(
1\leq N^{\prime}<N
).
∎

7.2.
Canonical diagonal liftings of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers

Denote by

(573)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{1,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Op}_{N,X_{0}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\right)

the set of isomorphism classes of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers on
X/S
(resp., dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers on
X_{0}/S_{0}
).
By regarding it as a (discrete) category, we obtain, from Proposition
4.2.4
, an equivalence of categories

(574)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{1,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\cong\mathcal{O}p_{1,g,0,R}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0,R},\sigma_{X}}S\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Op}_{N,X_{0}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\cong\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,0,\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0,\mathbb{F}_{p}},\sigma_{X_{0}}}S\right),

where
\sigma_{X}
(resp.,
\sigma_{X_{0}}
) denotes the classifying morphism
S\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,R}
(resp.,
S_{0}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
) of
X
(resp.,
X_{0}
).
Taking the diagonal reductions of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers yields a map of sets

(575)

\displaystyle\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\spadesuit}:\mathrm{Op}_{1,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{N,X_{0}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

(cf. (
294
)).
Then, we have the following assertion.

Theorem-Definition 7.2.1
.

Suppose that
X_{0}/S_{0}
is
N
-ordinary (cf. Definition
6.7.4
).
Then, the map
\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\spadesuit}:\mathrm{Op}_{1,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{N,X_{0}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is bijective.
In particular, for each dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0}
on
X_{0}/S_{0}
,
there exists a unique (up to isomorphism) dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
X/S
with

{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}}}\!\!\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}=\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0}
.
We shall refer to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
as the
canonical diagonal lifting
of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0}
.

Proof.

According to Proposition
4.4.3
, we can find a dormant
2^{(1)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
X/S
.
Denote by
\vartheta_{0}^{(N)}
the
2^{(N)}
-theta characteristic of
X_{0}/S_{0}
obtained as the diagonal reduction of
\vartheta
.
Now, let
\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,0},\phi_{0}^{(N)})
be a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
X_{0}/S_{0}
.
For each positive integer
N^{\prime}\leq N
,
we denote by
\phi^{(N^{\prime})}_{0}
the
(N^{\prime}-1)
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
induced by
\phi^{(N)}_{0}
.
The resulting dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N^{\prime})}
-oper
\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit(N^{\prime})}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,0},\phi_{0}^{(N^{\prime})})

corresponds, via
the bijection
\Lambda_{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit,\vartheta}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
(cf. Theorem
4.5.1
), to
a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N^{\prime})},\vartheta_{0}^{(N^{\prime})})
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}_{0}
on
X_{0}/S_{0}
.
Note that there exists a canonical identification
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta_{0}})=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{0}}
(where
\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\mathcal{E}_{B,0}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
), which is compatible with the respective
\mathcal{D}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}^{(N^{\prime}-1)}
-module structures, i.e.,
(\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})})_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}
and
\nabla_{\phi_{0}^{(N^{\prime})}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
.
In particular, we have

(576)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}))\left(=\mathcal{S}ol((\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})})_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}})\right)=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{0},\phi_{0}^{(N^{\prime})}}.

Under this identification, the morphism
D^{\prime\prime}_{\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}}
coincides with
D^{\prime\prime}_{\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit(N^{\prime})}}
(cf. (
531
)).
Hence, since
X_{0}/S_{0}
is
N
-ordinary, the morphisms
D^{\prime\prime}_{\nabla_{0}^{\diamondsuit(N^{\prime})}}
are bijective for all
N^{\prime}
’s.
By Corollary
7.1.3
,
there exists a unique (up to isomorphism) dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(1)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla_{N}^{\diamondsuit}
on
X/S
whose diagonal reduction coincides with
\nabla^{\diamondsuit(N)}_{0}
.
The dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
corresponding to
\nabla_{N}^{\diamondsuit}
specifies a diagonal lifting of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0}
.
The resulting assignment
\mathscr{E}_{0}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
specifies a well-defined map of sets
\mathrm{Op}_{N,X_{0}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{1,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
, and it gives an inverse map of
\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\spadesuit}
.
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

Remark 7.2.2

(Affineness assumption)
.

By the uniqueness assertion in the above theorem, the formation of the canonical diagonal liftings commutes with base-change over
S
-schemes.
Hence, Theorem-Definition
7.2.1
is verified to be true even when we remove the affineness assumption on
S
.

Remark 7.2.3

(Representability of an ordinary locus of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,0,R}
)
.

Denote by

\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}_{R}
) the open substack of
\mathcal{M}_{g,0,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}_{g,0,R}
) consisting of points lying on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0,N\text{-}\mathrm{ord}}
.
Since the projection
\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,0,\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0,\mathbb{F}_{p}}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
is étale,
there exists a unique (up to isomorphism) étale stack
\mathcal{U}
over
\mathcal{M}_{R}
whose reduction modulo
p
is isomorphic to

\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,0,\mathrm{ord}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0,\mathbb{F}_{p}}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
.

Now, let us take an étale scheme
S
over
\mathcal{M}_{g,0,R}
and a section
u
of
\mathcal{U}
over
S
.
The morphism
S\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g,0,R}
classifies a smooth curve
X/S
whose reduction
X_{0}/S_{0}
modulo
p
is
N
-ordinary, and the reduction modulo
p
of
u
defines a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0}
on
X_{0}/S_{0}
.
According to Theorem-Definition
7.2.1
,
we obtain the canonical diagonal lifting
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
of
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{0}
defined on
X/S
.
In particular, the pair
(X/S,\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
specifies an object of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,0,R}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0,R}}\mathcal{M}_{R}
over
S
.
One may verify that the functor

(577)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,0,R}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,0,R}}\mathcal{M}_{R}

given by assigning
u\mapsto(X/S,\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
becomes an isomorphism between the étale sheaves on
\mathcal{M}_{R}
represented by the respective fibered categories.
In particular, by using this isomorphism, we can construct a universal family of
ordinary dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers parametrized by
\mathcal{U}
.

Remark 7.2.4

(Comparison with
p
-adic Teichmüller theory)
.

One of the main achievements in
p
-adic Teichmüller theory asserts the existence of
a canonical
p
-adic lifting of a curve equipped with
a certain additional structure involving a
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper (=
a torally indigenous bundle).
Each object classified by the VF-stack, or the shifted VF-stack, of pure tone (cf.
[
Moc2
, Chap. IV, Definition 2.6]
) is related to the notion of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper because both are characterized by the vanishing of the
p
-curvature of the associated flat bundles.
However, despite the similarity in concept,
there seems to be no (at least direct) correspondence with
the canonical diagonal liftings asserted in the above theorem.

Also, other lifting constructions of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers
can be found in
[
Moc1
]
(which is also in the context of
p
-adic Teichmüller theory, but in a more classical setting) and
[
LSYZ
]
.
These constructions are entirely different from ours because the
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers treated there
never have vanishing
p
-curvature.

Corollary 7.2.5
.

Let
N
be a positive integer and
k
an algebraically closed field over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Denote by

W_{N}
the ring of Witt vectors of length
N
over
k
.
Also, let
X
be a geometrically connected, proper, and smooth curve of genus
g>1
over
W_{N}
.
Denote by
X_{0}
the reduction modulo
p
of
X
.
Then, there are only finitely many isomorphism classes of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers on
X
.
If, moreover,
X_{0}
is general in
\mathcal{M}_{g,0,}
, then
the cardinality
\sharp(\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,X})
of
\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,X}
satisfies the following equalities:

(578)

\displaystyle\sharp(\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,X})=\sharp(\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,X_{0}})=\mathrm{deg}(\Pi_{N,g,0,\mathbb{F}_{p}}).

Proof.

The assertion follows from Theorem
5.3.6
and Theorem-Definition
7.2.1
.
∎

Remark 7.2.6

(
p
-adic liftings of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(\infty)}
-opers)
.

Let
K
be an algebraically closed field over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and let
X_{0}
be a connected proper smooth curve of genus
g
over
K
classified by a
K
-rational
generic
point of
\mathcal{M}_{g,0}
.
Denote by
W
the ring of Witt vectors over
K
.
Also, choose
a deformation
X
of
X_{0}
over
W
.

According to Corollary
6.6.7
,
we can find
a collection of data

(579)

\displaystyle\{s_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}},

where

•

each
s_{N}
(
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
) denotes a
K
-valued generic point of an irreducible component of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}
;

•

the points
\{s_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}
are compatible with respect to the projective system (
292
), i.e.,
s_{N}
is mapped to
s_{N^{\prime}}
via the natural projection
\Pi_{N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N^{\prime},g,r}
for every
N^{\prime}\leq N
.

The point
s_{N}
determines a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}
on
X_{0}/K
.
The resulting
compatible
collection
\{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}
should be called a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper
of level
\infty
, or
dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(\infty)}
-oper
.
Since
X_{0}
is
N
-ordinary for every
N
, we can apply
Corollary
7.2.5
to the
\mathscr{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}
’s, and
obtain a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper
\mathscr{E}_{\infty}^{\spadesuit}
defined on a curve
X/W
via algebraization.
This construction would partially realize the expected correspondence (at least in the generic situation) between dormant opers of level
\infty

on a curve in characteristic
p
and dormant opers of level
1
on its
p
-adic lifting.

7.3.
Canonical diagonal liftings of
F^{N}
-projective structures

The notion of an

F^{N}
-projective structures (i.e., a Frobenius-projective structures of level
N
) on a curve in characteristic
p
was originally introduced by
Y. Hoshi (cf.
[
Hos
, Definition 2.1]
) and later investigated by the author (cf.
[
Wak4
]
,
[
Wak5
]
).
In this final subsection, we extend the definition of an
F^{N}
-projective structure to the case where
the underlying curve has characteristic
p^{N}
(cf. Definition
7.3.2
below).
After proving a bijective correspondence with dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers,
we formulate the canonical diagonal liftings of
F^{N}
-projective structures.

Let
l
be a nonnegative integer,

S
a flat
\mathbb{Z}/p^{l+1}\mathbb{Z}
-scheme,
and
X
a geometrically connected, proper, and smooth
curve of genus
g>1
over
S
.
Suppose that
S
is equipped with an
(N-1)
-PD structure extending
X
.
Denote by
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2})_{X}
the Zariski sheaf on
X
represented by
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
; this may be identified with the sheaf of automorphisms of the trivial
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-bundle
X\times\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
.
Also, denote by

(580)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)})_{X}

the subsheaf of
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2})_{X}
consisting of (locally defined) automorphisms of

X\times\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
preserving the
(N-1)
-PD stratification
\phi_{\mathrm{triv}}
(cf. (
56
) for the definition of
\phi_{\mathrm{triv}}
).

Next, let us write

(581)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\text{\'{e}t}}

for the Zariski sheaf of sets on
X
that assigns, to each open subscheme
U
of
X
, the set of étale
S
-morphisms
from
U
to the projective line
\mathbb{P}
over
S
.

Lemma 7.3.1
.

Let
U
be an open subscheme of
X
,
\varphi:U\rightarrow\mathbb{P}
an element of
\mathcal{P}^{\text{\'{e}t}}(U)
.
Also, let
h
be an element of
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)})_{X}(U)
, regarded as an
S
-morphism
U\rightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
.
Then,
the composite

(582)

\displaystyle{{}^{h}}\varphi:U\xrightarrow{(h,\varphi)}\mathrm{PGL}_{2}\times\mathbb{P}\xrightarrow{\Psi}\mathbb{P}

belongs to
\mathcal{P}^{\text{\'{e}t}}(U)
, where the second arrow
\Psi
denotes the usual
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-action on
\mathbb{P}
.

Proof.

After possibly replacing
U
with its covering with respect to the étale topology,
we may assume that
there exists an automorphism
\widetilde{h}
of
(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2},(\nabla_{X,\mathrm{triv}}^{(N-1)})^{\oplus 2})
inducing
h
via projectivization; we will use the same notation
\widetilde{h}
to denote the associated morphism
U\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{2}
.
Let us choose a point
q\in U(k)
, where
k
denotes a field.
The differential of
(h,\varphi):U\rightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{2}\times\mathbb{P}
at
q
yields a morphism of
k
-vector spaces

(583)

\displaystyle(dh,d\varphi):\mathcal{T}_{U/S}|_{q}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{PGL}_{2}}|_{h(q)}\oplus\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{P}/S}|_{\varphi(q)}\left(=\mathcal{T}_{(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}\times\mathbb{P})/S}|_{(h(q),\varphi(q))}\right).

Note that the differential of
\widetilde{h}
coincides with the zero map because
\widetilde{h}
lies in
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2},(\nabla_{X,\mathrm{triv}}^{(N-1)})^{\oplus 2})
.
Hence, since
h
factors through
\widetilde{h}
, the morphism
dh:\mathcal{T}_{U/S}|_{q}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{PGL}_{2}}|_{h(q)}
coincides with the zero map.
On the other hand, by the étaleness assumption on
\varphi
,
the morphism
d\varphi:\mathcal{T}_{U/S}|_{q}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{P}/S}|_{\varphi(q)}
is an isomorphism.
Also, the differential
d\Psi:\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{PGL}_{2}}|_{h(q)}\oplus\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{P}/S}|_{\varphi(q)}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{P}/S}|_{h(\varphi)(q)}
of the
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-action
\Psi

restricts to an isomorphism
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{P}/S}|_{\varphi(q)}\left(=0\oplus\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{P}/S}|_{\varphi(q)}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{P}/S}|_{{{}^{h}}\varphi(q)}
.
It follows that the differential
d({{}^{h}}\varphi)\left(=d\Psi\circ(dh,d\varphi)\right)
of
{{}^{h}}\varphi
at
q
is an isomorphism.
Since both
U
and
\mathbb{P}
are smooth over
S
, the morphism
{{}^{h}}\varphi
turns out to be étale.
This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

By the above lemma, the assignment
(h,\varphi)\mapsto{{}^{h}}\varphi
defines a
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)})_{X}
-action

(584)

\displaystyle\Psi^{(N)}:(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)})_{X}\times\mathcal{P}^{\text{\'{e}t}}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{\text{\'{e}t}}

on the sheaf
\mathcal{P}^{\text{\'{e}t}}
.

Definition 7.3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{\blacklozenge}
be a subsheaf of
\mathcal{P}^{\text{\'{e}t}}
.
We shall say that
\mathcal{S}^{\blacklozenge}
is a
Frobenius-projective

structure of level
N
(or simply, an
F^{N}
-projective structure
) on
X/S
if it is closed under the
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)})_{X}
-action
\Psi^{(N)}

and forms a
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)})_{X}
-torsor on
X
with respect to the resulting
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)})_{X}
-action on
\mathcal{S}^{\blacklozenge}
.

Remark 7.3.3

(Comparison with the classical definition)
.

Suppose that
l=0
.
Then, since

(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)})_{X}
coincides with the subsheaf
(F_{X/S}^{(N)})^{-1}((\mathrm{PGL}_{2})_{X^{(N)}})
of
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2})_{X}
,
the definition of an
F^{N}
-projective structure described
above
is the same as the classical definition discussed in
[
Hos
, Definition 2.1]
and
[
Wak4
, Definition 1.2.1]
.

Denote by

(585)

\displaystyle F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X}

the set of
F^{N}
-projective structures on
X/S
.
The following assertion generalizes
[
Hos
, Theorem A]
and
[
Wak5
, Theorem B]
(for
(G,H)=(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},B)
) to characteristic
p^{N}
.

Theorem 7.3.4
.

Suppose that either
l=0
or
N=1
is satisfied (hence the set
\mathrm{Op}_{N,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
can be defined as in (
573
)).
Then, there exists a canonical bijection of sets

(586)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{N,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X}.

In particular, there are only
finitely many
F^{N}
-projective structures on
X/S
.

Proof.

Since the assertion for
l=0
was already proved in
[
Hos
, Theorem A]
,
it suffices to consider the case of
N=1
.
First, we shall construct a map of sets
\mathrm{Op}_{1,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X}
.
Let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\nabla)
be a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper on
X/S
, and write
\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{E}_{B}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
.
Denote by
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})
the projective line bundle associated to
\mathcal{E}
.
The
B
-reduction
\mathcal{E}_{B}
of
\mathcal{E}
determines a global section
\sigma:X\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})
.
Next, let us take an open subscheme
U
of
X
on which
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is
trivialized
(cf.
Corollary
4.5.2
, (ii)).
Choose a trivialization

(587)

\displaystyle\alpha:(\mathcal{E}|_{U},\nabla|_{U})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(U\times\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\nabla_{\mathrm{triv}}),

where
\nabla_{\mathrm{triv}}
denotes the trivial
S
-connection on
U\times\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
(cf.
[
Wak6
, Eq. (78)]
); it induces an isomorphism

\alpha_{\mathbb{P}}:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})|_{U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}U\times_{S}\mathbb{P}
.
Thus, we obtain the composite

(588)

\displaystyle\varphi_{\alpha}:U\xrightarrow{\sigma|_{U}}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})|_{U}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{\mathbb{P}}}U\times_{S}\mathbb{P}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}\mathbb{P}.

Similarly to the proof of
[
Wak4
, Corollary 1.6.2]
, we see that
\varphi_{\alpha}
is étale.
Let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\blacklozenge}(U)
denote the set of étale morphisms
\varphi_{\alpha}
constructed in this way for all possible trivializations
\alpha
as in (
587
).
The set
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\blacklozenge}(U)
has a structure of
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(1)})_{X}(U)
-torsor with respect to the
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(1)})_{X}(U)
-action defined by
\varphi_{\alpha}\mapsto\varphi_{h\circ\alpha}
for each
h\in(\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(1)})_{X}(U)
.
Hence, the sheaf associated to the assignment
U\mapsto\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\blacklozenge}(U)
specifies a subsheaf

(589)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\blacklozenge}

of
\mathcal{P}^{\text{\'{e}t}}
.
One may verify that
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\blacklozenge}
forms an
F^{1}
-projective structure on
X/S
, and
the resulting assignment
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\blacklozenge}
defines a map of sets
\mathrm{Op}_{1,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X}
.
Moreover, we can reverse the steps in the above construction, so
the map
\mathrm{Op}_{1,X}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X}
is verified to be bijective.

Finally, the latter assertion follows from the former assertion together with Proposition
4.6.1
, (ii).
This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Hereinafter, we suppose that
l+1=N
.
Denote by
X_{0}/S_{0}
the curve obtained as the reduction modulo
p
of
X/S
.
Let us define a map

(590)

\displaystyle\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\blacklozenge}:F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X}\rightarrow F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X_{0}}

to be the unique map that makes the following square diagram commute:

\textstyle{F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\blacklozenge}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{e778})}
\textstyle{F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{e778})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\spadesuit}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Op}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,X_{0}}.}

Theorem-Definition 7.3.5
.

Suppose that
X_{0}/S_{0}
is
N
-ordinary.
Then,
the map
\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\blacklozenge}
is bijective.
That is to say,
for any
F^{N}
-projective structure
\mathcal{S}^{\blacklozenge}_{0}
on
X_{0}/S_{0}
,
there exists a unique
F^{1}
-projective structure
\mathcal{S}^{\blacklozenge}
on
X/S
which is mapped to
\mathcal{S}^{\blacklozenge}_{0}
via
\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}_{\!\!\blacklozenge}
.
We shall refer to
\mathcal{S}^{\blacklozenge}
as the
canonical diagonal lifting
of
\mathcal{S}^{\blacklozenge}_{0}
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Theorem-Definition
7.2.1
, Remark
7.2.2
, and Theorem
7.3.4
.
∎

Corollary 7.3.6
.

Suppose that
X_{0}/S_{0}
is
N
-ordinary.
Then, there are only finitely many
F^{1}
-projective structures on
X/S
and the following equality holds:

(595)

\displaystyle\sharp(F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X/S})=\sharp(F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Pr}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}).

Proof.

The assertion follows Theorem-Definition
7.3.5
.
∎
